# Open shells in ksmech: generality fixes and a pinned closed_base first, then repair what is not open-shell, admit open-shell species in polar steps, and add one-electron steps from RMechDB (via FlowER)

Status: plan agreed 2026-10-28 (all 23 decisions taken; generality-audit corrections and phase P0a approved). Nothing implemented or committed yet. Line numbers refer to main f6aa758 unless a step says otherwise.

## Contents

1. Philosophy
2. Decisions taken
3. Phases
4. Spec changes
5. Evaluation
6. Not doing
7. Appendix: change log and consistency log

## 1. Philosophy

Open shells were never rejected by one decision. They were deferred rule by rule (RADICAL_DRAWN, OPEN_SHELL_METAL, SPIN_PARITY, METAL_PLACEHOLDER, the fermi_smeared mask), and none of the 290 excluded PMechDB and ReactMech-pilot steps is a radical step. Most exclusions are drawing errors: a carbon or a CDI ring missing its H, a bare alkali atom missing its charge, an onium donor written as a covalent bond to Pd, a placeholder metal without its ligands. Some drawing errors even sit in training: the 49 ReactMech onium Pd/Ni steps are computed 2 electrons short. The genuine open shells are few: 16 open-shell species in 10 PMechDB steps and 2 resonance pairs, the carbenes and CO whose spin RDKit gets wrong, and the Cu(II)/Fe(III) Lewis acids. So the plan works from the outside in: merge claude/reactmech, add guardrails and snapshot the splits (P0); make the model path general before any baseline (P0a); pin closed_base and train the 3-seed baseline (P0b); return the drawing errors (P1, P5); measure the open shells (P2) and give them density-consistent tokens (P2b) before the genuine radicals (P3) and the rule-assigned carbenes, CO and metals (P4) are admitted, which closes Release A; then one-electron steps from a real radical source in three stacked branches (P6-P8, Release B) and the open-shell head (P9).

One principle governs every phase. Each rule left in the model path is a physical law or exact bookkeeping, a QM-derived input the model weighs, or an option behind a flag whose default an ablation justifies in decisions.md. Hand lists, element lists, percentiles and go/no-go thresholds are replaced by one of the three. Data curation stays, but it is counted, reported and applied by one law per phenomenon, never gated by element or parity. The generality audit found the model inputs clean and the hand rules around the model: d-type acceptor cells deleted from the store (10.4% of virtual tokens; more than half of the gold anchor's pooled weight gone in 15.2% of PMechDB training steps), the donor's own antibond and a site rule deleting 1.6% of cells (the arrow's own orbital pair in 237 training and 34 val steps, and the sigma -> sigma* cell of every homolysis in Release B), Kekule swaps in 9.4% of ReactMech labels, fixed decoder caps, a truncated spectral set, and metal repairs gated by element and parity. P0a enforces the principle before any baseline: every cell stays in the store with the old rules as flag channels, retained_fraction and E_r become inputs, the finite-difference f+/f- and an exact-degeneracy frontier column are stored as flagged inputs, the thresholds nobody resolved (tau_atom, the 0.2-1.8 band, the spectral-set merge, SMEAR_TOL) are measured for their reach, ReactMech is relabelled from the Kekule form of the product closest to the reactant, decoder caps come from each source's labels, and one dative law, one placeholder rule and one METAL_SUSPECT flag replace the gated repairs. Everything that changes or removes a training step or its inputs lands in that one re-ingest (SAMPLE_VERSION 17 -> 18), with the plan's only token rebuild (SCHEMA_VERSION 9 -> 10, additive: an exact-degeneracy frontier column beside the 0.05 eV one), so the baseline is trained once, on the store the plan keeps, as GENERAL (new flags on) against LEGACY (flags off), 3 seeds each. Afterwards a phase only returns excluded steps or adds a source.

Restricted GFN2 stays the main path. For organic radicals its charges are within 0.008 e of spGFN2 (heteroatom radicals 0.027 e) and its finite-difference Fukui within 0.001, while a global change would recompute about 182.5k records (about 86 GB) for identical closed-shell results. Restricted GFN2 fails on metal energetics (FeCl3's multiplicity order inverted, sextet charges off by 0.35 e), so the multiplicity comes from one module, ksmech.data.spin: the RDKit count, spin_table_v1 from a one-off spGFN2 two-multiplicity scan, and a named Cu(II)/Fe(III) table. Whether the metals stay in training is decided by a 3-seed ablation, and spGFN2 for u > 0 is a planned ablation after P9 (D2), not a trigger. Every gate compares content per named column, never header bytes. Out of scope: species with two or more unpaired electrons whose open shell is not one degenerate group (the Hund case; on disk only triplet O2 and O(3P)), so genuine diradical chemistry is out of domain; electron transfers without a bond change, open-shell singlets, ligand-less placeholder metals, alpha/beta tokens, contact geometry, a global QM method change, external QM data as training data, and any public copy of NC-ND-derived artifacts.

Versions. SAMPLE_VERSION 17 -> 18 (P0a) -> 19 (P8, Release B) -> 20 only if D2 adopts a refinement; D3, deferred, takes the next free number at its build. Token SCHEMA_VERSION 9 -> 10 in P0a (additive), none later; QMRecord SCHEMA_VERSION 1 throughout. FROZEN_SPLIT_SCHEMA 1 (P0), independent of SAMPLE_VERSION. FLOOR_VERSION 1 -> 2 (P0, report fields) -> 3 (P0a, cell switch) -> 4 (P7, own_break floor). DECODE_VERSION 1 -> 2 (P0) -> 3 (P0a, caps) -> 4 (P9, screen and filter). QM cache INDEX_VERSION (qm/index.py) 1 -> 2 (P4); browse INDEX_VERSION (pmindex.py) 2 -> 3 (P8). API_SCHEMA 1 -> 2 (S1) -> 3 (S2); ORBITAL_SET_VERSION and DENSITY_VERSION 1 -> 2 (S1); POOL_KEY_VERSION stays 2. LGBM_VERSION 2 (D1) and BOND_INDEX_VERSION 2 (D2, only if adopted). Cohort: closed_base (pinned in P0b on v18). Release A = P1-P5; Release B = P6-P8 (+P9).

## 2. Decisions taken

### U1. How are splits kept when steps are added?

Frozen append-only. The split files that exist today (PMechDB molecule and scaffold; ReactMech molecule, scaffold, loco and the pilot's 10,000 ids) are snapshotted in P0, before P0a changes any step, so old checkpoints stay evaluable without leakage; existing steps keep their part, new groups are placed by hash, and bridging steps are SPLIT_CONFLICT, counted. Frozen split and cohort files carry no file inputs and a schema independent of SAMPLE_VERSION, so the 17 -> 18 -> 19 bumps never refuse them; rmechdb is frozen at its first build, after the [Ar]/[He] strip, the N#N repair and the RC-wins dedup.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the U14 fact-check found split files stamped with SAMPLE_VERSION, so frozen files get their own schema; the new P0a puts every step change after the snapshot; the U17/U19 amendments fix rmechdb's ids before its freeze. The answer itself is unchanged.. *Blocks:* P0.

### U2. Which reference configuration and how many seeds for the comparability runs?

dense_plain x 3 seeds, trained in P0b on the v18 store after P0a. GENERAL (new flags on) x 3 is the reference; LEGACY (new flags off) x 3 is the ablation that justifies dropping the old rules. If GENERAL is worse than LEGACY beyond the U20 tolerance, the rule that costs accuracy stays as a flagged default with its evidence in decisions.md. GENERAL turns on P0a's pair_cells and orbital_extra; the stored FD f+/f- and exact-degeneracy frontier columns stay unselected until their P9 ablations. Old checkpoints are re-scored with flags off; the four pod runs (dense_plain, dense_resonance, flex_resonance, sparse_resonance) are re-scored only if you supply their weights (only SHA256SUMS.pod is on disk).

*Amended by:* audit: the baseline moves from P0 to P0b, on the v18 store, and splits into GENERAL and LEGACY arms; the answer (dense_plain x 3) is unchanged; consistency pass 2026-10-28: GENERAL's flag set made explicit for the columns of resolutions 5 and 6.. *Blocks:* P0b.

### U3. Spec versioning and approval: staged versions, each approved before its build?

Staged. v9.1 before the P0a build: the governing principle, P0a's generality fixes and domain laws (with the stored FD f+/f- and exact-degeneracy frontier columns and token SCHEMA_VERSION 9 -> 10), closed_base, frozen splits and P1's repairs. v9.2 before the P2b build: open-shell tokens, the spin rule, the metals. v10 before the P8 build: Release B. Each is drafted by Claude, approved by you and committed before its build, and never edited while a build runs from that checkout.

*Amended by:* audit: v9.1 now also carries P0a, so it must be approved before the P0a build instead of before P1; consistency pass 2026-10-28: v9.1 also carries the frontier and FD columns (resolutions 4-6).. *Blocks:* P0a.

### U4. Which drawing-error repair rules should ingest apply?

All, narrow. V1 completes the H only for a C with 3 radical electrons and for the CDI ring, tested on topology (5-ring C of degree 2 between two N with 1 radical electron; RDKit does not flag it aromatic); the beta-lactam N is a named per-step repair; A1 adds +1 only when the same alkali atom (same map number) is on both sides; ligand-less Sn is relabelled METAL_PLACEHOLDER by P0a's placeholder law. R3 is replaced by P0a's onium-to-metal dative law. PMechDB gains 13 steps and 1 resonance pair in P1; ReactMech gets 267 of the 269 pilot exclusions back (205 through P0a's dative law, 62 CDI through V1; about 3,121 of 3,165 on the full set), computed in P5 at about 12-19 CPU-h (estimate: 327 unique fragments without a record; the earlier 10-17 CPU-h counted the 291 drawn SPIN_PARITY fragments). The 3 'isopropoxides' stay excluded as ambiguous (possible sodium ketyls) on the U6 candidate list. The repair laws and the dative law apply to every source, rmechdb included, with no source switch (measured effect there: at most 1 line).

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check made the CDI test topological, corrected the counts (267/269, about 3,121/3,165), found the Ni clause moot and R3's cost to be 10-17 CPU-h, and moved the isopropoxides to the U6 candidates; the audit replaced R3's group-10 and parity gates with the dative law of P0a, required the same alkali atom on both sides for A1, and named the beta-lactam fix a per-step repair; consistency pass 2026-10-28: resolutions 1, 2 and 18 (Sn relabel in P0a; the repair laws on every source; P5's QM need measured at 327 fragments, 12-19 CPU-h).. *Blocks:* P0a (dative law, Sn relabel); P1 (V1, A1, beta-lactam).

### U5. What happens to the even-count suspicious metal drawings already in training?

One law per phenomenon, applied in P0a before the baseline instead of a split by source. The onium-to-metal dative law (metal -1 per uncompensated onium donor, for any element and any starting parity, applied only where the fragment's electron count ends up even and the pool's charge balances; a metal-charge clause keeps the 3 [Cl][Zn+] steps, a free-ion clause leaves a spectator [Ag+] alone, and the L1Pd(0) exemption stays; checked by the 20-case oxidation-state audit in P0a) re-charges the 49 ReactMech onium Pd/Ni steps (not 73; computed +2/+3, truly 0/+1; 29 in val; about 59 fragments, 2-4 CPU-h of QM) and returns 205 excluded pilot steps. The parity-independent METAL_PLACEHOLDER rule removes 24 placeholder-shaped even PMechDB steps; the 3 [Cl][Zn+] steps stay as defined ions. METAL_SUSPECT, a flag and slice, marks a first-row transition metal left with an open d shell: 35 ReactMech pilot steps (Cr(IV) 10, Ni(II) d8 25), 0 PMechDB. ReactMech results are reported with and without the 49.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check corrected 73 to 49, found them mis-charged rather than suspect and split the answer by source (PMechDB 27 and Cr(IV) flag-only, ReactMech repaired in P5); the audit replaced that with one dative law, one parity-independent placeholder rule and one METAL_SUSPECT policy, applied in P0a, so about 24 of the PMechDB 27 leave training before closed_base is pinned; consistency pass 2026-10-28: resolutions 1 and 3 (the even-end-state dative law with its clauses; METAL_SUSPECT = open-d-shell first-row metal, 35 ReactMech, 0 PMechDB).. *Blocks:* P0a.

### U6. Should the genuine organic radicals PMechDB draws with polar arrows be admitted?

Yes, all 10 training steps and 2 resonance pairs (your choice, against the 'spectators only' suggestion), including 5d007b38 (Et3B + O2, whose product holds a u = 2 species), which is a counted exemption of the one MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES policy. Admission is by counted rules (u conserved, R even, integral SOMO) with the hand-read list as the test oracle; every disagreement between rules and list is listed at P3's exit. The Birch step is tagged (its QM input is a Na...benzene complex, not the drawn Na-C bond). The 3 'isopropoxides' stay excluded as ambiguous through a counted 5-step curation file (with the 2 drawing errors the laws would pass), each with its reason. The cohort (16 open-shell species) is an end-to-end pipeline test, reported as counts.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check added the Birch tag, the U19 interaction of 5d007b38 and the end-to-end framing; the audit replaced the hand allow-list with counted rules (the list as oracle) and the rmechdb-scoped u >= 2 exclusion with one policy across sources and a counted exemption; consistency pass 2026-10-28: resolution 9 (counted 5-step curation file).. *Blocks:* P3.

### U7. QM treatment of open shells

Restricted GFN2 as today, with the multiplicity from data.spin, and no global method change (it would recompute about 182.5k unique records, about 86 GB, for identical closed-shell results). spGFN2 runs only in P2's one-off two-multiplicity scan (scratch cache) and in D2, now a planned ablation after P9 for u > 0, adopted by a paired per-sample test and non-inferiority, not trigger-gated.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check corrected the record count (182.5k and 86 GB, not 197k) and confirmed the FeCl3 failures; the audit made D2 a planned ablation instead of a triggered one and added the P2 scan that builds spin_table_v1.. *Blocks:* P2 (scan); P4.

### U8. Which spin rule (one RDKit-based module, ksmech.data.spin, read by QM, domain, key, masks and inputs)?

The RDKit count, plus spin_table_v1, a versioned species table from P2's one-off spGFN2 two-multiplicity scan of every carbene-, CO-, ozone- and u >= 2-shaped species of PMechDB, ReactMech and FlowER RS/RC (a multiplicity where the gap exceeds about 0.3 eV, otherwise MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS), plus the Cu(II)/Fe(III) table, named as a table. The hand clauses for carbenes and CO become tests of the table. spin.py rejects the CuH2 adducts (RDKit gives [CuH2] u = 1), CuI2 and every other odd metal (MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS or placeholder), so no hydride record is computed.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check added the CuH2 rejection and softened the AC12 argument (about 7 kcal/mol, not 23.59); the audit replaced the hand carbene and CO clauses with the scanned, versioned table. The user's amendment of 2026-10-30 derives every multiplicity twice, by spGFN2 at every allowed multiplicity and by the theory rules of spin_theory_v1, and combines them by P2 step 4's rule. *Blocks:* P2 (spin_theory_v1, spin_table_v1); P4.

### U9. Do the open-shell metals (PMechDB Cu(II) and Fe(III)) enter this extension?

Cu and Fe together, all or nothing (your choice, against 'separately'): 62 steps (Cu 23, Fe 39) and 6 resonance pairs are admitted in P4 as the open_shell_metal cohort. P2's restricted-vs-spGFN2 numbers are diagnostics, not a 0.1 e go/no-go; whether the metals stay in training is decided by a 3-seed with/without ablation under the U20 tolerance (one-sided, max(2 x pooled SD of the 6 runs, 1 point), with the paired McNemar test beside it). Cu=C is already METAL_PLACEHOLDER; data.spin rejects the CuH2 adducts and CuI2. Risk carried: an atom bonded to Fe is an arrow atom in 29 of the 39 non-resonance Fe steps (26 of them a Br of FeBr3 or FeBr4-), and restricted FeCl3 Cl charges differ from spGFN2 by 0.116 e.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check corrected the count (62 + 6), Cu=C's code and the CuH2 rule; the audit replaced the 0.1 e gate with admission plus a 3-seed ablation, which removes the risk that a failing Fe gate holds Cu back until D2; consistency pass 2026-10-28: resolution 16 (U20 tolerance for the metal ablation).. *Blocks:* P4.

### U10. Fermi-smeared samples: keep them flagged, or exclude them as spec tex:806 says?

Keep them flagged and report slices with and without them in every cohort (molecule 24/2/8 and scaffold 27/6/1 on v17; 18/2/8 and 21/6/1 on v18, after P0a's placeholder law removes 6 placeholder-shaped training samples, measured read-only), plus max|n - aufbau| as a distribution, not only the flag. The slice reporting is new code. tex:106, tex:806 and CLAUDE.md change to match. After P2b the flag stays on closed-shell molecules; near-degenerate Cu(II) adducts may add flagged samples to the metal cohort.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check added the scaffold counts, per-cohort slices and the note that slice reporting is new code; the audit added the distribution. It now blocks P0b, where closed_base is pinned with these samples in it.. *Blocks:* P0b.

### U11. When and how is claude/reactmech merged?

Merged whole as the first step of P0, with no data change (stop0 ended 19:02 on 2026-10-28; the only conflict is docs/decisions.md; 20 GB free). The worktree's data moves into main's data/ with cp -a --reflink=always on btrfs, not hard links, and ksmech-serve, which runs from main, is stopped before the merge and the copy and started again after (no tracked-file edit while serve runs from that checkout). The ReactMech labels are re-made from the Kekule-closest product in P0a's re-ingest, not at the merge.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the U14 fact-check showed that hard links let non-atomic writers (steps.parquet, the QM manifest, index.sqlite, metrics.jsonl) write through to main, so the move is by reflink; the audit's Kekule relabel goes to P0a.. *Blocks:* P0.

### U12. Form of the identity (sigma) key v2

v1 when the pool has no unpaired electron; otherwise v1 plus the per-fragment (sigma SMILES, charge, unpaired) multiset, with no '.' in the suffix. u comes from the non-bonding electron count N where the drawing gives it (samples, eval, decoded products), and from data.spin on RDKit only for bare SMILES. A step is resonance only when the product is among the enumerated forms (else NONCONJUGATED_SHIFT, counted), and O2 drawings are normalized so O=O and [O][O] are one species where the spin rule says triplet. At the merge the about 362 of 1,388 data/serve/pools states that hold RDKit-radical species are purged or re-keyed.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check added u from N, the '.' constraint and the serve-pool purge (S2's 'no migration' was wrong); the audit added the enumerated-forms resonance test and the O2 normalization.. *Blocks:* P6.

### U13. How are single-electron-transfer steps (no bond change) handled?

Excluded and counted as SET_STEP (0 on disk), with a counted NULL_STEP for the identity line (R = 0, identical sides), so it never enters resonance_pairs, and a separate counted code for dissociative electron transfer. Recount when D1's official CSV arrives.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check found 0 SETs (the '1' is an identity line) and added NULL_STEP; the audit gave dissociative electron transfer its own code.. *Blocks:* P6.

### U14. How does Release B land?

Stacked branches P6 -> P7 -> P8 in a worktree whose data root is seeded with cp -a --reflink=always (btrfs; not hard links), merged to main in order once P8's gates pass, with one SAMPLE_VERSION bump (18 -> 19, built in P8) and the migration into main also by reflink, with serve aware of it. Frozen split and cohort files have a schema independent of SAMPLE_VERSION. Budget about 5-7 GB of new data (btrfs at 96%); rebase the branches before P8 builds.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check replaced hard links with reflinks and added the frozen-file schema and the disk budget; P0a took the 17 -> 18 bump onto main, so Release B's one bump is now 18 -> 19.. *Blocks:* P6.

### U15. How do SOMOs enter the pair track, and which inputs are allowed?

A-hole: one virtual-kind hole token per main-group open-shell token, built at sample assembly; spin_role (+1 donor copy, -1 hole copy, 0) as a role flag, not m_s; and the per-atom restricted SOMO population as a continuous atom input, which does not change with the resonance drawing. The categorical u is kept for keys and bookkeeping; the input is chosen by a 3-seed ablation of none / categorical u / SOMO population. About 17 consumer files change; alpha/beta tokens would take p99 from 483 to 836 orbital tokens, with 33 samples over 1024.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check corrected the consumer count and the alpha/beta figures; the audit replaced the gated per-atom unpaired count (recomputed per resonance drawing) with the continuous SOMO population and made its ablation 3 seeds.. *Blocks:* P7.

### U16. Degenerate open shells and the radical frontier convention

For u > 0 only, in P2b: the occupied/virtual split and the open sub-block come from the aufbau occupation, and a degenerate group is a set of MOs with equal GFN2 occupations (no 0.05 eV window; group counts at 0.01/0.05/0.1 eV reported as diagnostics). The open group is both HOMO and LUMO, with frozen f+ = f- = its population as the default; the finite-difference f+/f- is an input variant behind a flag, ablated in P9. Closed shells are unchanged. Br., Cl., .OH, NO, superoxide and CuCl2 get deterministic, density-consistent tokens; they stay masked as donors and acceptors until P7. Separately, P0a stores an exact-degeneracy variant of the closed-shell frontier columns beside the legacy 0.05 eV ones (token SCHEMA_VERSION 9 -> 10, legacy default behind a flag), which P9 ablates; closed-shell legacy inputs stay as they are.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check found NO's pair split by numerical noise today, the FlowER Br/Cl counts to be fragment counts (146/103) and the band still masking them until P7; the audit replaced the 0.05 eV window with equal occupations and made the FD f+/f- a flagged variant; consistency pass 2026-10-28: resolution 6 (closed-shell exact-degeneracy column in P0a, ablated in P9).. *Blocks:* P2b.

### U17. Identity, scope and splits of the radical source

Source rmechdb over FlowER RC+RS: step_id from the map-free reaction after the [Ar]/[He] strip, the RC copy winning the 83 RS/RC collisions, the raw suffix stored as flower_suffix. Split rc_molecule (RC by molecule, RS train-only behind LEAK_GUARD), plus flower_given only for the RMechRP comparison; RS is measured as a training-data ablation in P9. The 5,088 lines are 4,535 distinct reactions after the [Ar]/[He] strip (4,559 before it); RC is 1,077/134/136 after dedup, LEAK_GUARD 74-81 RS steps (by the id labelling), and the RC test interval about +-8 points. FlowER's numeric-suffix set is about 2.14M lines, not 1.4M.

*Amended by:* factcheck: dedup after the strip with RC winning, the corrected split sizes, the wide RC test interval and the 2.14M count; consistency pass 2026-10-28: resolution 12 (numbers re-measured under the adopted dedup, P8).. *Blocks:* P8.

### U18. Where may rmechdb-derived artifacts (steps, samples, tokens, checkpoints) be stored or copied?

Like PMechDB: local copies, the private rhoMech-data and pods yes, the public repo never; cite RMechDB and FlowER; FlowER's MIT label is not relied on; test fixtures are hand-written, never real lines. PMechDB's CC-BY-NC-ND was read from a web search only (its site gave a TLS error), not verified. This is not legal advice; the NC term matters if ChemRho is ever offered commercially, and it governs what S3 may expose.

*Amended by:* factcheck: made the rule consistent with current PMechDB practice instead of stricter, ruled out reliance on the MIT label and added the NC note for serve.. *Blocks:* P8.

### U19. Species with more than one unpaired electron, N2 drawn [N]=[N], and bath-gas atoms

One MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES policy for every source: a species left with u >= 2 under data.spin whose open shell is not one degenerate group (the Hund case, which covers triplet O2 and O(3P)) is excluded, with counted exemptions (PMechDB 5d007b38), and the exclusions are config flags. Ozone and other paired drawings go through spin_table_v1, a Lewis pairing rule and a degenerate-frontier (Hund) test instead of the CO/N2/O3/O2 lists: the about 23 ozone lines are redrawn as closed-shell ozone and counted under PAIRED_DRAWING where the table assigns a singlet (MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS otherwise). [N]=[N] is repaired to N#N before the rmechdb splits are frozen (5 RC steps, also PAIRED_DRAWING), and [Ar]/[He] are stripped before step_id. Genuine diradical chemistry (Bergman, Norrish, Criegee) is declared out of domain: at most 66 rmechdb lines after dedup (RC 38, RS 28; an upper bound by RDKit count, P8), ozone no longer among them.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check added O3_DRAWN, the early N#N repair and the strip before step_id; the audit made the policy apply to all sources with counted exemptions (the answer had scoped it to rmechdb) and replaced the hand lists with the table, a pairing rule and a Hund test; consistency pass 2026-10-28: O3_DRAWN and the O2/O(3P) list replaced by P8's pairing rule and Hund test; the 88-step pre-dedup count replaced by P8's measured upper bound.. *Blocks:* P8 (its exemptions are read from P3 on).

### U20. PMechDB regression tolerance for the joint open-shell model

A one-sided chain on closed_base val full-R top-1, where only drops count: Release A against the P0b GENERAL 3-seed mean, pm_control against Release A, and joint against pm_control, each link within max(2 x pooled SD of the link's 6 runs, 1 point). Decode writes per-sample hits from P0 on, and every link reports the paired one-sided McNemar test (`ksmech-eval compare`, P0b) beside it; joint is also reported against the P0b mean; multi-source runs state their pooled best.pt selection and pooled pair statistics. The same tolerance judges GENERAL against LEGACY in P0b, the metal with/without ablation in P4 and every flag ablation.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check made the rule one-sided with a pooled SD and a 1-point floor and added McNemar and the report against the baseline; the audit moved the reference to P0b and reused the tolerance for GENERAL vs LEGACY and the flags; consistency pass 2026-10-28: resolution 16 (one pooled-SD tolerance for every link, as P4 and P9 already had it); resolution 17 (hits from P0).. *Blocks:* P0b (tolerance); P9.

### U21. When does serve support radicals?

Staged: S1 Workbench after P4 steps 1-3 (data.spin and the tagged records), S2 pools and Correct after Release B, S3 Predict after P9. Serve tests stay green from P2b on; the 'radicals' capability levels are an API schema change; serve's Cu/Fe records use template geometry (+metal-geom-v1), not the engine's ETKDG, so the Workbench can show tokens the model never saw; S2 makes the floor, gbdt and the 3-class network refuse radical pools; U18 governs what S3 exposes. Each stage merges while no build, QM batch or training runs from main, with ksmech-serve stopped during the merge and started again after.

*Amended by:* factcheck: S1 needs only P4 steps 1-3; the capability schema change, the geometry difference, the predictor refusals in S2 and the U18 link were added.. *Blocks:* S1.

### U22. Downloads for external reference probes (GMTKN55 RSE43, St. John 2020 radicals, cited IP/EA)

RSE43 and St. John 2020 wait for D2, now a planned ablation after P9, with a data-license check and your approval of the download; scratch cache only, never trained on. Now, in P2: a dozen cited gas-phase IP/EA literature values for P2's small radicals, typed with citations (no download), compared only with gas-phase delta-SCF levels, never with ALPB-water eigenvalues.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check added the cited IP/EA values and the gas-phase-only comparison and corrected the why; the audit turned D2's trigger into a planned ablation, so the references wait for D2 itself.. *Blocks:* P2 (IP/EA values); D2.

### U23. How are the small-gap open-shell cells of restricted GFN2 handled in G?

A new pair column g2: G recomputed with each canonical-pair denominator dE_km replaced by sign(dE_km) * D_km, D_km = (|dE_km| + sqrt(dE_km^2 + 4 beta_rs^2))/2, on all cells, with beta_rs the Wolfsberg-Helmholz cross-centre coupling within a molecule and one stated constant between molecules; beta^2/D is the exact stabilization of the two-level problem. The legacy g is untouched, so the 0-ulp gate holds. No percentile delta and no SOMO -> degenerate-partner exclusion. P7 tabulates |G| by cell kind on the v18 store, where the cells excluded_pair used to delete are kept as flagged cells; P9 ablates g vs g2 vs both with 3 seeds. The intermolecular constant is frozen once P8 builds.

*Amended by:* factcheck + audit: the fact-check found that excluded_pair already removed the peroxyl -0.704 and HO2 -0.818 cells (ratio 1.4-6.7x, not 11x) and proposed the two-level form; the audit replaced the percentile delta (0.757) and the C6H6.- partner exclusion with g2 on all cells and beta from the coupling, keeping g; consistency pass 2026-10-28: resolutions 13 and 14 (g2 = G with sign(dE) D denominators; tabulation on v18, the 1.4-6.7x ratio having been measured with the cells deleted).. *Blocks:* P7.

## 3. Phases

| Phase | Depends on | Name |
|---|---|---|
| P0 | — | Merge claude/reactmech, guardrails, frozen split snapshots, the open-shell survey and doc drift (no data change) |
| P0a | P0 | Generality fixes and unified domain laws before any baseline (SAMPLE_VERSION 18) |
| P0b | P0a | Frozen closed-shell cohort closed_base and the 3-seed baseline (GENERAL reference, LEGACY ablation) |
| P1 | P0, P0a, P0b | Drawing-error repairs that return excluded steps (closed-shell polar steps drawn wrongly, not open shells) |
| P2 | P0, P0a | Open-shell QM probe, the spin-state scan and the theory rules (scratch cache only; produces spin_theory_v1 and spin_table_v1, decides nothing by a tuned threshold) |
| P2b | P0, P0a, P0b, P2 | Open-shell token rule for u > 0: localization blocks from GFN2's equal-occupation plateaus, the radical frontier and the FD f+/f- input variant (Release A) |
| P3 | P1, P2, P2b | Genuine organic radicals in polar PMechDB steps, admitted by counted laws with the hand reading as test oracle |
| P4 | P1, P2, P2b, P3 | Spin from RDKit plus two versioned tables (spin_table_v1, the Cu(II)/Fe(III) table): singlet carbenes, CO and the metal cohort under a per-molecule tag, then the Release A retraining |
| P5 | P0a, P1, P2b | ReactMech returns: QM, tokens and samples for the steps V1 (CDI) and the dative law gave back, the 20-case oxidation-state audit, re-scores with and without the changed steps |
| P6 | P0b, P4, P5 | Electron bookkeeping, one-electron labels, the electron-flow codes and the spin-aware identity key v2 (code; Release B, stacked branch 1 of 3) |
| P7 | P0a, P2b, P6 | SOMO donors, hole tokens, the SOMO population input, occupation-weighted G, the own-pair channel and the two-level g2 column (code; Release B, stacked branch 2 of 3) |
| P8 | P2, P6, P7 | Radical source rmechdb (FlowER RC+RS), one spin and domain law for every source, and the Release B build (stacked branch 3 of 3) |
| P9 | P8, P0b, P4 | Open-shell decoder head, training and ablations |
| S1 | P4 | Serve 1: Workbench orbital view of radicals and Cu(II)/Fe(III) (no prediction) |
| S2 | P8, S1 | Serve 2: radical pools, step checks and fishhook readings in Correct |
| S3 | P9, S2 | Serve 3: radical prediction |
| D1 | P8 | Deferred: official RMechDB arrows, classes and canonical split (trigger: you request and download the CSV) |
| D2 | P9 | Planned ablation after P9: spin-polarized GFN2 and level refinements for open shells |
| D3 | P9 | Deferred: radical chains from FlowER's USPTO NBS sequences and a trained END_MECH stop head (trigger: the FlowER full-ingest plan) |

### P0. Merge claude/reactmech, guardrails, frozen split snapshots, the open-shell survey and doc drift (no data change)

**Depends on:** —

**Goal.** Bring ReactMech onto main before any edit, make the silent radical code paths fail loudly, snapshot today's split assignment in a schema that survives every SAMPLE_VERSION bump (so P0a can change the data and old checkpoints stay evaluable without leakage), and survey the excluded steps that P0a, P1, P3 and P4 act on. No dataset artifact changes.

**Steps.**

1. [M] Stop the ksmech-serve instance that runs from main first: the merge edits tracked files and the copy rewrites data its browse index reads. Merge claude/reactmech whole as the first change on main. The last job in its worktree, reactmech/sparse_resonance_stop0, finished on 2026-10-28 at 19:02 (train.log: 'epoch 30 of 30 done'; best epoch 27, val top-1 0.920). `git merge-tree` of main f6aa758 and claude/reactmech 3e385d5 reports one conflict, docs/decisions.md, where both sides appended entries: keep both, in date order. Main's 4 commits since the merge base 5193306 touch only serve, the UI, docs/serve_architecture.md and decisions.md, so the engine files after the merge are the branch's (delta_be.py, hydrogens.py, steps.py, data/cli.py, ingest.py, reactmech.py, loss.py, train/cli.py, train/loop.py). Then copy the worktree's data into main's data/ with `cp -a -n --reflink=always` (btrfs, copy-on-write, no new extents), never hard links (U14: steps.parquet, resonance_pairs.parquet, the QM manifest, index.sqlite and metrics.jsonl are written in place and would write through): data/processed/reactmech (659 MB), data/models/reactmech (345 MB), data/models/pmechdb+reactmech (173 MB), the two run logs under data/models/, and the worktree-only files of data/cache/{qm,tokens,bond_index} (19,199 files in each, 19 GB apparent; the other 14,479 qm files are already hard links of main's, same inode, link count 2). With 20 GB free a plain copy is not an option: if --reflink=always fails, stop and do not fall back. data/processed/pmechdb is not copied: steps.parquet, splits_molecule.json and the three molecule views are byte-identical in both trees (sha256). data/cache/overlap_reactmech stays where it is (main's copies won for the 344 shared molecules). Then `ksmech-qm index refresh` (main's index.sqlite), `ksmech-data provenance` on every moved view, store and run from main, start ksmech-serve again from main (U11), remove the worktree and delete the merged branch. reactmech/sparse_resonance (NaN at epoch 19) is copied but never scored. Line numbers in P0-P2 are post-merge, i.e. the branch's engine files.
   - Files: docs/decisions.md (the only conflict), src/ksmech/arrows/delta_be.py, src/ksmech/arrows/hydrogens.py, src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py, src/ksmech/data/reactmech.py, src/ksmech/data/cli.py, src/ksmech/data/ingest.py, src/ksmech/model/loss.py, src/ksmech/train/cli.py, src/ksmech/train/loop.py, data/processed/reactmech, data/models/{reactmech,pmechdb+reactmech}, data/cache/{qm,tokens,bond_index} (reflink copies)

A checkpoint under pmechdb+reactmech/ decodes PMechDB val with --run-source. The floor's and the stored GBDT model's per-sample ranks reproduce their aggregate reports.
5. [M] Frozen split snapshots, taken here from today's assignment. A new kind 'split_frozen' with its own FROZEN_SPLIT_SCHEMA = 1, independent of SAMPLE_VERSION (today's split files are stamped kind 'split', schema_version 17, with steps.parquet as input, so P0a's bump would refuse them). Frozen files are stamped with no file inputs: a content hash, with the source hashes only in `extra`, so re-ingests never make them STALE_ARTIFACT. ReactMech molecule (7,346 / 918 / 919), scaffold and loco (5 held-out classes). Also the ReactMech pilot's 10,000 step ids: keep_sample (ingest.py:51-60) draws over the sorted accepted ids, so a rule that changes the accepted set would redraw the pilot; from now on `ingest --sample` reads the frozen id list and counts accepted steps outside it as not sampled. _assign_groups gains a frozen path with three rules. (1) A group that holds frozen steps keeps their part. (2) A group whose frozen steps sit in two parts sends its new steps to excluded as SPLIT_CONFLICT, counted. (3) A group with no frozen step is placed by sha256(group key, seed) against the fractions, not by the global permutation (splits.py:61) that reshuffles val and test when one step is added. A frozen step that a later rule excludes leaves its view and is counted as frozen-excluded. rmechdb is frozen the same way at its first build (P8). Three runs depend on the ReactMech splits: reactmech/sparse_resonance_stop0, pmechdb+reactmech/sparse_resonance (also on the PMechDB molecule split) and reactmech/sparse_resonance (NaN, unusable).

Unit: added groups never move an existing step; a bridging step is SPLIT_CONFLICT and counted; a repaired step keeps its step_id; an excluded frozen step is counted, not silently dropped; the pilot keeps its 10,000 ids when the accepted set grows.

Each report gives the covered fraction per reason code (spec tex:806 promises it; nothing implements it).

7. [M] A read-only `ksmech-data open-shell-survey --source pmechdb|reactmech` that writes open_shell_survey.json; steps.parquet is not touched. Each excluded step, and each kept step with a suspicious metal, gets a subclass: C3_METHYL (C with 3 radical electrons and one neighbour); CDI_IMIDAZOLYL (topological: a C in a 5-ring, degree 2, two N neighbours, 1 radical electron; RDKit does not flag it aromatic, so an aromatic test matches none of the 792 full-set cases); BETA_LACTAM_N; BARE_ALKALI; SN_PLACEHOLDER; AL_DRAWING; NITRONE_DRAWING; P_FOUR_BONDS; AMBIGUOUS_KETYL (the 3 'isopropoxide' steps: possibly sodium acetone ketyl radicals, not proven H_MISSING; they stay excluded and go to P3's candidate list); CARBENE; CO_DRAWN; N2_DRAWN; O3_DRAWN; RADICAL_SPECTATOR; RADICAL_REACTIVE; OSM_CU2; OSM_FE3; CU_SUSPECT (Cu=C and CuI2 drawings); CUH2_LIGAND; and the shapes P0a's laws act on: PLACEHOLDER_EVEN (a placeholder-shaped metal in an even fragment of a kept step), ZNCL_ION, ONIUM_PD_NI_EVEN, PD_DATIVE (odd Pd with an uncompensated onium), L1PD (the [Pd][P+]R3 drawing), PD_ALREADY_NEG, CR_IV; OTHER. These are survey labels, never domain codes: CO, N2 and ozone drawings are handled by P8's pairing rule (PAIRED_DRAWING), not by an O3_DRAWN or N2_DRAWN code. The survey also gives each metal's oxidation state (charge + onium donors as L + bond orders to other non-metals + H, metal-metal bonds by Pauling electronegativity) and d count, the radical atoms R touches, and the label status. All 130 PMechDB RADICAL_DRAWN + OPEN_SHELL_METAL cases are read by hand; the ids read as RADICAL_SPECTATOR or RADICAL_REACTIVE become P3's list. P0a, P1, P3 and P4 move the classifier functions into domain.py and data/spin.py, so this report is their test oracle.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/survey.py (new), src/ksmech/data/cli.py, tests/test_data_survey.py (new)
   - Tests: One hand-written fixture SMILES per subclass. realdata oracle, PMechDB: RADICAL_DRAWN 52 = 12 genuine radical steps (16 open-shell species) / 10 carbene / 2 CO drawn / 9 C3_METHYL (8 + 1 resonance) / 8 SN_PLACEHOLDER / 4 BARE_ALKALI / 3 AMBIGUOUS_KETYL / 1 BETA_LACTAM_N / 1 AL_DRAWING / 1 NITRONE_DRAWING / 1 P_FOUR_BONDS; OPEN_SHELL_METAL 78 = Fe 43 / Cu 35 (6 CuH2, 4 CuI2, 6 resonance); METAL_PLACEHOLDER 24 (the Cu=C step among them); kept PLACEHOLDER_EVEN 24 steps + ZNCL_ION 3; SPIN_PARITY 100 = Cu(II) 41 / Fe(III) 20 / placeholders 34 / Ti 4 / P 1. ReactMech pilot: RADICAL_DRAWN 62 = CDI_IMIDAZOLYL 62; OPEN_SHELL_METAL 98 = PD_DATIVE 96 + PD_ALREADY_NEG 2; METAL_PLACEHOLDER 109 = L1PD 109; kept ONIUM_PD_NI_EVEN 49 (25 Ni, 24 Pd; 20 train, 29 val) and CR_IV 10 (test, product side).

Fixture SMILES are written by hand, never copied from a dataset. Every featurization function changed in later phases gets a test on them.

9. [S] Fix documentation drift. data_schema.md: the domain_exclusion row omits RADICAL_DRAWN, and the paragraph on bare metals still calls them spectators. decisions.md, 2026-10-24 'QM retry' entries (lines 3575 and 3670 before the merge): the 100 SPIN_PARITY molecules are not 'Fe radicals' but Cu(II) 41, Fe(III) 20, placeholders 34, Ti charge errors 4 and 1 P. CLAUDE.md says the orbitals are 'Pipek-Mezey localized ... started deterministically from the canonical MOs'; the code default is IBO (Lowdin populations, exponent 4) started from a pivoted Cholesky factorization of each block's density matrix (localize.py PMConfig: method 'ibo', start 'cholesky'), which spec tex:140 and :395 already state; Pipek-Mezey with Mulliken populations is only the comparison. CLAUDE.md's FlowER row says about 1.4M steps; the numeric-suffix set is about 2.14M lines (fact-check U17). CLAUDE.md's command list gains the survey, --run-source, --cohort and --slice. Record the merge, the reflink copy and the frozen schema in decisions.md with the date.

   - Tests: None (documentation); ruff and pytest stay green.

**Data effect.** None on any dataset artifact. ReactMech's views, store, caches and runs are copied unchanged from the worktree into main's data/ by reflink. New artifacts only: the frozen split files (PMechDB molecule and scaffold, ReactMech molecule, scaffold and loco, the pilot's 10,000 ids) and open_shell_survey.json per source. Reports gain fields (sample versions, run source, per-sample hits, cohort, slices, covered fraction).

**Rebuilds and bumps.** FLOOR_VERSION 1 -> 2 and DECODE_VERSION 1 -> 2 (report fields only). FROZEN_SPLIT_SCHEMA = 1, new and independent of SAMPLE_VERSION. No sample, token or QM bump. The copy costs no QM (the pilot's QM took about 408 CPU-h) and no new extents (reflink). Compute: the survey (minutes), provenance checks, one flex_first decode of PMechDB val for the reproduction check (1,239 samples at about 1.2 s each, about 25 min CPU), and the two ReactMech runs on ReactMech molecule val (908 samples each, about 18 min each at the same rate; estimate, sparse decode speed not measured). Re-scoring and training move to P0b.

**Exit criteria.** pytest green, both summary lines pasted. claude/reactmech is merged with ksmech-serve stopped, its data verifies from main, ksmech-serve was started again from main, the worktree is removed and the branch deleted. Re-splitting reproduces the ids per part of every frozen view, and the frozen files survive a scratch SAMPLE_VERSION bump. The survey matches its oracle: PMechDB RADICAL_DRAWN 52 = 12 / 10 / 2 / 9 / 8 / 4 / 3 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1, OPEN_SHELL_METAL 78 = Fe 43 / Cu 35, SPIN_PARITY 100 = 41 / 20 / 34 / 4 / 1, kept PLACEHOLDER_EVEN 24 + ZNCL_ION 3; ReactMech pilot CDI 62, PD_DATIVE 96, PD_ALREADY_NEG 2, L1PD 109, ONIUM_PD_NI_EVEN 49, CR_IV 10. On today's v17 store these reproduce exactly with the new report fields: the floor (val 15.6 / 26.0 / 31.1 on 1,239, test 17.8 / 27.3 / 31.6 on 1,182, top-1/3/5), the stored GBDT model (val 44.1 / 62.0 / 70.8 on 1,203, test 48.8 / 67.7 / 75.1 on 1,120) and flex_first decode (val 63.0 / 82.6 / 85.4), now with per-sample ranks and hits; the two ReactMech runs' per-sample hits on ReactMech molecule val are written.

**Risks.** --reflink=always needs both trees on the same btrfs filesystem; if it fails the move stops (a plain copy would take 19 GB of the 20 GB free). ksmech-serve must not run from main during the merge and the copy (tracked edits; the browse index hashes steps.parquet): stop it first and start it once the copy is verified. A frozen split cannot be quietly corrected later: any re-split becomes an explicit new frozen version. The survey heuristics can mislabel, which is why all 130 PMechDB cases are read by hand. The version check must warn rather than refuse, or flex_first (v15) and the pod runs (v16) cannot be re-scored.

### P0a. Generality fixes and unified domain laws before any baseline (SAMPLE_VERSION 18)

**Depends on:** P0

**Goal.** Remove the hand rules that change or delete steps or cells already used for training, before the closed-shell baseline is pinned: the audit's code items 1-5 (each new behaviour behind a flag whose default is today's, so old checkpoints and the floor reproduce), the stored FD f+/f- and exact-degeneracy frontier columns (resolutions 5 and 6; one additive token rebuild), a reach report of the thresholds nobody resolved (resolution 7), and one law per domain phenomenon (dative onium-to-metal bonds, ligand-dropped metal placeholders, metal centres whose spin state electron counting does not fix), applied to every element and every parity and counted. Rules that only return excluded steps stay in P1 (PMechDB) and P5 (ReactMech QM).

**Steps.**

1. [L] Keep every (occupied, virtual) cell in the store and turn the exclusion rules into pair channels (audit code items 1 and 2). Today valid_pairs (physics/pairs.py:43-67) deletes three kinds of cells and the store keeps only pair_valid cells (store.py:11-17, VALID_ONLY :38): acceptors that are d-type (tokens.py:183-186, main centre mostly d on an element outside VALENCE_D, groups 3-11), the donor's own antibond (is_antibonding_partner) and site self-pairs (site_excluded_matrix, site_weight 0.20, 1.3 r_cov). Measured by the audit on the v17 store: d-type tokens are 10.4% of virtual tokens, and in 1,520 of 9,997 PMechDB train samples (15.2%) the filter removes over half of the gold anchor's pooled weight (O-Si 265, Br-C 170, C-S 154, O-P 150, Al-O 105); excluded_pair removes 1.6% of cells, among them the arrow's own orbital pair in 237 train and 34 val samples; pair_valid is about 89% of cells. Change: a new pairs.pair_flags returns three 0/1 (n_occ, n_virt) arrays, d_acceptor, own_antibond and site_self, stored as binary pair features (BINARY_FEATURES, bit-packed, SCALING entries 'none'); pair_valid stays stored as the v17 mask; the store keeps pair_z, pair_defined and pair_gap_sign on every cell; the orbital-pair labels are stored unfiltered (the legacy label filter is applied at load and still counted as label_filter). ModelConfig gains pair_cells: 'valid' (default, today) zeroes z and defined outside pair_valid before standardization and pools G^atom and y_ij over pair_valid only, bit-identical to v17; 'all' feeds every cell, the three flags and pair_valid as channels (pair_channels) and as pooled means in y_ij, and pools over every cell. standardized_pair_z selects the checkpoint's pair_stats.names from the batch's superset by name (today it raises when they differ, inputs.py:111-125), so v15-v17 checkpoints read the v18 store. Pair statistics are written for both cell sets. The GBDT (physics/lgbm.py, physics/baseline.py) and the floor (eval/floor.py) take --cells valid|all, default valid; with all, hard and random negatives come from every cell and the flags are features. The fractional band (0.2 < n < 1.8) is untouched here (P2b, P7); its reach is measured in step 9.

4. [S] ReactMech labels from the Kekule structure of the product closest to the reactant (audit code item 3). A step without arrows takes R as the difference of its mapped sides (mapped_matrix, delta_be.py:181-210), and parse_mapped kekulizes each side with RDKit's own choice, so an unchanged aromatic ring often enters R as an alternating cycle of +-1 entries, and every product form enumerated from that product carries it. dataset_matrix's no-arrow branch (:215-232) takes R = without_kekule_cycles(mapped_matrix(reac, prod), reac) (only bonds that stay bonds take part), so the product it applies, checks and enumerates (product_forms) is the Kekule structure closest to the reactant; drawings.kekule_closest shares the helper. resonance.resonance_forms is unchanged: KEKULE_ALL would multiply the class while RESONANCE_CAP already truncates 41% of ReactMech labels (2,964 of 7,189 train). Steps with arrows (PMechDB) are untouched. Measured (estimate: alternating cycles removed from the stored v17 class variants, scratchpad revise/caps_scan.py): 836 ReactMech labels shrink (695 train, 66 val, 75 test; the audit counted 759 of 8,097 train+val with a cycle in every variant); labels with more than 8 bond entries go from 212 / 13 / 12 to 0; 0 PMechDB labels change. 6. [M] Unified domain laws in data/domain.py, replacing R2 (domain.py:103-108) and the element- and parity-gated R3 and U5 repairs. (a) Dative law. An onium donor is a positively charged non-metal other than C bonded to an acceptor (a metal, or B). For each acceptor, u = its onium donors minus the negative charge it already carries (never below 0). In each fragment, the acceptors' charges are lowered by the largest j <= sum(u) that leaves the fragment's electron count even, keeps the side's net charge >= 0 and gives both sides of the step the same total; otherwise the drawing stays. The step then re-validates (charge balance, maps, arrow replay where arrows exist) and falls back to the unrepaired screen. Rule 'D dative onium->M', logged in charge_repair and charge_repairs.csv. This is closed-shell electron bookkeeping for every element and every parity: on odd fragments it reproduces R2's and R3's targets, on even fragments it re-charges the two-onium Pd/Ni drawings of U5, and it leaves correctly charged cation adducts alone. A 20-case oxidation-state audit runs before the build and needs no QM (scripts/probes/dative_audit.py: 20 repairs drawn at a fixed seed, stratified over the 49 even Pd/Ni re-charges, the 96 odd-Pd and the 109 L1Pd returns; each read by hand: the repaired fragment's oxidation state from the survey's helper, now domain.oxidation_state, equals the one the chemistry implies (Pd(0)L1/L2, Pd(II)L2XR, Ni(0)L2, Ni(II)L2XR, or the cationic Pd-PyOx +1 its PF6- matches), the d count, the counterion and the pool balance); it is recorded in decisions.md, and a failed case stops P0a's build until the law is fixed, because after P0b the law could no longer change training steps. R1 (a -1 on a four-bonded group-14 atom) stays. The law applies to every source; rmechdb meets it at its first ingest in P8 (measured effect there: at most 1 line). (b) Placeholder law, any parity, checked on every fragment before every other code: a non-s-block metal with exactly one neighbour (H counted) and formal charge <= 0, unless that neighbour is an onium donor the metal compensates (an ML1 complex: the L1Pd(0) drawing [Pd-][P+]R3). A free atom or ion (no neighbour) is a defined species, and MX+ with the charge drawn on the metal ([Cl][Zn+]) is a defined ion; neither is a placeholder. (c) METAL_SUSPECT, a flag and never an exclusion: an even fragment with a first-row transition metal (Sc to Cu) whose oxidation-state bookkeeping (onium donors as L, other bonds to the more electronegative partner, metal-metal bonds by Pauling electronegativity, H as hydride) leaves an open d shell (d1-d9): 3d ligand-field splittings are comparable to the pairing energy, so the closed-shell singlet the QM computes is an assumption. Flagged in domain_class and reported as a slice; P2's scan measures these species. (d) The nullable STEP_SCHEMA column domain_class (null, REPAIRED_DATIVE, METAL_SUSPECT; P1, P3, P4 and P8 add their values), moved here from P1 because P0a writes the first values. training_steps takes a per-source admit set, recorded in provenance. stats.json counts every distinct domain_exclusion and domain_class value, not only the PRECEDENCE codes (stats.py:124-144), and DEFINITIONS is updated. Measured by a read-only simulation of (a)-(c) on today's steps (scratchpad revise/sim_laws_c.py, sim_laws_e.py): PMechDB, (a) changes 0 steps (R2 never fired: charge_repairs.csv holds one R1 repair); (b) removes 24 kept steps (22 samples, molecule split 21 train and 1 val, plus 2 resonance pairs: C=[O+][M] and C[O][M] for Ti, Zn, Sc, Cu and Fe, and a Cu carbenoid), keeps the 3 [Cl][Zn+] steps (val), moves the 8 Sn steps from RADICAL_DRAWN to METAL_PLACEHOLDER, and keeps all 24 current METAL_PLACEHOLDER steps (Cu=C among them); (c) flags 0 (the 13 Gilman cuprates [Li][Cu]Ar2 read Cu(I) d10). ReactMech pilot: (a) re-charges the 49 even onium Pd/Ni steps (+2/+3 -> 0/+1; 25 Ni, 24 Pd; 20 train, 29 val) and returns 205 excluded steps (109 METAL_PLACEHOLDER L1Pd(0) and 96 OPEN_SHELL_METAL); 2 stay OPEN_SHELL_METAL (Pd already -1); (b) changes no kept step, and its free-ion clause keeps the 4 returned Pd steps that carry a spectator [Ag+]; (c) flags 35 kept steps (Cr(IV) H2CrO3 10, test; Ni(II) d8 25: 9 train, 16 val). The literal form 'metal -1 per onium donor' with only the pool gate was rejected on measurement: it would re-charge 101 PMechDB kept steps (Li, Na, K, Mg, Ca, Sc, Cu, Zn and B onium adducts such as [Li][O+]=C, whose drawn charge is right) and 58 more ReactMech ones (51 [OH2+][Mg], 7 [OH2+]B), and it would leave odd two-onium Pd fragments odd.

   - Tests: Hand-written fixtures, never dataset SMILES. Repaired: a [Pd][P+](C)(C)C + PhBr -> Pd(II) step (both sides -1); a Ni(0)(PMe3)2 -> Ni(II)(Cl)(CH3)(PMe3)2 step drawn +2 on both sides with two P+ (metal -2).
