New QM: about 59 unique re-charged Pd/Ni fragments, 2-4 CPU-h (fact-check U5), plus their Wiberg sidecars and tokens. Samples: about 10 min for PMechDB (samples_build.log: 590 s) and a similar time for ReactMech (estimate); the GBDT about 100 s to load and 100-120 s to fit each model (fact-check U6). Disk: the stores grow about 11-12% (PMechDB 1.2 GB, ReactMech 0.65 GB today; estimate). ksmech-serve is stopped for the commit and the rebuild and started after. Spec v9.1 is approved before the build.

**Exit criteria.** pytest green, both summary lines pasted. p0a_changes.json lists every changed, removed, returned and flagged step by rule, and its counts equal the P0 survey oracle and the simulation: PMechDB placeholder -24 (21 train, 1 val, 2 resonance), Sn 8 relabelled, dative 0, METAL_SUSPECT 0; ReactMech pilot dative 49 re-charged and 205 returned, 2 still OPEN_SHELL_METAL, METAL_SUSPECT 35, KEKULE_CLOSEST about 836. With every new flag off, the floor, the stored GBDT model and flex_first decode reproduce per sample on the unchanged PMechDB samples (test floor 17.8 / 27.3 / 31.6 exactly), and the two ReactMech runs on the unchanged ReactMech samples. Every rebuilt token file equals its schema-9 payload on every legacy field. The 20-case oxidation-state audit is clean. threshold_reach.json is written for both sources. No step_id changes and no frozen step changes part. The GENERAL configuration (pair_cells all, orbital_extra set, caps labels; frontier_blocks and the FD columns stay off until P9) runs a smoke fit (`--limit 64 --epochs 1`) and a decode on v18. decisions.md holds the entries of the last step.

**Risks.** The dative law re-charges ReactMech steps that have no arrows to replay; the safeguards are the evenness and pool clauses, equal totals on both sides and the 20-case oxidation-state audit. The placeholder law's charge clause keeps a one-ligand metal whose charge is drawn on the metal; a future source could draw a ligand-dropped Lewis acid that way (the survey counts the shape per source). METAL_SUSPECT rests on the first-row versus heavier-row ligand-field argument; it only flags. Name-based input selection is what keeps old checkpoints usable on v18; it is tested on a v17-shaped fixture and by the reproduction gate. The 205 returned ReactMech steps sit admitted without samples until P5, counted as MISSING_TOKENS by domain_class, never silently. The token rebuild relies on deterministic localization: a legacy field that differs stops the build. Serve's own token files under data/serve/ carry schema 9 after the bump; the serve tests in this exit must show they are recomputed or refused, never read silently.

### P0b. Frozen closed-shell cohort closed_base and the 3-seed baseline (GENERAL reference, LEGACY ablation)

**Depends on:** P0a

**Goal.** Pin the closed-shell cohort every later phase is measured against on the v18 store after P0a, re-score what exists, and decide with seeds whether the generality fixes cost accuracy, so each remaining flag default is backed by an ablation.

**Steps.**

1. [S] Pin closed_base (the name replaces closed_v17 everywhere). data/processed/pmechdb/cohort_closed_base.json, in the frozen schema (no file inputs, content hash): the step ids of the PMechDB molecule split after P0a (expected 12,396 samples: train 9,976, val 1,238, test 1,182; counted at the build). Beside it, the GBDT subsets with complete ArrowLabels for each cell set (valid: val 1,203 and test 1,120 on v17, re-counted on v18; all: counted). Per-sample digests per named column on the v18 store go to baseline_closed_base.json: atom_categorical[:, :2], the pair_z and pair_defined columns by the v18 names (the v17 names plus the three flag features), pair_valid, orbital_features by descriptor name (with the two new columns), the atom features by name (with the FD f+/f- and exact-frontier columns), the labels and the ids. Later phases add columns without failing the gate.
   - Files: data/processed/pmechdb/cohort_closed_base.json (artifact), data/processed/pmechdb/baseline_closed_base.json (artifact), src/ksmech/eval/cli.py (cohort pin command)
   - Tests: realdata: the cohort reloads from a re-ingest unchanged; the digests reproduce on a second build of the same store.
2. [M] Re-score what exists on closed_base val and test (float32), all with per-sample hits: the floor with --cells valid and all; the GBDT legacy refit from P0a (the new GBDT reference) and the GENERAL GBDT (--cells all, flags as features) on their own denominators; the old checkpoints with every flag off: flex_first (the only PMechDB checkpoint on disk), pmechdb+reactmech/sparse_resonance (--run-source pmechdb+reactmech, on closed_base and on ReactMech's frozen molecule val and test), reactmech/sparse_resonance_stop0 (ReactMech). The pod weights of dense_plain, dense_resonance, flex_resonance and sparse_resonance are not on disk (only SHA256SUMS.pod and run.json, which says sample_version 16); if the user supplies them, their sha256 is checked against SHA256SUMS.pod and they are re-scored the same way. reactmech/sparse_resonance (NaN at epoch 19) is not scored.
   - Files: src/ksmech/eval/cli.py, data/models/*/decode_report_* and per-sample hit files (artifacts)
   - Tests: The floor on closed_base test equals the P0a gate's numbers; flex_first's per-sample hits on closed_base val equal P0a's.
3. [M] Train dense_plain (encoder dense, no resonance draws, 30 epochs) with seeds 0, 1 and 2 in two configurations on closed_base train: GENERAL (pair_cells all, orbital_extra ('retained_fraction', 'energy_ev'); frontier_blocks 'window' and the FD columns unselected, both P9 ablations) = the reference (U2), and LEGACY (every new flag off) = the ablation that justifies dropping the old rules. Decode val with caps legacy and caps labels, per-sample hits stored. Test is read once, for the selected configuration.
   - Files: src/ksmech/train/cli.py (a --preset general|legacy that sets the ModelConfig flags), data/models/pmechdb/dense_plain_{general,legacy}_s{0,1,2}/ (runs)
   - Tests: Each run's run.json records sample_version 18, the flags and the seed; a smoke fit of each preset passes in pytest.
4. [S] Decide, U20-style and one-sided. GENERAL fails against LEGACY if its mean val decode top-1 is lower by more than max(2 x pooled SD of the 6 runs, 1 point). `ksmech-eval compare` (new here: it reads the hit files of two arms and gives the one-sided exact McNemar test on majority-of-3 hits, the 9 seed-pair tests, the per-arm means and the pooled SD) reports the paired test beside it; a significant McNemar result inside the tolerance is recorded and explained in decisions.md. If GENERAL fails, leave-one-flag-out runs (pair_cells, orbital_extra; 3 seeds each) find the flag that costs accuracy, and that flag keeps the legacy default with the evidence in decisions.md. Report the old checkpoints next to the new means, so drift is visible.
   - Files: src/ksmech/eval/paired.py (new), src/ksmech/eval/cli.py (compare), docs/decisions.md, data/processed/pmechdb/baseline_closed_base.json
   - Tests: Unit: the tolerance and the McNemar test on synthetic hit vectors; McNemar on a hand-made pair of hit vectors matches the exact binomial value; compare refuses arms decoded on different cohorts or decode versions.

**Data effect.** None on any dataset artifact: the cohort file, the baseline file, reports, per-sample hit files and six (up to twelve) runs.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** None. Compute: 6 dense_plain runs of about 45 min each on a pod (dense_plain's 30 epochs took 45.1 min there, metrics.jsonl), about 4.5 GPU-h, longer on the RX 6600 XT; up to 6 more if leave-one-flag-out is needed. CPU decoding at about 1.2 s per sample per checkpoint (flex_first's report: 1.18 s): about 48 min per checkpoint for closed_base val and test (2,420 samples), about 36 min for ReactMech val and test (1,810).

**Exit criteria.** closed_base is pinned with its content hash and per-column digests. The floor, both GBDTs and the old checkpoints are recorded on closed_base with per-sample hits. The 3 GENERAL and 3 LEGACY seeds are recorded with mean and spread; the decision, the tolerance and the McNemar p-value are in decisions.md, and every flag default has its ablation. pytest green, summary line pasted.

**Risks.** A 3-seed SD has 2 degrees of freedom per arm, hence the pooled SD over 6 runs and the 1-point floor. Val is also the selection set, slightly optimistic for every arm alike. Pod runs must use the v18 stores pushed to rhoMech-data. If the pod weights never arrive, the old PMechDB comparison rests on flex_first alone.

### P1. Drawing-error repairs that return excluded steps (closed-shell polar steps drawn wrongly, not open shells)

**Depends on:** P0, P0a, P0b

**Goal.** Return the steps excluded only because a closed-shell species was drawn with missing H or a missing charge. These exclusions look like open shells and are not. No spin, QM method, token or model change, and no kept step changes (the rules that change kept steps ran in P0a).

**Steps.**

1. [M] Rule V1, H completion, limited to signatures that cannot be read as a radical: a C with 3 radical electrons and one neighbour (the [C:20]-type methyls), and the imidazol-2-yl C of CDI, recognized by topology (a C in a 5-ring, degree 2, two N neighbours, 1 radical electron; RDKit does not flag it aromatic, so an aromatic test would match none of the 792 full-set cases). In each case the atom must be drawn the same way on both sides (same map number, or an unmapped spectator). V1 adds the H count with NoImplicit, or a mapped H with a fresh map number where the source draws H explicitly, and runs before the RADICAL_DRAWN check. A carbon with 1 radical electron is never completed: structure cannot tell the 3 AMBIGUOUS_KETYL steps (possibly sodium acetone ketyls) from a genuine radical such as the Birch [CH]; they stay excluded and go to P3's candidate list. V1 reaches ReactMech in the same re-ingest (P5 computes its QM) and mech-USPTO's 92 CDI cases once that loader exists. It applies to every source; P8 meets it on rmechdb (measured: at most 1 RC line has the shape).
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/domain.py (screen_side, before the RADICAL_DRAWN check), tests/test_data_domain.py
   - Tests: Repaired: the [C:20] methyl and O=C(n1[c]ncc1)n1[c]ncc1. Not repaired: a peroxyl, C[C](C)[O][Na], the Birch step [CH]1C=CC=C[CH:11]1[Na:10], Cl[C]Cl, and a radical drawn on one side only. A repair never changes step_id.
2. [S] The beta-lactam N (pmechdb-65a6c92b04e820b5, N:21 drawn without its H on both sides) is a named per-step repair, not a rule: one row (step id, atom map, change, reason) in a small tracked repair table, counted under BL_NAMED, with its own decisions.md entry. Any later per-step repair needs its own decision.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/domain.py, src/ksmech/data/repairs_v1.json (new, tracked)
   - Tests: The named repair applies to that step only and re-validates with its arrows replayed.
3. [S] Rule A1: a bare neutral alkali atom (group 1) with no neighbour gets charge +1 only when the same atom (same map number, or the same unmapped spectator) is drawn identically on both sides, so a metal that reacts, such as the Birch Na, is never touched. 4 steps (037a93db, 4592f8b0 with K; 5bf7fb8d, 946b7e9b with Na). No new ion QM: [K+] and [Na+] are cached.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/domain.py
   - Tests: Fixtures with [K] and [Na] spectators re-validate; a [Na] that changes between the sides is not repaired.
4. [S] The Sn relabel is already done by P0a's placeholder law (the 8 Sn steps of degree 1 with 2 or 3 radical electrons moved from RADICAL_DRAWN to METAL_PLACEHOLDER); P1 only checks the count. The one Al step (29a4a03883007779; Al of degree 3 and 2) stays RADICAL_DRAWN, as do the nitrone ef20cdaad5325eca and the four-bonded P e57aaee9d354b63c.
   - Files: tests/test_data_domain.py (realdata count)
   - Tests: realdata: METAL_PLACEHOLDER holds the 8 Sn steps; the Al, nitrone and P steps stay RADICAL_DRAWN.
5. [S] Re-validate every repair (charge balance, maps, and arrow replay where arrows exist), falling back to the unrepaired screen. Log V1, A1 and BL_NAMED in the charge_repair JSON, count them per rule in ingest_report.json, charge_repairs.csv and stats.json (which since P0a counts every code), and set domain_class REPAIRED_V1, REPAIRED_A1 or REPAIRED_BL. Each later phase adds its own codes to PRECEDENCE.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/validation.py, src/ksmech/data/ingest.py, src/ksmech/data/stats.py, src/ksmech/data/domain.py
   - Tests: The ingest report test covers the new counts; every repaired PMechDB step replays its arrows.
6. [M] Rebuild (spec v9.1, approved before P0a's build, already lists V1, A1 and the named repair). Re-ingest PMechDB and ReactMech: `ksmech-data ingest --source pmechdb`, `--source reactmech --sample 10000 --seed 0` (frozen pilot ids), `stats`. PMechDB: `ksmech-qm run --source pmechdb --workers 8` (a top-up for the H-completed species: the morphinans, the methyl-oxonium and the beta-lactam pair, a few dozen small molecules, minutes), `ksmech-qm bond-index`, `ksmech-orbitals build --source pmechdb --workers 4`, `ksmech-arrows samples --source pmechdb` (SAMPLE_VERSION stays 18), then `ksmech-physics gbdt`, whose refit is recorded as the new GBDT reference. The 13 returned steps are placed by the frozen-split hash path and form the 'added' cohort; closed_base does not change. ReactMech: the 62 pilot CDI steps (792 on the full set) are admitted at ingest; `ksmech-arrows samples --source reactmech` and its pair stats are rebuilt (the re-ingest makes them stale) and skip the CDI steps as MISSING_TOKENS, counted, until P5 computes their QM.
   - Files: (CLI runs)
   - Tests: The build logs every skip with its reason and the counts at the end.
7. [S] Record each rule with its date and decision (U4) in decisions.md and document it in data_schema.md; spec tex:806 'Domain coverage' lists V1, A1 and the named beta-lactam repair.
   - Files: docs/decisions.md, docs/data_schema.md, docs/ksmech_architecture.tex:806
   - Tests: None (documentation).

**Data effect.** PMechDB gains 13 training steps (8 [C]-methyls, 4 bare K/Na, 1 beta-lactam N-H) and 1 resonance pair (the [C]-methyl step that is an allyl-cation shift). RADICAL_DRAWN 44 -> 30 (12 genuine radical steps, 10 carbenes, 2 CO drawn, 3 ambiguous ketyls, Al, nitrone, P); METAL_PLACEHOLDER stays 56. ReactMech pilot: the 62 CDI steps are admitted (no samples until P5); with P0a's 205, 267 of the 269 pilot exclusions are admitted and 2 stay OPEN_SHELL_METAL. Full set: about 3,121 of 3,165 (fact-check simulation). mech-USPTO's 92 CDI cases come back when its loader exists (it stays held out). closed_base is unchanged.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** Re-ingesting steps.parquet makes the QM manifest, the views, the samples, the pair statistics and the GBDT stale, and they are rebuilt; frozen split and cohort files stay valid. SAMPLE_VERSION 18, token SCHEMA_VERSION 10 (since P0a) and QMRecord SCHEMA_VERSION 1 do not change; existing records and tokens are cache hits. New QM: a few dozen small molecules (minutes). Samples about 10 min, GBDT a few minutes.

**Exit criteria.** Per-rule counts equal the P0 survey (C3_METHYL 9, BARE_ALKALI 4, BETA_LACTAM_N 1, CDI_IMIDAZOLYL 62 in the pilot). Every repaired PMechDB step re-validates with its arrows replayed. No step_id changes, the ReactMech pilot keeps its 10,000 ids and no frozen step changes part. On closed_base the per-column digests, the floor and flex_first decode are identical to P0b. pytest green, summary line pasted.

**Risks.** A wrong H completion changes chemistry without any error. The mitigations are the narrow V1 signatures, the both-sides rule, arrow replay on PMechDB and the logged charge_repairs.csv. The CDI rule meets ReactMech without arrows, so its only checks there are the topological test and the mapped balance. Named per-step repairs must not grow into a list: each one needs its own decision.

### P2. Open-shell QM probe, the spin-state scan and the theory rules (scratch cache only; produces spin_theory_v1 and spin_table_v1, decides nothing by a tuned threshold)

**Depends on:** P0, P0a

**Goal.** Before any open-shell artifact is written, measure what restricted GFN2 gets right or wrong for each species class at the geometries production uses, and replace the hand-written carbene and CO clauses by a measured, versioned species table. Every multiplicity is derived twice, independently, and the two are compared (user amendment, 2026-10-30): (A) spGFN2 at every allowed multiplicity (step 2), (B) theory rules coded as principles: ligand field, group and representation theory, Hund's rule (step 3), (C) one combination rule (step 4). The metal numbers of step 1 are diagnostics of the method; the metals' multiplicity rows come from C, and their place in training from P4's 3-seed ablation.

**Steps.**

1. [M] Probe script: restricted and spGFN2 single points (Calculator.add('spin-polarization', 1.0)) on (1) the OPEN_SHELL fixtures, including superoxide, C6H6.-, CO and N2; (2) the 61 PMechDB Cu(II)/Fe(III) species (SPIN_PARITY Cu(II) 41 + Fe(III) 20) at the table multiplicity and at the geometry P4 will produce: restricted tblite L-BFGS from the engine's embedding, ETKDG + UFF where UFF has parameters (the four-coordinate [Fe-] species such as FeBr4- and FeCl3.L) and raw ETKDG for FeCl3 and the Cu species (embed.py:70-81; fact-check U7); (3) the METAL_SUSPECT species of P0a as extra diagnostic rows (H2CrO3, Cr(IV) d2, and the Ni(II) d8 fragments of the re-charged ReactMech steps, whose QM exists after P0a). For each run record the energy per multiplicity; the occupations (integral, degenerate or smeared) and max|n - aufbau| as a distribution, not only the fermi_smeared flag; which block (occupied or virtual, by today's 0.5 threshold) each open MO lands in (NO's half-filled pi* pair sits on it and floating-point noise already splits it, U16); the max |dq| and, separately, the restricted-versus-spGFN2 |dq| on the non-metal atoms the arrows touch (the Br of Br2.FeBr3, the carbonyl O bound to Cu); eps_SOMO against the alpha-HOMO/beta-LUMO midpoint; the time per run. These are diagnostics: there is no 0.1 e go/no-go. Records go to a scratch cache root inside the ignored data/cache (data/cache/probe_open_shell/), keyed as usual, so every QM result stays cached and nothing reaches the production caches; the exception is recorded in decisions.md.
   - Files: scripts/probes/open_shell_probe.py (new), src/ksmech/qm/embed.py:70-81 (read, unchanged), src/ksmech/qm/geometry.py (read, unchanged), src/ksmech/qm/xtb_calc.py (read, unchanged), docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: Reproduces the known [CH3] values: restricted SOMO -8.911 eV; spGFN2 alpha HOMO -9.687 / beta LUMO -8.150 eV. Ethane dE = 0.00.
2. [M] (A) One-off spGFN2 scan at every allowed multiplicity. Each species is optimized at every multiplicity its electron count allows (u, u - 2, ... down to 0 or 1) in ALPB water, as the production records, and the adiabatic energies are recorded.
   - Organic species: every carbene-, CO-, ozone- and u >= 2-shaped species of PMechDB, ReactMech and FlowER RS/RC. Measured: PMechDB 8 (Cl[C]Cl, Br[C]Br and three vinylidenes in 10 steps, [C]=O, [O][O], the Et3B-O2 adduct CC[B-](CC)(CC)[O+][O]); ReactMech pilot 0 (its u >= 2 drawings are CDI dimers and [C]-methyls, which V1 repairs); FlowER RS/RC about 56 u >= 2 species (fact-check U19; the list comes from step 6's census, which runs first), every state u, u - 2, ... for u >= 3, with [C]=O, the ozone drawings [O]O[O] and [O][O+][O-], and [N]=[N] as a check (repaired in P8).
   - Metal species of step 1: Fe(III) d5 at S = 5/2, 3/2 and 1/2; mononuclear Cu(II) d9 at its single state S = 1/2, recorded as a control; a polynuclear species at every total spin its metal centres allow, enumerated. The METAL_SUSPECT rows (Cr(IV) d2, Ni(II) d8) at S = 1 and 0, as diagnostics.
   - A's verdict for a species is its lowest state when that state lies below every other by more than the method's error bar; otherwise A is inconclusive. The organic error bar is spGFN2's published singlet-triplet accuracy (AC12: about 7 kcal/mol, 0.30 eV; Moradi et al. 2024, arXiv 2405.05761). AC12 is organic only: a published spGFN2 error bar for 3d spin-state energetics is looked up and stated once in decisions.md with its citation, and until there is one, A alone decides no metal species. The bounds are the method's error bars, not tuned.
   - Files: scripts/probes/spin_scan.py (new), docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: Every species records the energy of every allowed state, or a counted failure reason per state; the Fe(III) species carry three states and the Cu(II) control one.
3. [M] (B) The theory rules, coded as principles, in a separate, versioned, tracked table spin_theory_v1, written by one module from the structure and the restricted GFN2 record. Per species it records the point group, d^n, the geometry class, the predicted S and the rule chain that gave it, or 'undetermined'. No rule names a species.
   - d^n from data.domain.oxidation_state and d_count, the functions the dative law already uses.
   - Ligand field: the coordination number and the geometry class, read from the production geometry, give the d splitting; Delta against the pairing energy is read from a cited spectrochemical series (Tsuchida's, as tabulated in a standard inorganic text; the citation goes in the table). In Oh, d1-d3, d8, d9 and d10 have a single outcome; d4-d7 depend on the field, and a ligand in the middle of the series gives 'undetermined'. Td is high spin. A Jahn-Teller-active configuration is annotated, not decided by it. A geometry class with no rule gives 'undetermined'.
   - Group and representation theory: the point group at a tolerance (the plain-numpy detector serve already has, serve/workbench/symmetry.py:781, moves to an engine module that serve re-imports, because the engine never imports serve); the frontier irreps from the GFN2 MOs (the characters of the group operations on the MO coefficients); for a degenerate open shell, Hund's rule and the ground term (O2's pi_g*^2 gives 3Sigma_g-).
   - Carbenes: the Gleiter-Hoffmann criterion on the sigma-p_pi gap of the carbene centre from the restricted GFN2 MOs: singlet above about 2 eV, triplet below about 1.5 eV (Gleiter and Hoffmann, J. Am. Chem. Soc. 90, 5457 (1968); Bourissou et al., Chem. Rev. 100, 39 (2000)), 'undetermined' between.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/spin_theory.py (new; read by step 4 and the tests, never by data.spin), src/ksmech/data/spin_tables/theory_v1.json (new, tracked), src/ksmech/serve/workbench/symmetry.py (the detector moves to the engine), tests/test_spin_theory.py (new)
   - Tests: Cu(II) d9 gives S = 1/2 in any geometry; a Td d5 FeX4- gives S = 5/2 by 'Td high spin'; a d5 Oh species with a mid-series ligand gives 'undetermined'; [O][O] gives S = 1 by Hund's rule (3Sigma_g-); Cl[C]Cl gives the singlet or 'undetermined', never the triplet; every entry carries its rule chain and citation; no SMILES or SMARTS literal names a species in spin_theory.py (grep test); the serve symmetry tests pass unchanged after the move.
4. [M] (C) The combination rule (the user's choice, 2026-10-30), applied to every species of steps 2 and 3:
   - A and B agree (the same lowest state): the multiplicity is assigned.
   - B is 'undetermined': A decides within its error bar, so a conclusive A is assigned and an inconclusive A is MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS. For a metal with no published error bar, A is never conclusive.
   - Every disagreement is MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS, including a lowest state of A that differs from B's inside A's error bar, and each one is reported per species in decisions.md.
   - The metal_v1 rows (P4) are confirmed the same way: the Fe(III) high-spin row stays only if A and B confirm it; Cu(II) d9 is S = 1/2 by count, and its scan is the control.

   The result is spin_table_v1, a tracked, versioned species table: canonical SMILES -> multiplicity or MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS, every state's energy, A's verdict, B's verdict with its rule chain, the case of C that decided, method, solvent, geometry, content hash and spin_theory_v1's digest. data.spin reads it (P3 creates data.spin, P4 completes it). The hand-written carbene and CO clauses become tests of the table. Any later change is spin_table_v2 and a rebuild.
   - Files: scripts/probes/spin_scan.py, src/ksmech/data/spin_tables/species_v1.json (new, tracked; committed between builds), tests/test_spin_table.py (new), docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: The combination is a pure function of A and B, unit-tested on its four cases; the table validates against its schema, its content hash and spin_theory_v1's digest; Cl[C]Cl, the vinylidenes and [C]=O resolve to singlets or are listed MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS, [O][O] resolves to the triplet; every entry records every state's energy.
5. [S] A dozen cited gas-phase IP/EA values for P2's small radicals (CH3, OH, HO2, NO, NO2, NO3, CH3O., Br., Cl., O2 -> superoxide, allyl, phenoxyl), typed from the literature with their citations (NIST Chemistry WebBook ion energetics; Rienstra-Kiracofe et al., Chem. Rev. 102, 231 (2002) for electron affinities); no dataset download. They are compared only with gas-phase delta-SCF levels (restricted GFN2 and spGFN2, vertical E(N-1) - E(N) and E(N) - E(N+1) at the radical's geometry), never with ALPB-water eigenvalues (U22). RSE43 and St. John stay deferred to D2.
   - Files: scripts/probes/open_shell_probe.py, docs/decisions.md (the values with their citations)
   - Tests: Each literature value carries its citation; the delta-SCF runs are gas-phase.
6. [S] An RDKit census of the 4,284 unique FlowER RS/RC species: the distribution of u; how many are atoms or linear radicals whose open shell is symmetry-degenerate (fermi_smeared under today's rule); how many steps contain them; the CO, N2, ozone and other u >= 2 drawings. It fixes the species list of the scan.
   - Files: scripts/probes/flower_species_census.py (new), data/raw/flower/flower_new_dataset/{train,test}.txt (read)
   - Tests: The census reproduces the fact-check counts: about 56 u >= 2 species, CO in 33 steps (RC 5, RS 28), [N]=[N] in 5 RC steps.
7. [S] Write the numbers into decisions.md with a date as evidence for decisions the user has already taken (U7 restricted with the spin rule, U8 the spin rule, U9 Cu and Fe together, U10 fermi_smeared kept and sliced, U16 degenerate shells in P2b). The Cu/Fe numbers feed P4's metal cohort and its 3-seed with/without ablation, not a gate. spin_theory_v1 and spin_table_v1 are committed with no build running.
   - Files: docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: None (documentation).

**Data effect.** None on any dataset artifact. Probe and scan records live only in data/cache/probe_open_shell/, which no pipeline command reads. spin_theory_v1 (src/ksmech/data/spin_tables/theory_v1.json) and spin_table_v1 (src/ksmech/data/spin_tables/species_v1.json) are new tracked files that nothing reads until P3; data.spin reads only spin_table_v1.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** None. Compute (estimate): about 65 scan species at every allowed multiplicity (two for most, more for u >= 3), one spGFN2 optimization each; the 61 Cu/Fe species restricted and spGFN2 at production geometry, the Fe(III) ones also optimized at three multiplicities; the METAL_SUSPECT rows at two; the theory rules (seconds); and about 12 radicals x 3 charge states in the gas phase: about 1 h at 8 workers. It runs after P1 is closed, never beside a build, within the host's 15 heavy processes.

**Exit criteria.** spin_theory_v1 and spin_table_v1 are committed, with every species resolved by rule C or MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS, every A-B disagreement listed per species in decisions.md, the metal_v1 rows confirmed or not by C, and the transition-metal error bar stated with its citation (or its absence recorded); the Cu/Fe and METAL_SUSPECT diagnostic tables (occupations, max|n - aufbau| distribution, block membership, |dq| overall and on arrow-touched atoms, spGFN2's lowest multiplicity against the table) and the IP/EA table are in decisions.md; no threshold decides the metals. The known [CH3] values and ethane dE = 0 reproduce. Already measured (scratch, ALPB water, idealized geometries): restricted Br. and Cl. come out 3 x 1.667, .OH 2 x 1.5 and NO 2 x 0.5 (max |n - aufbau| 0.667 / 0.667 / 0.5 / 0.5), all four flagged fermi_smeared today; triplet O2 (2 x 1.0) and [CH3] (1.0) are integral; restricted versus spGFN2 charges differ by 0.0008 e for CH3 and 0.002 e for OH (re-measured), at most 0.027 e over the heteroatom radicals of the earlier probe; FeCl3 gives 0.116 e per Cl, and restricted puts its doublet 1.93 eV below the sextet. pytest green, summary line pasted.

**Risks.** Idealized geometries for the organic probe, and ALPB versus gas (the IP/EA check is gas-phase on purpose). GFN2 spin-state energetics for 3d metals have limited reliability (Moradi 2024), so the Cu(II)/Fe(III) table and the P4 ablation decide, and spGFN2 is only a diagnostic. The 0.30 eV bound is the method's AC12 error, so a species near it is MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS rather than guessed. The theory rules decide nothing alone: they reach data.spin only through rule C, so a wrong rule makes a species ambiguous, not wrong. Their geometry classes come from production geometries, raw ETKDG for the Cu species and FeCl3, and a class no rule reads gives 'undetermined'. Without a published transition-metal error bar, a metal passes only when A and B agree. The scratch cache root must never be passed to a pipeline command. Do not run the probe from a checkout where a build is writing artifacts, and commit spin_table_v1 between builds (tracked edits mark artifacts dirty).

### P2b. Open-shell token rule for u > 0: localization blocks from GFN2's equal-occupation plateaus, the radical frontier and the FD f+/f- input variant (Release A)

**Depends on:** P0, P0a, P0b, P2

**Goal.** Give open-shell molecules tokens that keep the density-invariance law (rotate only orbitals of equal occupation), with degenerate groups read from GFN2's own occupations instead of an energy window, and frontier inputs that mean the same thing for radicals, before any open-shell step is admitted. Closed-shell token files, every QM record and every v18 sample store stay as they are; closed_base (pinned in P0b) does not move.

**Steps.**

1. [M] Localization, for rec.unpaired > 0. Reference occupations: walk the MOs in energy order and group neighbours whose GFN2 occupations are equal (to 1e-6 e, a numerical equality, not a chemistry threshold) into plateaus, then average the aufbau occupations over each plateau: Br. 3 x 5/3, .OH 2 x 3/2, NO 2 x 1/2, superoxide pi* 2 x 3/2, C6H6.- 2 x 1/2, linear CuCl2 2 x 3/2, triplet O2 2 x 1. There is no energy window: GFN2's Fermi smearing gives exactly degenerate levels equal occupations, so a plateau is a degenerate group the SCF itself formed, and an accidental near-degeneracy keeps distinct occupations and shows up as off-reference. Measured read-only on the 94 u > 0 records on disk: exact degeneracies agree to 4.4e-14 e, and no two adjacent levels with different aufbau occupation come closer than 1e-3 e, so the reference is the same for any tolerance between 1e-13 and 1e-3 e. (1) Occupied vs virtual: an MO with reference occupation > 0 is occupied. Today's raw test occupations >= 0.5 (localize.py:427-428, qm.fukui.OCC_THRESHOLD) sends NO's 0.5000000000108 / 0.4999999999892 pair to different blocks. (2) Within the occupied block the sub-blocks are the reference values (2, each open-group value, 1). Rotations happen only among equal reference occupations, so the density changes only by the Fermi tails (the 2026-10-20 rule). Today DOUBLE_THRESHOLD = 1.5 (localize.py:40, 414) puts superoxide's 1.5/1.5 pi* in the 'double' sub-block with its 2.0 pi pair, and two tokens come out at occupation 1.750 and -11.32 eV, mixing pi (-13.27) with pi* (-9.36). (3) An MO further than SMEAR_TOL (0.05, unchanged) from its reference stays unrotated in its own block, flagged and masked as today. Closed shells keep today's path and thresholds. Measured read-only, the same rule on the 14,172 PMechDB and 19,510 ReactMech closed-shell records changes the reference of 0 and 1 (O=[C-]Cl) records, and changes blocks only for MOs already flagged smeared (7 and 24 records). So the u > 0 gate only keeps those flagged closed-shell files fixed for the closed_base gate. The rule is written for every molecule; its extension to closed shells (U16 keeps it to u > 0) is recorded in decisions.md as unscheduled, since P0a's schema-10 rebuild is the plan's only full token rebuild and P8 rebuilds none.
   - Files: src/ksmech/orbitals/localize.py:40-41, 125-142, 405-431, src/ksmech/qm/fukui.py:25 (read; OCC_THRESHOLD stays for closed shells and homo_index), tests/test_orbitals_localize.py:61-76, tests/fixtures/open_shell.py (new)
   - Tests: Closed-shell token payloads of the 10 fixtures and of 500 PMechDB molecules are identical (content hash, not header bytes). New open-shell fixtures, restricted tblite single points at fixed idealized geometries as in P2's probe: [CH3], .OH, NO, triplet O2, superoxide, Br., Cl., C6H6.- and linear CuCl2 at u = 1. NO, C6H6.- and O2: the open MOs land in the occupied block as one sub-block. Superoxide: no token mixes pi with pi* (each token's spectral set lies on one side of the pi/pi* gap). CuCl2 1.5/1.5 forms one sub-block. Br., Cl., .OH, NO, superoxide and CuCl2 are no longer fermi_smeared, and every closed-shell molecule flagged today stays flagged. A tolerance sweep from 1e-12 to 1e-4 e gives the same reference on every fixture. The [CH2] triplet test is updated.
2. [M] Radical frontier convention (U16), for u > 0 only, in orbitals/frontier.py: a u > 0 variant of frontier_blocks and frozen_orbital_fukui_blocks, which tokens read at tokens.py:444-445. HOMO and LUMO are the electron-removal and electron-addition levels, so for a radical both are the open group of step 1 (every MO with 0 < reference < 2), taken from the occupations and not from DEGENERACY_EV. HOMO-1 is the highest level with reference 2 and LUMO+1 the lowest with reference 0, each summed over frontier.py's existing block window as for a closed shell, so a column keeps one meaning across molecules. Frozen-orbital f+ = f- = the open group's population per MO. Today [CH3] f+ is a sigma*(C-H): C 0.436, each H 0.188. qm/fukui.homo_index and the record fields fukui_fmo_plus/minus and hardness stay as they are, so no QM record changes meaning under its key. The FD dual (rec.fukui_dual) does not change: about +0.006 on the [CH3] C, not 0. A radical's philicity enters through the gaps to its open level, which P7's suite checks. The closed-shell window DEGENERACY_EV = 0.05 eV (frontier.py:19, tex:386) is not changed here, because closed-shell payloads must stay identical: P0a already stores the exact-degeneracy columns beside it (resolution 6; the window merges levels split by more than 1e-4 eV in 3,140 of 14,233 PMechDB records, 22%), and P9 ablates the flag without a rebuild.

The finite-difference f+/f- from the N+-1 single points (rec.fukui_plus/minus) are already in every record and token file as diagnostics (tokens.py:127-128); for u = 1 the N+-1 states are closed shells. They separate the addition site from the removal site, which the frozen convention of step 2 makes identical for radicals. ModelConfig gains atom_fukui = 'frozen' | 'fd' | 'both'. The default 'frozen' is today's input, so every checkpoint and the floor reproduce. Atom inputs are selected by name. The variant applies to every molecule, not only radicals, so a column means one thing everywhere. A NaN (FUKUI_SCF_FAIL) becomes 0, as other missing atom values do. The v18 store carries the two FD columns as stored, unselected atom inputs since P0a (resolution 5), so the switch needs no rebuild. P9 runs the ablation with 3 seeds, and 'frozen' stays the default until then, recorded in decisions.md.

(c) max|n - aufbau| and max|n - reference| as distributions, not only the fermi_smeared flag. Today on PMechDB u > 0, against aufbau: median 0, p90 0.090, max 0.5; against the reference: p90 0.049, max 0.305. ReactMech pilot u > 0: median 0.090, p90 0.257, max 0.393, unchanged by the rule. (P0a's build summary already holds the closed-shell frontier-window counts.) **Risks.** A degenerate open group localizes to an arbitrary point on its plateau. The density is invariant, but the per-token centre descriptors of NO, OH and superoxide are not unique. Bent or T-shaped L-CuX2 adducts may have hole levels split by a few kT. They keep distinct occupations, stay off-reference and are flagged and masked (reported per cohort in P4). That is the honest outcome, not a failure of the rule. A reader that bypasses the helper would load a stale u > 0 file unflagged, and the grep test guards this. The closed-shell 0.05 eV frontier window stays the default until P9's ablation of P0a's exact-degeneracy columns.

### P3. Genuine organic radicals in polar PMechDB steps, admitted by counted laws with the hand reading as test oracle

**Depends on:** P1, P2, P2b

**Goal.** Return the open-shell species that PMechDB draws in polar steps when they obey a polar step's electron bookkeeping. The decision comes from counted laws, not a hand list, and the hand reading only checks the result. No QM, label or model change; the SOMO stays neither donor nor acceptor until P7.

**Steps.**

1. [S] ksmech/data/spin.py, first part. It is RDKit only; P4 adds the metal table and the QM routing. It is created here because P3's spin-agreement law must already see P2's table. species_u(mol) is RDKit's radical count for a species with u <= 1, since a doublet has only one multiplicity. Every species with RDKit u >= 2 (carbenes, CO drawn [C]=O, O2, ozone, diradicals: the cases where the pairing is ambiguous) takes its value from spin_table_v1. That is P2's versioned, QM-derived species table, tracked as src/ksmech/data/spin_tables/species_v1.json with its scan provenance. A u >= 2 species missing from the table is MULTIPLICITY_UNTABULATED. One whose entry is ambiguous (gap under P2's bound) is MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS. Both are counted codes, and the remedy is a new table version, not a rule. After P1, PMechDB's RADICAL_DRAWN steps hold 8 such species, all with ok records: the 6 carbene/CO species, [O][O] and CC[B-](CC)(CC)[O+][O]. SPIN_RULE_VERSION and the table digest are stamped into the provenance of every artifact that reads the module.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/spin.py (new), src/ksmech/data/spin_tables/species_v1.json (new; P2's scan output), tests/test_data_spin.py (new)
   - Tests: [CH3] -> 1. [O][O] -> the table's 2. Cl[C]Cl, Br[C]Br, the three vinylidenes and [C]=O -> the table's value. The textbook singlet clauses become the oracle: each must read 0 in spin_table_v1, and a disagreement fails the test and is resolved in the table, not in code. An untabulated u = 2 species -> MULTIPLICITY_UNTABULATED. The ten fixtures -> 0.
2. [M] Admission by counted laws. screen_step takes a per-source DomainPolicy(open_shell = exclude | admit_by_rule). PMechDB gets admit_by_rule, and every other polar source keeps exclude until P8. A step whose drawing has unpaired electrons is admitted when three laws hold, each failure counted under its own reason. (u) The total unpaired count under data.spin is conserved across the step, and every species' data.spin count equals RDKit's. Until P4 computes tagged records, a species the table re-assigns (a singlet carbene) has only its old RDKit-spin record, so its steps wait as SPIN_RULE_PENDING. (R) Every R atom (diagonal) entry is even, so every atom's non-bonding count keeps its parity. It is read from the dataset arrows: a polar step neither creates, destroys nor moves an unpaired electron. Failures count as NOT_ELEMENTARY. (S) Every open-shell reactant species has an integral SOMO in its restricted GFN2 record: max|n - reference| <= SMEAR_TOL under P2b's plateau reference. The 12 hand-read pools deviate by at most 0.018 (Birch), so their outcome holds for any tolerance above 0.02. (u) and (R) are decided at ingest (RDKit and arrow replay). (S) needs the QM record, so the sample builder checks it and skips a failure as SOMO_NOT_INTEGRAL, counted. Measured read-only on the 52 RADICAL_DRAWN steps of today's ingest: all 12 hand-read genuine steps pass all three. Of the 30 left after P1, the laws alone also pass the 3 'isopropoxides' (9532a9a3, 20a9346c, 19a76e67), 2 unrepaired drawing errors (29a4a038, a dialkylaluminium missing its H; ef20cdaa, a nitrone drawn [OH+] for [O-]) and 2 carbene-spectator steps (0923213b, a045da08, Cl[C]Cl on both sides), which (u)'s spin agreement holds back. They fail e57aaee9 (u 1 -> 0) and the 10 other carbene/CO steps. Curation closes the difference from the hand reading; the laws are not narrowed. A tracked, counted exclusion file (src/ksmech/data/curation/pmechdb_open_shell.json: step id, reason, decision date; no SMILES) lists the 3 isopropoxides as RADICAL_AMBIGUOUS (possibly sodium ketyls, U4/U6) and the 2 drawing errors as DRAWING_ERROR_UNREPAIRED. Excluded steps keep the code RADICAL_DRAWN with that domain_class. An admitted step's domain_class is RADICAL_SPECTATOR, or RADICAL_REACTIVE when an R entry touches a radical atom: 11 / 1 at atom level. About 5 are spectators in the orbital sense, reported alongside.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/domain.py:47-59, 162-200, src/ksmech/data/validation.py:167-200, src/ksmech/data/curation/pmechdb_open_shell.json (new), src/ksmech/arrows/delta_be.py (is_elementary, read), src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py (SOMO_NOT_INTEGRAL skip, counted in samples_report), tests/fixtures/pmechdb_radical_survey.json (new: the hand reading, test oracle only), tests/test_data_domain.py
   - Tests: Unit: a doublet spectator drawn the same on both sides is admitted. A step that creates or destroys a radical fails (u). An odd R_ii fails (R). Cl[C]Cl as a spectator waits as SPIN_RULE_PENDING. A smeared SOMO is skipped at build. realdata oracle on the post-P1 ingest: the admitted set equals the hand reading's 12 genuine steps exactly. Every other RADICAL_DRAWN step carries a counted reason (a failed law, spin pending, or a curated exclusion). Every curated id passes the laws, so the curation file holds nothing the laws already exclude.
3. [S] Two counted per-step records, neither a model input. (a) A Lewis bond that R changes but the QM geometry does not hold. The QM connectivity check skips Li, Na, Mg and K (qm/geometry.py:26). The sample builder flags SKIPPED_BOND_STRETCHED when an R entry sits on such a bond and the reactant record puts its two atoms further apart than conn_cov_factor (1.3, QMConfig) x the covalent-radius sum. The constants already exist, so the Birch tag is a general check, not an id. 4bda5440 must be flagged: its record puts Na 3.25-3.38 A from every ring C (a Na...benzene partial charge-transfer complex, SOMO 58% on Na), so R breaks a bond the input does not have. Other flagged steps (ionic alkali-metal bonds drawn as Lewis bonds) are counted and reported as a slice. (b) 5d007b38 (Et3B + O2) holds u = 2 species: triplet [O][O] among the reactants and the adduct CC[B-](CC)(CC)[O+][O] among the products. The single MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES policy for all sources lands in P8. Until then P3 writes the counted exemption that the policy will read, in src/ksmech/data/curation/multi_radical_exemptions.json (source, step id, species, reason 'U6, 2026-10-28'). The ingest report counts admitted steps that hold a u >= 2 species (1). A test asserts that every such step is in the file, which is the invariant P8 enforces. If spin_table_v1 does not give the adduct u = 2, law (u) holds 5d007b38 back on its own, and the oracle reports that one difference to the user.
   - Files: src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py, src/ksmech/qm/geometry.py:26 (read), src/ksmech/data/curation/multi_radical_exemptions.json (new), src/ksmech/data/stats.py, tests/test_arrows_steps.py
   - Tests: realdata: 4bda5440 is flagged SKIPPED_BOND_STRETCHED and the slice count is reported. Every admitted step with a u >= 2 species is in the exemption file (today: 5d007b38 only).
4. [S] The 2 genuine-radical resonance steps (db877060, dcf0a54a) enter the domain and become resonance pairs (io keeps resonance steps whose domain_exclusion is None). eval gets the open_shell cohort, split into spectator and reactive, through P0's --cohort option, with the fermi_smeared slice (U10) per cohort.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/io.py:41-57, src/ksmech/eval/cli.py
   - Tests: resonance_pairs.parquet gains exactly db877060 and dcf0a54a.
5. [S] Rebuild. Spec v9.2 Scope and Domain coverage text (tex:349-350, 806): open-shell species in polar steps are in the domain when they obey the three laws, curated exclusions are listed and counted, and their SOMO is neither donor nor acceptor until P7. Then re-ingest PMechDB, run `ksmech-arrows samples --source pmechdb` (v18; new samples placed by P0's frozen-split rules) and the pair stats, then `ksmech-physics gbdt` (the refit is recorded as the new GBDT reference) and `ksmech-physics textbook`. Dated decisions.md entry: the three laws, the measured rule-vs-oracle table, the curation and exemption files, and the SKIPPED_BOND_STRETCHED check.
   - Files: docs/decisions.md, docs/ksmech_architecture.tex:349-350, 806, docs/data_schema.md (domain_class values, the two curation files)
   - Tests: realdata: the closed_base per-column digests are unchanged after the rebuild.

**Data effect.** PMechDB gains 10 training samples and 2 resonance pairs: the Birch radical anion (1), peroxyl deprotonations (3), peroxyl -> superoxide eliminations (4), the superoxide protonation (1), Et3B + O2 (1, the only one reactive at atom level), plus the resonance steps db877060 and dcf0a54a. All 27 species of the 12 steps have ok records and token files. After P2b none of the 12 reactant pools is smeared; superoxide's used to be. They cover about 5 distinct chemistries and are reported as counts (cohort under 30). RADICAL_DRAWN goes 30 -> 18: 12 carbene/CO steps wait for P4 (SPIN_RULE_PENDING), 3 are RADICAL_AMBIGUOUS and 2 DRAWING_ERROR_UNREPAIRED (curated), and 1 fails (u) (e57aaee9). No closed_base sample changes.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** Re-ingest, samples (SAMPLE_VERSION stays 18), pair stats, the GBDT refit and textbook. No QM, token or schema bump. About 30 min of CPU: the last full sample build took 590 s, and the GBDT spends about 100 s loading plus about 220 s fitting per split.

**Exit criteria.** The admitted set equals the hand reading's 12 (10 samples + 2 resonance pairs), and every other RADICAL_DRAWN step has a counted reason. No admitted step has an odd R_ii, and no admitted reactant pool is smeared. 4bda5440 is flagged SKIPPED_BOND_STRETCHED, and the exemption file holds 5d007b38. The closed_base per-column digests, the floor and flex_first decode are identical, and the GBDT refit is recorded. pytest green: `uv run pytest -n 14 -m "not realdata"` and `uv run pytest -m realdata`, both summary lines pasted.

**Risks.** Restricted doublets are adequate inputs. Restricted and spGFN2 charges agree to 0.0008 e for CH3 and 0.002 e for OH (re-measured), and to 0.027 e over the earlier heteroatom set (not re-measured). The three laws admit any radical drawn consistently, so a new polar source needs its own curation review before it gets admit_by_rule; the oracle test guards PMechDB only. Whether 5d007b38 stays depends on spin_table_v1's adduct entry (U6 assumed u = 2). Key v1 cannot tell [O][O] from O=O; the spin-agreement part of (u) keeps out any step where such a species changes spin. Until P6 the eval octet count (2 x lone pairs) undercounts a radical atom by one electron, which makes it permissive, not wrong-way. RDKit's ResonanceMolSupplier does not move radicals, so resonance draws do nothing at the radical centres. 10 steps support no claim on their own.

### P4. Spin from RDKit plus two versioned tables (spin_table_v1, the Cu(II)/Fe(III) table): singlet carbenes, CO and the metal cohort under a per-molecule tag, then the Release A retraining

**Depends on:** P1, P2, P2b, P3

**Goal.** Compute the species whose multiplicity RDKit gets wrong (carbenes, CO) or cannot see (odd metals) from one module that holds no chemist's clause in code. Every existing record key stays as it is, and serve behaves as before until S1. Cu and Fe come in together as a cohort whose place in training a 3-seed ablation decides. Then retrain Release A with the GENERAL configuration and compare it with P0b.

**Steps.**

1. [M] Complete ksmech/data/spin.py, RDKit only. It is the single source of spin for QM, domain codes, key v2 (P6), the masks and atom inputs (P7, P9) and MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES (P8). It lives in ksmech.data because data must decide domain codes and keys without running QM; qm imports it. assign(mol) returns per-atom u and its source, one of three. (1) 'rdkit': RDKit's count for species with u <= 1 and no odd metal fragment. (2) 'species_v1': spin_table_v1, P2's rule C over the spGFN2 scan at every allowed multiplicity and the theory rules of spin_theory_v1, for every species with RDKit u >= 2 (P3). The hand clauses of the old plan (dihalocarbene and vinylidene singlets, CO [C]=O, isocyanide C, N2 drawn [N]=[N]) are no longer code. They are tests that the table must pass, and N2's bond-order repair is P8's. (3) 'metal_v1': metal_spin_table_v1, a named, versioned table (src/ksmech/data/spin_tables/metal_v1.json) for odd-electron metal fragments. Each row is element, oxidation state and ligand-field class -> u, with its literature basis. OS = q_M + bond orders to non-metals + H on M. Rows: Cu(II) -> u = 1 (d9, S = 1/2 in any geometry); Fe(III) without a strong-field ligand (CN-, CO, polypyridyl, porphyrin) -> u = 5, high spin S = 5/2 (FeX4-, FeX3, FeCl3.L, Fe(OTf)2-OR). Each row holds only where P2's rule C confirms it: the Fe(III) high-spin row stays for a species only if the scan (A) and the ligand-field chain (B) both give S = 5/2; Cu(II) d9 is S = 1/2 by count, its scan the control. A species of a row that C does not confirm is MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS, counted, and a row no species confirms is removed; if the Fe(III) row goes, whether Cu enters without Fe (U9 joins them) goes back to the user. Cu(II) with a hydride or iodide ligand has an explicit REDOX_INCOMPATIBLE row with no u: Cu(II) oxidizes I- and H-, and CuI2 disproportionates to CuI + 1/2 I2. So the 6 CuH2 adducts (RDKit gives bare [CuH2] u = 1) and the 5 CuI2 species are rejected, and no hydride or iodide record is computed. Any odd metal fragment without a row is MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS. One code for 'no multiplicity can be assigned' replaces OPEN_SHELL_METAL as an exclusion: on PMechDB at this phase's re-ingest, on ReactMech (2 pilot / 44 full odd-Pd steps) at its next re-ingest (P8); the enum keeps the old code so old artifacts still read. The Cu=C drawing (26e17638) stays METAL_PLACEHOLDER, as today. SPIN_RULE_VERSION and both table digests are stamped into the provenance of every artifact that reads the module.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/spin.py, src/ksmech/data/spin_tables/metal_v1.json (new), tests/test_data_spin.py
   - Tests: Table oracles: Cl[C]Cl, Br[C]Br, the vinylidenes and [C]=O read 0 from spin_table_v1, [CH2] and [O][O] read 2. CuCl2 and C[NH+](C)[Cu-](Cl)Cl -> 1. That is 78 OPEN_SHELL_METAL minus 6 resonance, 6 CuH2 and 4 CuI2 steps. Cu is the acceptor in 21 of the 23 Cu steps. The Fe atom is untouched in 37 of 43 Fe steps, but the arrows run through an Fe-bound atom in 29 of the 39 non-resonance ones. These counts assume spin_table_v1 makes all 6 carbene/CO species clear singlets and that P2's rule C confirms the Fe(III) row for the Fe species; an ambiguous entry keeps its steps out, counted. QM: 55 species under '+spin-v1'. SPIN_PARITY goes 100 -> 51. Release A total on PMechDB (P1 + P3 + P4): +95 training samples (13 + 10 + 10 + 62) and +11 resonance pairs (1 + 2 + 2 + 6), minus counted failures and minus the metals if the ablation removes them. After Release A, RADICAL_DRAWN is 6 (5 curated, 1 failing (u)), MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS 10 (6 CuH2, 4 CuI2) plus any ambiguous table entry, OPEN_SHELL_METAL 0 on PMechDB, CHARGE_UNREPAIRED 1, and METAL_PLACEHOLDER as P0a and P1 left it. No closed_base sample changes.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** QMRecord SCHEMA_VERSION 1 and SAMPLE_VERSION 18 are unchanged. New records exist only under '+spin-v1', and no existing key changes (QMugs, 148,876 closed-shell records, is untouched). The old untagged triplet records of the 6 carbene/CO species stay in the cache, unused by the engine. The QM cache INDEX_VERSION 1 -> 2 is a cheap header rebuild. Then re-ingest, samples (v18), pair stats, the GBDT refit and textbook. QM: 55 GFN2 species plus their N+-1 points, about an hour (estimate). Training: 6 GPU runs (3 Release A + 3 metal ablation), about 45 min each on a pod (dense_plain measured 45.1 min) and longer on the RX 6600 XT (estimate).

**Exit criteria.** Every existing record key is unchanged. Every assigned record converges or is counted by reason, and every tagged record verifies with its sidecar found. The closed_base per-column digests, the floor and old-checkpoint decode are identical, and the GBDT refit is recorded. Release A's closed_base val top-1 (GENERAL, 3 seeds) is not below P0b's GENERAL mean by more than max(2 x pooled SD of the 6 runs, 1 point), with the paired McNemar test reported beside it, or the gap is explained in decisions.md. The metal retention decision is taken by the 3-seed with/without ablation and recorded. Serve's tests are green and its keys and refusal codes are unchanged. pytest green: `uv run pytest -n 14 -m "not realdata"` and `uv run pytest -m realdata`, both summary lines pasted.

**Risks.** GFN2 spin states are unreliable for 3d metals (Moradi 2024). At an idealized geometry restricted FeCl3 sextet charges differ from spGFN2 by 0.349 e on Fe and 0.116 e per Cl, and the doublet sits 1.93 eV below the sextet (harmless with a fixed table). Since U9 is all or nothing, a failing ablation drops Cu with Fe until D2. Cu species and FeCl3 get raw ETKDG (no UFF parameters), while 4-coordinate [Fe-] species get UFF; serve's metal path uses templates (+metal-geom-v1), so engine and serve geometries differ (S1). Near-degenerate Cu(II) adducts may stay smeared and masked. The metal table covers Cu(II) and Fe(III) only, so any other odd metal is MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS. Even open-shell-capable metals (Cr(IV) d2) get RDKit's 0 and are P0a's METAL_SUSPECT. A table revision bumps SPIN_RULE_VERSION and re-keys the tagged species and every artifact that read them. Until S1, serve keeps RDKit spin and refuses Cl[C]Cl and [C]=O (RADICAL_NOT_SUPPORTED), so it never shows the old triplet records.

### P5. ReactMech returns: QM, tokens and samples for the steps V1 (CDI) and the dative law gave back, the 20-case oxidation-state audit, re-scores with and without the changed steps

**Depends on:** P0a, P1, P2b

**Goal.** Compute and sample the ReactMech pilot steps that P1's V1 (CDI) and P0a's dative and placeholder laws returned to the domain, without moving the frozen ReactMech splits or the pilot's 10,000 ids. Check the dative law by oxidation-state arithmetic, and show what every change since P0 did to the two usable ReactMech runs. The 49 even onium Pd/Ni re-charges were applied and computed in P0a, not here.

**Steps.**

1. [L] QM for the returns. The steps are the pilot steps admitted at P0a's and P1's ingest but skipped by the sample builder as MISSING_TOKENS. The fact-check simulation of the rules (V1-CDI by ring topology; dative onium -> Pd with the L1Pd exemption) returns 267 of the 269 pilot exclusions, and P0a's laws reproduce it (205 dative and placeholder returns measured, revise/sime_reactmech.out, plus V1's 62 CDI): 62 CDI (RADICAL_DRAWN), 96 odd-Pd OPEN_SHELL_METAL and 109 L1Pd METAL_PLACEHOLDER. 2 stay excluded (cfd05e0cae5eee42, 5e7499636a4c0e94: Pd already -1 with three X ligands plus P+). Measured read-only by applying those rules to the 269: 327 unique fragments lack an ok record, 292 of them Pd fragments re-charged by the dative law (L1Pd(0) included) and 35 from the CDI steps. They have 88 atoms median with H, p90 115, max 136, and 81 have 100 or more. The count was measured before P0a with the fact-check rules; fragments P0a already computed for the 49 re-charges are cache hits, and P0a's changed-step list re-counts them before P5 starts. `ksmech-qm run --source reactmech --workers 8` (at most 15 heavy processes on the host), then a retry of the largest with `--retry-reason TIMEOUT --timeout 1800` (the method string does not change), then `ksmech-qm bond-index`. The old radical records (CDI doublets) and the drawn SPIN_PARITY tombstones stay in the cache under their old keys, unused; they count in index stats.
   - Files: (CLI runs), data/processed/reactmech/qm_manifest.parquet (rebuilt view)
   - Tests: realdata: every return has an ok record or a counted failure reason, and reactants.py finds every sidecar (no NO_BOND_INDEX).
2. [S] The 20-case oxidation-state audit ran in P0a, before its build (scripts/probes/dative_audit.py). Here it is re-run with the same seed on the same 20 cases as the odd-Pd and L1Pd returns enter the samples: every row must reproduce P0a's (OS(M) from domain.oxidation_state equal to the oxidation state the chemistry implies; pool charge-balanced). One row per case goes into decisions.md. A changed row stops P5 and goes to you, because revising the law now would change training steps after P0b. The Cr(IV) d2 by-product (10 test steps, product side only) stays P0a's METAL_SUSPECT slice.

Then `ksmech-arrows samples --source reactmech` (v18; labels from the mapped sides with P0a's Kekule-closest product) and the pair stats. Returned steps are new samples placed by P0's frozen-split rules: a group holding frozen steps keeps their part, a bridging step is SPLIT_CONFLICT (counted), and the rest are hash-placed. The pilot keeps its 10,000 ids, and the 2 still-excluded steps stay in it as excluded. Steps beyond the per-source decoder caps (P0a) stay in the denominators as misses.

Each is reported with and without the changed slices, and per-sample hits are written for paired comparisons (U20). reactmech/sparse_resonance went NaN at epoch 19 and is not used.

The full ingest of 114,199 unique lines excludes 3,165 (RADICAL_DRAWN 792 + OPEN_SHELL_METAL 1,251 + METAL_PLACEHOLDER 1,122), and about 3,121 return (all 792 CDI, all 1,122 placeholders, 1,207 of the 1,251). 44 stay, and their QM belongs to a full ReactMech build, not this phase. **Data effect.** The ReactMech pilot gains up to 267 of its 10,000 steps as samples (62 CDI, 96 odd-Pd, 109 L1Pd), minus counted QM or label failures. 2 stay OPEN_SHELL_METAL until ReactMech's next re-ingest under data.spin (P8), where they become MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS. Pilot SPIN_PARITY rows go from 291 to the fragments of those 2 steps. On the full set about 3,121 of 3,165 are returnable, computed only when a full ReactMech build runs. PMechDB is untouched.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** Rebuilt: the ReactMech QM manifest view, the Wiberg sidecars, the tokens (new species only), the samples and the pair stats. SAMPLE_VERSION stays 18 and nothing else is bumped. QM: 327 unique fragments, about 12-19 CPU-h (estimate: 327 x the measured 137-213 s mean of ok 70-136-atom records; the sheet's 10-17 CPU-h was for the 291 drawn SPIN_PARITY fragments), about 1-2 h wall at 8-15 workers. CPU re-scoring of two checkpoints.

**Exit criteria.** Per-rule counts are reported for the pilot and the full set. Every return is a sample or has a counted reason. The pilot keeps its 10,000 ids, and every frozen view keeps the parts and per-column digests of its pre-P5 samples. The re-run of P0a's 20-case audit reproduces its rows, or P5 stopped. On slice (a) both runs reproduce their P0 numbers exactly. On the whole val they reproduce P0a's re-score of the pre-P5 samples, since P5 only adds samples. Slices (b)-(d) are reported with and without. pytest green: `uv run pytest -n 14 -m "not realdata"` and `uv run pytest -m realdata`, both summary lines pasted.

**Risks.** The largest Pd fragments (136 atoms) may hit TIMEOUT; a retry with a larger --timeout keeps the method string. ReactMech has no arrows, so the dative law gets no arrow replay, and the oxidation-state audit is its only check. P0a's placeholder law keeps the L1Pd exemption ([Pd-][P+]R3, a Pd whose only ligand is a dative onium donor, is monoligated Pd(0), not a placeholder; resolution 1); without it the 109 pilot / 1,122 full placeholder steps would not return, and P0a's changed-step list confirms them before P5 starts. 29 of the 49 P0a re-charges sit in the ReactMech val, so old-run numbers on the whole val move with P0a, not P5. Returned steps placed in val/test are new to the old checkpoints, which never trained on them, so slice (d) measures generalization to the returned chemistry, not regression.

### P6. Electron bookkeeping, one-electron labels, the electron-flow codes and the spin-aware identity key v2 (code; Release B, stacked branch 1 of 3)

**Depends on:** P0b, P4, P5

**Goal.** Make the data layer, labels and Lewis checks carry single electrons; tell resonance, identity lines, electron transfers and spin changes apart by one bookkeeping law instead of by the sigma key alone; and keep every closed-shell label, key and resonance flag of the v18 data (P0a) exactly as it is.

**Steps.**

1. [M] Store N (non-bonding electrons, B_ii proper) and u (unpaired) per atom in the Lewis structure, the sample, the drawings and the store, with lone_pairs = N//2 derived. u comes from N, not from RDKit: data.spin gains spin_of_drawing(fragment, N), where u = the number of odd-N atoms, raised only where spin_table_v1 (P2) holds the species as high spin (see the key step). RDKit's radical count is used only for bare SMILES (serve inputs, later sources), as U12 requires. reactant_lewis returns N and u beside the bonds, lone pairs and Wiberg indices. All live callers are updated here: steps.py:351, eval/embedding.py:290, serve/embedding/service.py:315, serve/predict/network_predictor.py:322 and eval/embedding_page.py:215 (lines on main f6aa758; found by function name after the P0/P0a merges). Otherwise a live sample would default to can_lose_electron False everywhere and silently mask every -1/-2 entry in the embedding page, the serve embedding job and the network predictor. check_edit tests N + d >= 0. Batches carry can_lose_electron (N >= 1) and can_lose_pair (N >= 2), and collate asserts that the N array has the atom count, so a live sample without it fails loudly. drawn(k) sets N and u per drawing. eval lewis_apply tracks N (octet = N + 2*sum(order)) and replaces the P0 raise.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/spin.py (spin_of_drawing), src/ksmech/arrows/lewis.py:67-81, 119-140, src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py:123, 144-147, 168-183, 351, src/ksmech/arrows/drawings.py:63, 155-166, src/ksmech/arrows/store.py:43, 131, src/ksmech/arrows/batches.py:152-180, src/ksmech/eval/decode.py:47-60, 83, 97, src/ksmech/eval/embedding.py:290, src/ksmech/eval/embedding_page.py:215, src/ksmech/serve/embedding/service.py:315, src/ksmech/serve/predict/network_predictor.py:322
   - Tests: Closed-shell samples have nonbonding = 2*lone_pairs and unpaired = 0. Recombination at a carbon radical (N=1, d=-1) passes check_edit. A live sample built without N fails collate. [Br+], [OH+] and a singlet [CH2] produced by a +2 lone-pair R have u = 0 (even N), where RDKit's count would give u > 0.
2. [M] One bookkeeping law for identity, electron transfer and resonance (new; it replaces 'the product is among RDKit's enumerated forms'). On the mapped sides, build the changed-bond graph (atoms joined by bonds whose order changes). The flow is BALANCED when every atom whose N changes lies on a changed bond and every connected component has sum dN + 2 sum dB = 0, i.e. electrons move only between bonded atoms (the closure of arrow moves on the drawn skeleton, with no enumeration cap). Four counted codes follow, each in EditSkip/SkipReason or the ingest reasons, PRECEDENCE and stats: NULL_STEP (R = 0, identical sides; decided at ingest before is_resonance_step, which today sends the identity line to resonance_pairs); NONCONJUGATED_SHIFT (one sigma key with maps kept, but the flow is unbalanced for every reactant drawing x product form: an electron jump across saturated atoms, not resonance; ingest); SET_STEP (R has atom entries only; labels); DISSOCIATIVE_ET_STEP (R has bond entries but the flow is unbalanced in every variant of the label class: an electron transfer coupled to bond cleavage; labels; kept separate from SET_STEP). Measured (revise/probes/conjlaw.py): the law holds on all 471 PMechDB and all 548 ReactMech-pilot resonance pairs as drawn, so no closed-shell flag flips. On the 5,088 FlowER RS/RC lines as drawn: 4,880 elementary lines and 192 same-sigma lines obey it, 1 is NULL, 0 are SET, and 15 break it: 1 same-sigma ozone redraw ([O-][O+]=O >> [O][O+][O-], handled by P8's pairing rule) and 14 with a sigma change (12 NO3. hydrogen abstractions whose H lands on the drawn O-, 1 persulfate O-O homolysis with the charge drawn on the far O, 1 DDQ step). All 14 are resonance-drawing artifacts that the label class (reactant drawings x product forms, anion proxy) should clear, so DISSOCIATIVE_ET_STEP is expected at 0, confirmed by the dry run. RDKit's ResonanceMolSupplier cannot be the membership test: measured (revise/probes/nonconj.py), from the drawn reactant it reaches the drawn product for only 147 of 471 PMechDB pairs (it does not enumerate from octet-deficient centres such as [CH+]-NH2 or P+-O-) and 512 of 548 ReactMech-pilot pairs (397 of them at the 256 cap).
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/resonance.py (is_resonance_step; new electron_flow), src/ksmech/data/validation.py:167-205 (is_resonance_step call at :175), src/ksmech/data/domain.py (reason codes, PRECEDENCE), src/ksmech/data/stats.py, src/ksmech/arrows/delta_be.py (EditSkip, _label, parsimonious_class), src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py (SkipReason), tests/test_data_resonance.py
   - Tests: Both allyl-cation drawings: BALANCED and resonance. [CH2+]CC[CH2-] >> [CH2-]CC[CH2+]: NONCONJUGATED_SHIFT. D. + A >> D+ + A.-: SET_STEP. R-X + D. >> R. + X- + D+ in one step: DISSOCIATIVE_ET_STEP. Identical sides: NULL_STEP, absent from resonance_pairs. NO3. + Et2O with the H drawn on the O-: LABEL_OK through the NO3 drawing that carries the radical on that O. realdata: the 471 PMechDB and 548 ReactMech resonance pairs stay resonance.
3. [M] A per-source elementary rule that is a label rule only, located by function name (is_elementary, parsimonious_class, _label, drop_undecodable_drawings; the P0/P0a merges moved their lines). At label time the source's DomainPolicy chooses is_elementary(open_shell): sources with an open-shell policy (rmechdb) accept |R_ii| in {1, 2}; PMechDB and ReactMech keep R_ii in {-2, 0, +2}. The source never becomes a sample input, a ModelConfig field or a decoder argument, so nothing at inference knows it (the per-source decoder caps of P0a are eval configuration, not model input). The parsimonious class follows. Anchors do not change. Homolysis takes the existing break sign (724 PMechDB samples already have break anchors, v17 count). 4. [M] Identity key v2 (U12). For a pool with no unpaired electron the key is the v1 key unchanged. Otherwise it is v1 plus the sorted multiset of (fragment sigma SMILES, fragment charge, fragment unpaired count), with u from spin_of_drawing where N is known (samples, eval, decoded products, serve's predicted states) and RDKit's count only for bare SMILES. The suffix uses ',' between fields and ';' between fragments and never '.', because serve's split_pool_key (keys.py:92-94) splits pool keys on '.'; a single-fragment species key stays '.'-free. Resonance needs both one v2 key and a BALANCED flow (previous step); one v2 key with an unbalanced flow is NONCONJUGATED_SHIFT. O2 drawings are normalized through the spin table, not a name list: a spin_table_v1 row whose verdict is a degenerate frontier (the Hund case, P8) holds for every drawing with that v1 key, so O=O and [O][O] key as one triplet species; a row with any other verdict holds only for the drawing it was scanned on, so C=C never inherits a [CH2][CH2] row. Measured (revise/probes/o2_census.py): PMechDB draws O2 only as [O][O], in one step (5d007b38) of 13,090 rows; the ReactMech pilot (10,000 rows) has none; FlowER RS/RC draws it only as [O][O] (1,299 lines). The normalization changes 0 Release B steps; it serves species matching in the leakage audit, serve and later sources. [C]=O and [C-]#[O+] stay one species (N(C) = 2 is paired in the BE drawing, v2 = v1). Eval's _pool_mol and arrows.symmetry.lewis_mol (unsanitized, NoImplicit, UpdatePropertyCache only, so RDKit never assigns radicals today) rebuild the drawing's radical electrons from the tracked N and then call data.spin. Dated docs/decisions.md entries: the flow law replacing RDKit enumeration as the resonance test (with the 147/471 measurement); u from N; the ',' and ';' suffix format; the per-source R_ii rule as a label rule only; NULL_STEP, SET_STEP and DISSOCIATIVE_ET_STEP as separate codes; the serve re-key at the merge.
