**Data effect.** None to the v18 data. Code, a serve migration command that runs only at the P8 merge, and a scratch dry-run report.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** Release B (P6-P8) runs in its own worktree whose data root is seeded with `cp -a --reflink=always` from main's data/ (btrfs copy-on-write, about 0 disk at seeding), not hard links: steps.parquet, resonance_pairs.parquet, the QM manifest, index.sqlite, metrics.jsonl and the write_text reports are written in place and would reach main's files through a hard link. SAMPLE_VERSION 18 -> 19 is set on this stacked branch but built only in P8. Key v2 re-keys every pool with u > 0, admitted or not: the P3/P4 admissions, the steps still excluded in steps.parquet (v17 counts: PMechDB 58 with RDKit radicals, ReactMech pilot 62) and serve's stored states. Every closed-shell key, pool id and product key stays byte-identical. The browse INDEX_VERSION 2 -> 3 (pmindex.py:43) lands in P8 (no index exists on main today). POOL_KEY_VERSION stays 2: the states whose keys change are re-keyed or purged at the merge.

**Exit criteria.** On the v18 data, closed-shell steps get identical PMechDB and ReactMech edit-labels content (compared by content, not header bytes); the P3/P4 open-shell steps are listed as expected diffs, since the anion proxy can grow their label classes. Resonance flags are identical on every closed-shell pool of PMechDB and ReactMech (the flow law keeps all 471 + 548 pairs); flips on open-shell pools are listed and explained (CO does not flip). A hash over all closed-shell sigma keys is unchanged. The 8 radical shapes round-trip. The identity line is NULL_STEP and absent from resonance_pairs. The FlowER dry run gives at least 95% LABEL_OK on non-resonance steps (the existing edit-labels coverage gate); DISSOCIATIVE_ET_STEP and NONCONJUGATED_SHIFT are listed line by line (expected 0 each once the label class applies); every P9 mask and screen violation is counted, and the PMechDB/ReactMech closed-shell labels have none. `ksmech-serve rekey-pools --dry-run` on a reflink copy of data/serve/pools reports its count. pytest green: `uv run pytest -n 14 -m "not realdata"` and `uv run pytest -m realdata`, both summary lines pasted.

**Risks.** RXNMapper maps of radical steps can be wrong: 260 RS/RC lines change 4 or more bond orders (re-measured). The flow law is a necessary condition for an arrow-pushing path; it is the resonance test, while the label class still relies on enumeration (RDKit for closed shells, the anion proxy for radicals), which misses 3-electron N-O forms. The per-v1-key Hund rows assume that a degenerate frontier is a property of the species, which holds for symmetry degeneracy only. Stacked branches drift from main (steps.py, delta_be.py, decode.py also change in Release A and S1), so rebase before P8 builds.

### P7. SOMO donors, hole tokens, the SOMO population input, occupation-weighted G, the own-pair channel and the two-level g2 column (code; Release B, stacked branch 2 of 3)

**Depends on:** P0a, P2b, P6

**Goal.** Let open-shell orbitals act as one-electron donors and, through a hole token, as acceptors; give homolysis its own physics channel; add a bounded two-level gap column on every cell while the legacy g stays bit-identical; then measure the untrained physics on radical selectivity as diagnostics, before any model is trained.

**Steps.**

1. [M] Candidate rule. CandidateConfig gets somo_roles, plus a bookkeeping flag somo (model_input=False) taken from P2b's aufbau group. A main-group open-shell token is a donor, and its hole is an acceptor. Fermi-smeared levels are neither, by the aufbau test. Metal-d open-shell tokens are a flag, metal_d_open_shell, default masked (today's behaviour) until P9's ablation sets the default, recorded in decisions.md. Cells are never deleted (P0a keeps every occupied x virtual cell and turned excluded_pair into a mask channel); the validity channel marks a token with its own hole and every open-row x hole-column cell within one fragment (an excitation inside the open shell). It does not mark 'open-shell pairs within one fragment', which would remove the SOMO->pi* cells of radical cyclization and the sigma->hole cells of beta-scission, and there is no SOMO->partner exclusion: the C6H6.- SOMO -> its Jahn-Teller partner pi* stays a valid cell, bounded by g2. asdict(CandidateConfig) is stamped into steps._config only, not into token_config: a key missing from a file header counts as a change (provenance.py:285-292), so stamping it there would make every token file stale.
   - Files: src/ksmech/orbitals/tokens.py:154-176, 289-302, 394-417, src/ksmech/physics/pairs.py:43-70, src/ksmech/arrows/reactants.py:428-439, src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py:556-562
   - Tests: test_orbitals_descriptors.py:93-102 and test_orbitals_tokens.py:225-235 are rewritten on the open-shell fixtures. Closed-shell candidate sets are identical. A cyclization SOMO->pi* cell within one fragment is valid; the C6H6.- SOMO->partner cell is valid. With metal_d_open_shell off, the Cu(II)/Fe(III) candidate sets of Release A are reproduced. No token file goes stale.
2. [M] Hole tokens, built at sample assembly (U15). Each open-shell token gets a VIRTUAL-kind twin with the same coefficients, centres, descriptors, occupation and spectral set. spin_role is a sample-level orbital column appended in reactants.py and steps.py, not an OrbitalDescriptors field: load_tokens reads every descriptor field by name (tokens.py:733-736), so a new field would break every token file. It is +1 on the open-shell donor copy, -1 on its hole copy and 0 elsewhere: a role flag for a duplicated orbital, the same split as the occupied/virtual block it extends, not an m_s quantum number (degeneracy-averaged tokens have no definite m_s). The two copies have identical token inputs without it; the pair track already tells them apart by orientation, and spin_role makes that explicit at the embedding. About 17 source files use the occupied x virtual block (fact-check count, not 10); batches.py needs no change, while align.py and apply_predicted.py learn the hole so fishhooks can align on SOMO and hole tokens.
   - Files: src/ksmech/arrows/reactants.py:318-440, src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py, src/ksmech/arrows/align.py, src/ksmech/arrows/apply_predicted.py, src/ksmech/model/inputs.py:35-76, 90-108 (fixed scale for rare inputs), src/ksmech/arrows/batches.py:58-73 (checked, unchanged)
   - Tests: [CH3]: 1 donor row plus 1 hole column, and the self-pair is masked. Br.: 3 holes. A homolysis/recombination fishhook pair aligns onto SOMO and hole tokens on the open-shell fixtures. The ethane and acetone grids are unchanged.
3. [M] Per-atom restricted SOMO population, a continuous atom input (U15 as revised: it replaces the gated categorical unpaired count as the model's spin input). rho_A = sum over the open-shell tokens t of min(n_t, 2 - n_t) * w_tA, with n_t the token occupation and w_tA its centre weight on atom A, both already in P2b's token files. sum_A rho_A is the restricted unpaired count (Br. 3 x 1/3 = 1; .OH 2 x 1/2 = 1; O2 2 x 1 = 2). It is QM-derived and does not depend on the resonance drawing, unlike P6's categorical u, which stays for keys and bookkeeping. It is 0 for every u = 0 species, Fermi-smeared closed shells included, since they have no open sub-block. It is appended at sample assembly as a sample-level atom column somo_population, not an AtomDescriptors field (token files are read by field name, tokens.py:135-150, 371-386). ModelConfig.atom_spin_input in {none, categorical_u, somo_population}: from_dict fills 'none' for old configs, and P9's 3-seed ablation sets the default, recorded in decisions.md.
   - Files: src/ksmech/arrows/reactants.py, src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py, src/ksmech/model/inputs.py:35-76, 90-108, src/ksmech/model/config.py:34-38
   - Tests: Closed-shell fixtures: all zeros. [CH3]: the C carries most of the 1. Br.: sum 1. The two allyl-radical drawings give the same rho, while their categorical u differs. An old checkpoint loads with atom_spin_input 'none' and ignores the column.
4. [M] Occupation-weighted gap factor for the legacy column g: G(r,s) = sum (n_k - n_m) p_rk p_sm / (E_k - E_m). After P2b every MO in a token's spectrum has the token's occupation, so the weight comes from token occupations and no token field is added. The constant-2 fast path is kept for closed-shell blocks, so their G is bitwise identical (GFN2's smeared occupations are not exactly 2). SOMO->empty and doubly->hole cells carry weight 1 (one- and three-electron interactions); open-row x hole-column cells leave g and log_abs_g undefined (n_k - n_m = 0).
   - Files: src/ksmech/physics/ks_terms.py:35, 57-71, 101-115
   - Tests: 0-ulp equality on the 10 fixtures and on every closed-shell pair_z of a 500-sample closed_base slice. Ethyl SOMO->virtual and doubly->hole weights are 1. Br. pooled doubly->hole weight is 1.
5. [M] Own-pair channel (new). Own-pair cells are a bonding token and its own antibonding partner (sigma->sigma*, pi->pi*), the cells P0a's own-antibond flag channel already marks and keeps. P7 adds value columns own_g (the cell's g) and own_g2 (its g2), defined only on flagged cells, in a separate named tuple OWN_PAIR_FEATURES, not in STAGE_A_FEATURES, so the GBDT feature list (baseline.py:48 FEATURE_NAMES, checked at :132 and lgbm.py:270-271) and serve's gbdt predictor are untouched. Own-pair cells are kept out of G^atom and y_ij pooling (pooled_gap, atom_pair_physics) and pooled on their own into the bond's atom pair, B^own_ij = sum over own cells of (w_i^r w_j^s + w_j^r w_i^s) own_g, in the same 1/eV units as G^atom. A SOMO's own hole is not an own-pair cell (it is masked by the previous step). Statistics come from defined entries only, with a fixed fallback scale where a split has none. This is what makes homolysis reachable for the floor: 1,096 of the 5,088 RS/RC lines are break-only (21.5%; RC 169, RS 927), 983 after P8's dedup (RC 129, RS 854), measured.
   - Files: src/ksmech/physics/stage_a.py:37-50, 136-171, src/ksmech/physics/pairs.py:107-111, src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py:132-140, src/ksmech/model/inputs.py:148-175 (pooled_gap and atom_pair_physics unchanged; new pooled_own)
   - Tests: The ethane C-C sigma->sigma* cell is flagged and carries own_g. Own cells never enter pooled_gap: G^atom of ethane and acetone is unchanged to 0 ulp. B^own is defined only on bonded atom pairs. STAGE_A_FEATURES and FEATURE_NAMES are unchanged, and the own-pair PairStats refuses a mismatched feature list loudly.
6. [M] Two-level column g2 (U23 as revised; no percentile delta, no SOMO->partner exclusion). On ALL cells, intramolecular and intermolecular, closed and open: g2(r,s) = sum_km (n_k - n_m) p_rk p_sm / (sign(dE_km) D_km), with dE_km = E_k - E_m, sign(0) = -1 and D_km = (|dE_km| + sqrt(dE_km^2 + 4 beta_rs^2)) / 2 (resolution 13): G with each canonical-pair denominator replaced by the exact two-level one, which tends to |dE| for a large gap and to |beta| at degeneracy, so g2 stays finite, is bounded by 1/|beta| per unit weight, and equals g as beta -> 0 on every cell. Where a level crossing inverts a pair (the intermolecular NO3/NO2/OH SOMOs below donor HOMOs), g2 takes g's sign; the earlier one-sign form (-1/D on every pair) differs only on those inverted pairs and is recorded in decisions.md as the alternative for your decision. beta_rs is the cell's Wolfsberg-Helmholz cross-centre coupling (cross_coupling at KSParams.K = 1.75) for intramolecular cells, and one stated constant beta_inter for intermolecular cells, which have no geometry. beta_inter is set once from the tabulation step below (its value and derivation recorded in decisions.md, not tuned on any exit test) and frozen before the P8 build, because g2 is baked into the v19 samples. New columns g2 and log_abs_g2 in their own tuple G2_FEATURES, selected by name; legacy g and log_abs_g are untouched, so the closed-shell 0-ulp gate holds. The GBDT keeps FEATURE_NAMES by default; a GBDT and a network with g2 are P9's 'g vs g2 vs both' arms.
   - Files: src/ksmech/physics/ks_terms.py (gap_kernel_two_level beside gap_kernel_matrix), src/ksmech/physics/stage_a.py, src/ksmech/physics/params.py (beta_inter), src/ksmech/model/inputs.py:134-138
   - Tests: With beta = 0, g2 equals g on every fixture cell to 1e-12 relative, away from the 1e-3 eV clamp (the 0-ulp gate stays on g). g2 is finite on every cell of the open-shell fixtures, C6H6.- and the anionic peroxyls, and |g2| <= sum |n_k - n_m| p_rk p_sm / |beta_rs|. A synthetic two-level pair swept through dE = 0 stays bounded by 1/|beta| on both sides and flips sign with dE, as g does. Legacy g is unchanged to 0 ulp.
7. They form a separate open-shell pair channel, OPEN_SHELL_PAIR_FEATURES, with its own arrays and statistics from defined entries only.

   - Tests: The legacy floor reproduces closed_base exactly. own_break proposes the O-O bond of a peroxide fixture, and a fixture whose only admissible anchor is a homolysis is reachable under own_break and unreachable under the legacy floor.
11. Diagnostics only: no count of cases triggers anything. D2 (spGFN2 for u > 0) is a planned ablation after P9.
   - Files: src/ksmech/physics/textbook.py:58-76, 161-166
   - Tests: Each case builds its QM records and tokens on demand (small species).
12. [S] A scratch v19 build of the closed_base val and test parts in the Release-B worktree data root (about 1.2 GB, estimate; 20 GB free on /home, measured 2026-10-28). P7's gates need a v19 superset store, and the real one exists only in P8.
   - Files: (CLI runs in the Release-B worktree)

13. [S] Diagnostics and docs: OrbitalType SOMO and SOMO_HOLE (model_input=False); hole counts in the samples report; spec v10 tokens and physics sections (the occupation-weighted G, the two-level g2 with beta_rs and beta_inter, the own-pair channel outside G^atom, tex:618 as 'open-row x hole-column cells within one fragment'); the CLAUDE.md paragraph 'Until the radical extension ... neither'; data_schema.md for hole tokens, spin_role, somo_population, G2_FEATURES, OWN_PAIR_FEATURES and OPEN_SHELL_PAIR_FEATURES; drafts of the wiki pages klopman-salem.md (G with the factor 2, occupation weights, the two-level form) and orbital-tokens.md, published at the P8 merge. Dated decisions.md entries: g2 as a new column beside g; beta_inter's value and how it was set; no percentile delta and no SOMO->partner exclusion; own-pair cells outside G^atom; the metal-d flag default pending P9; the SOMO population as the default spin input candidate.

   - Tests: Docs only; the wiki renders through GET /api/wiki in the serve tests.

**Data effect.** No step is added or removed, and no token file changes (the localization and frontier rule landed in P2b). In PMechDB about 104 open-shell tokens become donor candidates, each with one hole at sample assembly (fact-check count). Cu(II)/Fe(III) d open shells stay masked under the default flag. Closed-shell pair cells are unchanged on every legacy column; g2, the own-pair channel, spin_role and somo_population are new columns.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** No token or QM rebuild. STAGE_A_FEATURES and the GBDT feature list are unchanged. The new named channels (G2_FEATURES, OWN_PAIR_FEATURES, OPEN_SHELL_PAIR_FEATURES) and the spin_role and somo_population columns enter the samples, which are built once in P8 at SAMPLE_VERSION 19. beta_inter is frozen before P8; changing it afterwards means a v19 rebuild, refit pair statistics and GBDT, and retraining. FLOOR_VERSION 3 -> 4. Old checkpoints keep working through name-based selection. Serve's physics_fingerprint includes CandidateConfig, so stored pool analyses are recomputed once (closed-shell values unchanged). Serve keeps refusing radical pools until S2, so its constant-2 gatom never meets an open shell.

**Exit criteria.** Closed-shell legacy g is identical to 0 ulp. On the scratch v19 build, the closed_base per-column digests, the legacy floor and the P0b checkpoint decodes are unchanged. On the open-shell fixtures each open LMO gives one donor and one hole, and somo_population sums to u. g2 is finite and bounded on every cell of the open-shell fixtures, C6H6.- and the anionic peroxyls, and equals g at beta = 0 on closed-shell intramolecular cells. The |g|/|g2| table (on v18, with and without the flagged cells), both floors and the radical suite are reported with and without intramolecular open-shell cells, as diagnostics with no threshold. beta_inter is recorded in decisions.md. pytest green: `uv run pytest -n 14 -m "not realdata"` and `uv run pytest -m realdata`, both summary lines pasted.

**Risks.** The restricted SOMO lies inside the closed-shell gap near one edge (ethyl -8.21 eV between -11.79 and +3.60; peroxyl 1.1 eV above the doubly occupied manifold), so the gaps toward that edge are too small; g2 bounds them through beta, and D2's split levels are a planned ablation after P9. beta_inter is one constant for every intermolecular cell, so g2 between molecules is compressed, not ranked by contact; P9 measures g vs g2 vs both. Own-antibond identification inherits P0a's flag (the site rule, site_weight 0.20 and 1.3 r_cov, tokens.py:289-303): a missed partner leaves that bond's B^own undefined and its homolysis unreachable, counted. Recombination rests on somo_pair and somo_level_diff alone. The duplicated column breaks 'C_loc columns are S-orthonormal' (tokens.py:23-24, 330-334); holes exist only in samples, so cube rendering is unaffected. Open-shell tokens are about 1e-4 of the occupied tokens of PMechDB, so rmechdb is what trains them.

### P8. Radical source rmechdb (FlowER RC+RS), one spin and domain law for every source, and the Release B build (stacked branch 3 of 3)

**Depends on:** P2, P6, P7

**Goal.** Ingest genuine one-electron elementary steps already on disk under one fixed normalization (bath-gas strip, Lewis pairing decided by spin_table_v1 and a degenerate-frontier test, RC-wins dedup), compute their species, build every sample store once at SAMPLE_VERSION 19 with the closed-shell samples identical to v18 on every v18 named column, and move it all to main.

**Steps.**

1. [S] Inspect data/raw/flower/flower_new_dataset/{train,test}.txt and document it in data_schema.md (never fabricate a column). Cover: the 'mapped rxn|SUFFIX' format; the subset counts (RC 1,383/136, RS 3,223/346, PM 12,322/294, PC 14,746/1,653; val has none); the file's 2,174,470 lines, 2,140,367 with a numeric suffix (about 2.14M, not the 1.4M in CLAUDE.md's table); the suffix meanings (RS/RC inferred as RMechDB specific/core, PM as PMechDB, PC unknown, all marked as inferred); full RXNMapper maps with explicit H (0 unmapped atoms, 0 heavy atoms with implicit H); the bath gases [Ar]/[He] (165 lines: RC 137, RS 28; unbonded with one map number on both sides in every case); O2 drawn only as [O][O]; CO only as [C]=O; ozone as [O]O[O] or [O][O+][O-]; N2 as [N]=[N] in 5 RC lines and N#N in 8; metal-bearing lines Sn 56, Na 12, K 3, Mg 3, all RC, with Na and K often drawn covalently ([O][Na] ketyls, the Na...benzene Birch adduct, K persulfate); and the license chain (RMechDB CC-BY-NC-ND per its article PMC9976277 -> FlowER, MIT label on the repo and on figshare 32513667, not relied on, U18). The stored column is the raw suffix. The inferred core/specific meaning lives only in data_schema.md and a derived view, replaced when D1 joins the official CSV.
   - Files: docs/data_schema.md
   - Tests: Docs only.
2. [M] Loader data/rmechdb.py. It streams only the lines ending in |RS or |RC, without loading the 3.1 GB train.txt into memory. Before the id, in this order, each a counted repair: NOBLE_GAS_STRIPPED removes [Ar]/[He] third bodies and re-validates; then the drawing normalization of the next step (PAIRED_DRAWING, which includes [N]=[N] -> N#N in 5 RC lines, decided now so no id moves after the split freezes; HIGH_SPIN_DRAWING, 0 on disk). step_id is make_step_id('rmechdb', map-free canonical reaction after both), i.e. 'rmechdb-' + 16 hex of sha1 as for the other sources. One step per id: on an RS/RC collision the RC copy wins (83 reactions; 101 RS lines dropped for an RC copy), otherwise the first line in file order (train before test); duplicates are DUPLICATE_STEP (553 lines), and a reaction in both FlowER train and test (120 after the strip, 121 before) is flagged. So the RC split depends neither on file order nor on whether RS is ingested. Measured (revise/probes/p8_split.py): 5,088 lines -> 4,559 distinct before the strip -> 4,535 after it (the N#N repair merges none) -> kept RC 1,347 and RS 3,188. Columns: flower_suffix (RS|RC, raw) and flower_part (train|test), provenance via=flower; arrows=[] and require_arrows=False. Add LOADERS[Source.RMECHDB]; the enum already exists. FlowER PM and PC are never ingested.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/rmechdb.py (new), src/ksmech/data/cli.py:21-25, src/ksmech/data/schema.py:14-19, src/ksmech/data/validation.py:58 (make_step_id), tests/fixtures/mini_flower.txt (new, hand-written)
   - Tests: Unit test on 9 lines written by hand in FlowER's format (homolysis, recombination, HAT, addition, beta-scission, resonance, a bath-gas line and its bath-free twin, an RS/RC collision), as tests/fixtures/mini_pmechdb.csv is, never copied from FlowER: the twins share one id, the collision keeps RC, [N]=[N] is repaired before the id. realdata: 5 real lines read from data/raw/flower at run time.
3. [M] One spin and domain law for every source (PMechDB, ReactMech, rmechdb), replacing the CO/N2/O3/O2 lists and O3_DRAWN. Multiplicity of a drawn species: (i) spin_table_v1 (P2's rule C over the spGFN2 scan at every allowed multiplicity and spin_theory_v1, for every carbene-, CO-, ozone- and u >= 2-shaped species of the three sources), MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS where C assigns none; (ii) otherwise the degenerate-frontier (Hund) test on the restricted record: a partly filled degenerate highest group (equal GFN2 occupations, P2b's grouping) is high spin; (iii) otherwise u from N parity (P6). A Hund-case verdict holds for every drawing with the species' v1 key (O=O and [O][O]); any other verdict only for the drawing it was made on, so a genuine diradical drawing is never paired by default. Lewis pairing rule: a drawing with odd-N atoms whose species is decided singlet is redrawn as the closed-shell Lewis structure on its sigma skeleton reached by electron moves between bonded atoms (P6's flow law), ranked by complete octets, then fewest formal charges: N#N, [O-][O+]=O, O=[N+]=O; counted PAIRED_DRAWING. A paired drawing of a Hund-case high-spin species is redrawn unpaired (HIGH_SPIN_DRAWING, O=O -> [O][O]; 0 on disk). MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES: a species with u >= 2 after the decision whose open shell is not one degenerate group (the Hund case is the only u >= 2 form P2b's tokens represent); excluded and counted in every source. Exemptions are a counted config table with a reason each; today one entry, PMechDB 5d007b38 (Et3B + O2, U6), whose product CC[B-](CC)(CC)[O+][O] is kept as drawn. Measured upper bounds (RDKit count, after dedup; revise/probes/p8_census.py, p8_u2.py): 66 rmechdb lines (RC 38, RS 28) hold a u >= 2 species other than CO, N2, O3, O2 and O; of their 50 species, 9 have the radical centres on one atom or on two bonded atoms (pairing candidates) and 41 on distant atoms (Bergman p-benzyne, Norrish and terpene peroxyl-alkoxyl biradicals, Criegee [CH2]O[O], [O][N+]([O])=O); pairing and the table can only lower this. Ozone (23 lines: RC 11, RS 12; 18 after dedup) is admitted as closed-shell ozone where the table says singlet. CO (33 lines; 23 after dedup) stays: N(C) = 2 is already paired in the BE drawing. O2 (1,299 lines; 1,175 after dedup) and O (8; 7) are Hund cases. The P1 repair laws and P0a's metal laws apply to rmechdb too, with no source switch; measured, the V1 shape (a C with 3 radical electrons) occurs in at most 1 RC line, A1 (one neutral alkali atom unchanged on both sides) and CDI in 0, and the metal-bearing lines go through METAL_PLACEHOLDER, METAL_SUSPECT and P4's policy, counted per code. domain_class is RADICAL_STEP for rmechdb; P6's R_ii rule is the only per-source switch, at label time. Every new code is added to PRECEDENCE and stats.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/spin.py, src/ksmech/data/domain.py:28-33, 47-59, 162-200, src/ksmech/data/validation.py:167-205, src/ksmech/data/stats.py, src/ksmech/data/rmechdb.py
   - Tests: [N]=[N] -> N#N, counted. [O]O[O] -> [O-][O+]=O given a singlet table row. O=O keys and draws as [O][O] given a Hund row. C=C does not take a [CH2][CH2] row. A distant-centre biradical is MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES under the pmechdb and rmechdb policies alike. 5d007b38 is kept and counted as an exemption. [C]=O is admitted with v2 = v1. Ar is stripped and counted. A spectator [CH] radical is not H-completed (V1's signature is a C with 3 radical electrons only).
4. [M] Split strategies, frozen at their first build (frozen split files carry their own schema from P0, independent of SAMPLE_VERSION). rc_molecule (the default) is the molecule split over RC. RS is training data only, and RS steps that share a non-trivial species with an RC val/test group are excluded as LEAK_GUARD and counted; RS cannot be split by molecule (3,425 of its 3,569 lines form one group). flower_given is FlowER's own train/test, with val drawn as 10% of the FlowER-train RC molecule groups; it is leaky by construction and used only for the RMechRP comparison. Strategies are dispatched by name, so both go into STRATEGIES (steps.py:94), split_ids (steps.py:455-475) and the CLI choices (arrows/cli.py:42); register DEFAULT_SPLIT[Source.RMECHDB]. Measured under the previous step's rule with today's _assign_groups at seed 0: RC 1,077/134/136 (401 molecule groups, the largest 434); LEAK_GUARD 74 RS steps with sha1 step ids (81 with line-index labels), so about 3,114 RS training steps; 34 of the 270 RC val/test steps have no non-trivial species. The earlier RC 1,078/134/136 and LEAK_GUARD 115-420 came from first-seen or no dedup. RC test (136) gives a 95% Wilson interval of about +-8 points at 60%.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/splits.py:53-79, 160-180, src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py:94, 455-475, src/ksmech/arrows/cli.py:42
   - Tests: rc_molecule: no non-trivial species is shared between training (RC train plus RS) and RC val/test (asserted). Seeded, deterministic, frozen; re-ingesting places only new ids, by hash.
5. [M] QM and tokens, in the Release-B worktree's data root (new keys only). A spin census asserts that only u in {0, 1}, Hund-case high-spin species (one degenerate open group) and counted exemptions reach QM. Then `ksmech-qm run --source rmechdb --workers 8` (restricted, data.spin), `ksmech-qm bond-index`, `ksmech-orbitals build --source rmechdb --workers 4` (P2b's rule applies to every u > 0 species), and `ksmech-arrows edit-labels --source rmechdb` with the coverage gate at 95%. Size, before dedup and by RDKit's count: 4,284 unique species (1,841 closed-shell, 2,387 doublets, 53 u=2, 3 u=3), 210 already cached, so about 4,000 new records (median 20 atoms with H, p95 44, max 134; 61 above 60 atoms). At most 15 heavy processes on the host.
   - Files: src/ksmech/qm/batch.py:290-320, (CLI runs)
   - Tests: The census refuses a u = 2 species without a Hund verdict or an exemption.
6. [S] Leakage audit in both directions, using the molecule-split species rule (non-trivial means more than 3 heavy atoms, splits.py:128-130). Map-free reaction keys and molecule overlap of the rmechdb test parts are checked against PMechDB, ReactMech and mech-USPTO, with species matched through key v2 (so O2 matches however it is drawn). rmechdb training steps (RC train and RS) that share a non-trivial species with closed_base val/test are excluded from joint training as LEAK_GUARD, counted. PMechDB training steps that share a species with RC val/test are not removed, so the PMechDB training set stays identical across pm_control and joint. RC val/test and closed_base are reported with and without the overlapping samples. Measured against today's PMechDB molecule split (closed_base's proxy before P0a/P0b; revise/probes/p8_xleak.py): 38 rmechdb training steps (RC 19, RS 19) share a species with its val/test; 14 of the 270 RC val/test steps share one with its train; 143 PMechDB train steps share one with RC val/test (a slice, not removed).

   - Tests: The audit report lists every overlapping step id in both directions.
7. [M] The Release B build. Spec v10 is approved and committed first. In the Release-B worktree: re-ingest PMechDB and ReactMech under key v2 and the spin and domain law (closed-shell steps unchanged; the open-shell pools of P3/P4 and the still-excluded radical pools re-keyed; browse INDEX_VERSION 2 -> 3, pmindex.py:43). Set SAMPLE_VERSION 18 -> 19 with CandidateConfig stamped in steps._config. Build samples for pmechdb, reactmech and rmechdb (rc_molecule and flower_given); the admit set is read in build_all_samples (steps.py:565-578). Store any mask channel you adopted after P0a's threshold reach report (resolution 7). Refit pair_feature_stats. Refit the GBDT models on the v19 views, since their sample-file inputs are replaced (lgbm.py:261-265), and record the new GBDT reference. Run both floors on the radical cohort by R shape. scripts/compare_stores.py compares PMechDB and ReactMech v19 against the v18 stores of P0a/P0b on the v18 named columns. Then: push the rmechdb artifacts to the private rhoMech-data as for PMechDB (U18), never to the public repo; migrate the worktree's new records, tokens, stores, views and browse index into main's data/ with `cp -a --reflink=always` (not hard links), with the ksmech-serve instance on main stopped (the browse index sha reads steps.parquet, and the merge below edits tracked files); run `ksmech-serve rekey-pools --dry-run`, then the re-key or purge; merge P6, P7 and P8 to main in order; verify every artifact from main with `ksmech-data provenance` before the v18 stores are deleted; start ksmech-serve again.

   - Tests: compare_stores.py has a unit test on two tiny stores that differ in one named column. The frozen PMechDB and ReactMech split tests pass on v19.
8. [S] Docs: CLAUDE.md data-sources table (RMechDB via FlowER RS/RC now and the official CSV later, its license and role; FlowER's numeric-suffix set about 2.14M lines) and commands (rmechdb ingest, splits, survey, rekey-pools); data_schema.md (rmechdb section, flower_suffix, NOBLE_GAS_STRIPPED, PAIRED_DRAWING, HIGH_SPIN_DRAWING, MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES and its exemption table); the wiki pages reaction-matrix.md (+-2 and the closed-shell sigma key at :25, :78), klopman-salem.md and orbital-tokens.md, published at the merge; the citations of RMechDB (PMC9976277) and FlowER (repository and figshare 32513667); a license note (CC-BY-NC-ND; artifacts kept like PMechDB's: local, private rhoMech-data and pods, never public; not legal advice; NC matters if ChemRho is offered commercially). Dated decisions.md entries: the dedup rule (strip, pairing, RC wins) and the measured split; the spin and domain law and the exemption table; the repair laws on every source; U18 storage.
   - Files: CLAUDE.md, docs/decisions.md, docs/data_schema.md, content/wiki/reaction-matrix.md, content/wiki/klopman-salem.md, content/wiki/orbital-tokens.md
   - Tests: Docs only; no real FlowER RS/RC line appears in any tracked file (grep in a test).

**Data effect.** rmechdb: 5,088 lines (RC 1,519, RS 3,569) -> 4,535 distinct reactions after the strip and the pairing normalization; kept RC 1,347 and RS 3,188. Same-sigma lines: 194 before dedup (RC 143, RS 51): 192 obey the flow law and go to resonance_pairs, recounted under v2 (the 6 that change per-fragment u become steps), 1 is NULL_STEP, 1 is the ozone redraw that pairing turns into an ozone resonance pair. Excluded and counted: MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES at most 66 lines after dedup (RC 38, RS 28; upper bound), NOT_ELEMENTARY 14 after dedup (15 lines), SET_STEP 0, NULL_STEP 1, DISSOCIATIVE_ET_STEP and NONCONJUGATED_SHIFT 0 expected, LEAK_GUARD (RC rule 74 with sha1 ids; cross-source 38 against today's PMechDB split), DUPLICATE_STEP 553 lines, the metal codes of P0a/P4 (71 kept RC lines carry a metal). Repairs: NOBLE_GAS_STRIPPED 165 lines, PAIRED_DRAWING (N#N 5 RC lines, ozone up to 23), HIGH_SPIN_DRAWING 0. Break-only (homolysis) lines: 1,096 (RC 169, RS 927), 983 after dedup (RC 129, RS 854). 19 lines (17 after dedup) have 3-4 atom entries and 8 have more than 8 bond entries; rmechdb's caps come from its own label maxima by P0a's rule (4 atom and 15 bond entries on the raw lines), so they stay in reach. rc_molecule at seed 0: RC 1,077/134/136 and about 3,114 RS training steps. Expected training target: about 3,900-4,100 steps (estimate). QM: about 4,000 new records. PMechDB and ReactMech: steps unchanged; the open-shell pools of P3/P4 re-keyed and their resonance flags listed; the multi-radical law counted there too (PMechDB 5d007b38 exempt).

**Rebuilds and bumps.** SAMPLE_VERSION 18 -> 19 refuses and rebuilds every store once: PMechDB about 12.5k samples, ReactMech about 9k, rmechdb about 4.5k, about 30 min on 8 workers (estimates). The frozen split and cohort files keep their own schema and are not refused. pair_feature_stats are refit. The GBDT is refit on v19 (a refit cannot reproduce the P0b numbers bit for bit, because LightGBM samples 80% of the columns), and its numbers are recorded. Browse INDEX_VERSION 2 -> 3. QM takes about 1-2 h on 8 workers, tokens about 20 min on 4 (estimates). No QM or token schema bump, and no existing cache key changes. The migration into main is by reflink copy. Disk: about 5-7 GB of new data (estimate) against 20 GB free on /home (btrfs, 96% used, measured 2026-10-28). Serve's pool states are re-keyed or purged once; POOL_KEY_VERSION stays 2.

**Exit criteria.** PMechDB and ReactMech v19 equal the v18 stores on every v18 named column for every closed-shell-pool sample; the open-shell samples' diffs are listed. The closed_base legacy floor is identical, and the new GBDT reference is recorded. The PMechDB and ReactMech frozen-split tests pass, and the rmechdb splits are frozen with their ids. rmechdb label coverage is at least 95% of non-resonance steps, and every skip, repair and exemption is counted. The leakage audit is reported in both directions. Both floors are reported on the radical cohort by R shape with their unreachable counts. The serve re-key or purge is done and its count reported. rmechdb artifacts are in the private rhoMech-data and in no public repository or tracked fixture. Release B is merged to main with ksmech-serve stopped, every artifact verifies from main, and serve is started again. pytest green: `uv run pytest -n 14 -m "not realdata"` and `uv run pytest -m realdata`, both summary lines pasted.

**Risks.** The FlowER suffix meanings are inferred. RXNMapper maps of radical steps can be wrong. Atmospheric RS data (O2 in 1,299 lines, NO3, OH, HO2) dominates and is narrow, which is why RS is train-only and measured as an ablation. QM timeouts on the 61 species above 60 atoms. Sn radicals (Bu3Sn., 56 Sn lines) are untested in IBO. The license chain is CC-BY-NC-ND -> MIT; U18 treats rmechdb like PMechDB (not legal advice). The exemption table is a hand list of one entry: a measured law alternative (an inherited triplet: the u >= 2 product holds the atoms of a reactant [O][O] or [O] with u conserved) admits 5d007b38 plus 2 RS lines of genuine diradicals, so it is not adopted without your decision. The pairing rule reads spin_table_v1: a later table version that changes a normalized drawing changes that step's id, and re-ingest places it as a new step and lists it. Split sizes depend on the id labelling (LEAK_GUARD 74 vs 81). Commit before building, because tracked edits mark outputs dirty.

### P9. Open-shell decoder head, training and ablations

**Depends on:** P8, P0b, P4

**Goal.** Train a model that writes one-electron steps without losing closed-shell accuracy, with every rule left in its decode path either electron bookkeeping or a flag whose default a recorded ablation sets, and measure what the SOMO channels, the own-pair channel, g2, the atom unpaired input, the metal-d mask, the FD frontier variant, the RS data and the optional -1 decode filter add.

**Steps.**

1. [S] ModelConfig gains open_shell=False, somo_channels and own_pair_channel; the atom spin input is P7's atom_spin_input ('none' | 'categorical_u' | 'somo_population'). The G variant (g, g2 or both) is a pair-feature name selection, so it uses drop_pair_features, which exists today (CLAUDE.md: '--drop-pair-features g log_abs_g'). Every new field defaults to the behaviour of today's checkpoints, and from_dict fills missing keys with those defaults (config.py:34-38). So the existing PMechDB, ReactMech and pmechdb+reactmech runs and the P0b GENERAL and LEGACY runs load strictly with their 3-class head. New runs write their full configuration into run.json: the P0b GENERAL flags, open_shell=True and the P7/P2b input defaults.
   - Files: src/ksmech/model/config.py:34-38, src/ksmech/train/cli.py (one option per field, as today)
   - Tests: A run.json without the new keys (dense_plain, flex_first) loads strictly. A 5-class configuration round-trips through to_dict/from_dict.
2. [M] Head, masks and loss. With open_shell, the atom head has 5 classes {-2,-1,0,+1,+2}. The per-atom masks are electron bookkeeping and stay: -1 needs N >= 1 and -2 needs N >= 2 (non-negative non-bonding counts, from P6's N). No pool-level mask enters the head or the loss. The old 'no -1 entry when the drawing's u = 0' rule is an inference about which step types exist, not a conservation law. In the raw FlowER RS/RC lines it would give zero probability to 2 of the 795 u = 0 labels: an ozone drawing, which P8's pairing rule redraws (PAIRED_DRAWING), and a persulfate O-O homolysis drawn with the radical on a terminal O (rshape_scan.py re-run 2026-10-28, approximate spin rule; P6's dry run counts them on the real labels). The loss takes its value-to-class table from the config (P0's explicit table). The decoder takes ATOM_VALUES from the config and keeps the pair (P,3) and atom (A,5) log-probabilities as separate arrays. The sign count comes from ANCHOR_SIDE in decode, greedy_edit and embedding.
   - Files: src/ksmech/model/decoder.py:41-53, 73-96 (entry_logits masks), src/ksmech/model/loss.py (_entry_sums, greedy_edit), src/ksmech/model/decode.py:45-58, 100-115, 176-197, 214, 250-260, src/ksmech/eval/embedding.py:215
   - Tests: Loss and decode round-trip the 8 radical shapes. A -1 label on a u = 0 pool has a finite loss. A -1 entry on an atom with N = 0 and a -2 entry on an atom with N < 2 stay at -inf. 3-class checkpoints decode closed_base with identical per-sample hits.
3. [M] Decode screen and optional filter. The closed-shell screen is always on, for pools with u = 0 (u from the drawing's N, U12): every odd +1 atom entry must sit on an endpoint of some bond with a -1 entry. It is bookkeeping under the domain's exclusion of electron transfer (SET_STEP and P6's dissociative-ET code): with no unpaired electron in the pool and no electron hop, an atom's N turns odd only by keeping one electron of a bond it loses. It replaces 'odd entries only as +1 on both endpoints of one -1 bond', which refused 20 of the 795 u = 0 RS/RC lines in the re-run scan. 18 of them pass the new screen: azo initiators (AIBN, azomethane), Cl2/Br2 + RH and concerted fragmentations. The other 2 are the -1 lines above. The -1 part is a separate decode flag, --closed-minus1-filter (refuse any -1 atom entry on a u = 0 pool), outside the head and the loss; step 9 sets its default by ablation. With both on, the screen reads 'each odd entry is +1 on some broken bond'. Screened and filtered completions are counted under DECODE_DROPPED:<reason>, like today's CHARGE_NOT_CONSERVED and OCTET_EXCEEDED drops. DECODE_VERSION 3 -> 4 (1 today, 2 after P0's report fields, 3 after P0a's per-source caps).
   - Files: src/ksmech/model/decode.py (completion checks beside the existing drops), src/ksmech/eval/cli.py:45-46 and the decode options
   - Tests: These pass on a u = 0 pool: a homolysis (+1/+1 on one broken bond), azomethane fragmentation (two C +1 on two broken C-N bonds, hand-written SMILES), and Cl2 + RH (Cl +1 and C +1 on different broken bonds). A lone +1 on an atom with no broken bond is dropped. A -1 entry passes with the filter off and is dropped with it on. The screen does not act when u > 0. The drop counts appear in the report.
4. [S] Atom input and ablation switches at batch time. Per atom_spin_input, the atom spin input reads either the third atom_categorical column (categorical u, P6, recomputed per resonance drawing) or P7's per-atom restricted SOMO population (continuous, the same for every drawing). Every ablation arm is a batch-time selection out of the v19 superset store (P0a keeps all (occupied, virtual) cells; P7 step 8 extends the name-based selection). The selectable inputs are: the pair-feature names (g and log_abs_g, g2 and its log, the own-pair channel, P0a's d-type, own-antibond and site channels); the open-shell pair channel with its defined mask; the hole columns; the atom frontier inputs (frozen f+ = f- or P2b's FD f+/f- variant; P0a's legacy 0.05 eV or exact-degeneracy frontier blocks); any threshold flag you adopt after P0a's reach report; and P7's metal-d mask flag. somo_channels=False zeroes the open-shell pair channel and clears pair_valid on open-shell rows and hole columns. own_pair_channel=False zeroes that channel. No arm needs a store rebuild; an arm that would is reported as such and not run.
   - Files: src/ksmech/model/encoder.py:22-51, src/ksmech/model/inputs.py, src/ksmech/train/loop.py (batch transforms)
   - Tests: Each switch changes only its named columns: the digests of the other columns on a fixture batch are unchanged. Flex and sparse read the same switches as dense.
5. [M] Multi-source plumbing. (a) A per-source split map in the trainer (--split pmechdb=molecule,rmechdb=rc_molecule, recorded in run.json). The merged trainer applies one --split to every source and would look for rmechdb samples_molecule_* views, which do not exist. (b) Every cohort is decoded with --run-source (P0), separate from the samples' --source. (c) Serve's checkpoint NAME_RE ([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,99}, serve/ml/checkpoints.py:41) accepts '+', with a test. Today :184 skips such directories, so serve does not list the merged pmechdb+reactmech/sparse_resonance run either. (d) SOURCE_STOP_WEIGHT['rmechdb'] = 0.0 (isolated steps). The minimum over sources already exists (loop.py:102-107), so every joint run has lambda_stop 0, and END_MECH stays untrained (no source builds stop labels; see D3). (e) Per-source decoder caps: rmechdb's caps follow P0a's rule from its P8 label maxima, and each cohort decodes with its own source's caps. On the raw RS/RC lines the maxima are 4 atom entries (19 of 5,088 lines above the default 2) and 15 bond entries (8 lines above the default 8; caps_scan.py 2026-10-28); the caps are taken from the P8 labels. (f) run.json and every joint report state how the joint run is selected and standardized. best.pt is picked on pooled val_top1, the anchor top-1 over closed_base val and RC val together (loop.py:18-21, better_epoch :434-440). The pair statistics are fitted on the pooled training parts (run_pair_stats, loop.py:398-405).
   - Files: src/ksmech/train/loop.py (split, stop_weight, run.json), src/ksmech/train/cli.py, src/ksmech/eval/cli.py, src/ksmech/model/decode.py (DecodeConfig per source), src/ksmech/serve/ml/checkpoints.py:41, 184
   - Tests: A joint run resolves each source's own views. A checkpoint under pmechdb+rmechdb/ is listed by serve and decoded on closed_base and on RC val. Decode picks its caps by the samples' source. run.json records the split map, the pooled selection and the pooled statistics.
6. [S] Per-sample hits, paired comparison and metrics. Decode has written per-sample hits since P0 (step id, rank of the first hit or none), and `ksmech-eval compare` exists since P0b (one-sided exact McNemar on majority-of-3 hits, the 9 seed-pair tests, per-arm means and pooled SD). This step adds the drop reasons of P9's screen and filter to the hit files and the per-source cohorts to compare. Also: per-source and per-subset metrics in metrics.jsonl; R-shape diagnostics computed from R alone (homolysis, recombination, addition, abstraction/SH2, beta-scission, other; model_input=False); SOMO and hole labels in the embedding page; and the radical textbook suite evaluated with trained models.
   - Files: src/ksmech/eval/cli.py (decode hits, compare), src/ksmech/eval/paired.py (from P0b), src/ksmech/train/loop.py (metrics), src/ksmech/eval/embedding_page.py:79-80, src/ksmech/physics/textbook.py
   - Tests: A hits file reproduces the aggregate top-1/3/5. McNemar on a hand-made pair of hit vectors matches the exact binomial value. compare refuses arms decoded on different cohorts or decode versions.
7. [S] Smoke run: `ksmech-train fit --source pmechdb+rmechdb --split pmechdb=molecule,rmechdb=rc_molecule --open-shell --limit 64 --epochs 1`, then `ksmech-eval decode --run-source pmechdb+rmechdb --source pmechdb --limit 32` and `--source rmechdb --limit 32`, each with and without --closed-minus1-filter, then `ksmech-eval compare` on the two hit files.
   - Files: src/ksmech/train/cli.py, src/ksmech/eval/cli.py
   - Tests: The smoke fit and the four decodes finish; each writes a hit file whose aggregate equals its report; compare runs on the two hit files of each source and refuses a mismatched pair.
8. [L] Gate runs (U20). Each arm has 3 seeds, uses the dense encoder (the P0b reference) and the P0b GENERAL flags. pm_control: PMechDB only, open_shell=True, v19. joint: pmechdb + rmechdb rc_molecule with RS, open_shell=True, the P7/P2b input defaults; it is the reference arm of every ablation. pm_v19: PMechDB only, v19, open_shell=False, 1 seed, raised to 3 if pm_control misses its tolerance; it separates P7/P8's input changes from the head change. The gate is one-sided on closed_base val full-R top-1 from decode. Link 1: pm_control vs P4's 3 Release-A models. Link 2: joint vs pm_control. A link passes when the later arm's mean is not below the earlier arm's mean by more than max(2 x pooled SD of that link's 6 runs, 1 point); only drops count. Every link also reports the paired one-sided McNemar test on per-sample hits (majority of 3 seeds, the 9 seed pairs listed). Joint is also reported, not gated, against the P0b baseline (dense_plain GENERAL, 3 seeds), so the drift the chain allows is visible. The report states that joint's best.pt is chosen on pooled val and that its PMechDB inputs are standardized with pooled statistics; both are part of the joint effect. Val selects, and each test part is read once, for the selected configuration.
   - Files: data/models/ (outputs), docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: No unit test beyond P0b's compare tests (training runs); each run.json records the split map, the flags, the seed and sample_version 19, and every link's report carries its tolerance, means, pooled SD and McNemar p-value.
9. [L] Ablations, 3 seeds each, as arms of joint, each decoded on closed_base val and RC val with the cohort slices: (i) own-pair channel off, reported as top-1/3/5 by R shape with homolysis and the break-only steps first (1,096 of the 5,088 raw RS/RC lines, 21.5%, are break-only), plus closed_base's break-anchor samples; (ii) g vs g2 vs both, the two arms other than P7's default; (iii) atom input none / categorical u / SOMO population, the two arms other than the default; (iv) the metal-d mask flag flipped, run only if P4 kept the Cu/Fe cohort in training (otherwise recorded as moot), reported on the open_shell_metal cohort as counts and on closed_base val; (v) P2b's FD f+/f- frontier variant; (vi) without RS; (vii) somo_channels=False; (viii) --closed-minus1-filter, decode-only, on the 3 joint and the 3 pm_control checkpoints, with no training; (ix) P0a's exact-degeneracy frontier blocks against the 0.05 eV window (resolution 6); (x) max_candidates 48 against a larger cap, decode-only on the 3 joint checkpoints (P0a deferred it here); (xi) each threshold flag you adopt after P0a's reach report (resolution 7), 3 seeds each. For reference only, 1 seed each: rad_only (rmechdb only) and rad_given (flower_given, for the RMechRP comparison only). Default rule, recorded per flag in decisions.md with its numbers: a rule (a mask or filter) is on by default only if the paired test shows it better (one-sided p < 0.05 on the cohort it targets) with no link-style drop on closed_base val. A physically motivated input (g2, SOMO population, own-pair channel, FD f+/f-, open-shell pair channel) stays in the default when it is better or non-inferior within max(2 x pooled SD of the arm's 6 runs, 1 point); otherwise it is dropped. Where (U18: rmechdb artifacts are treated like PMechDB): on a rented pod. The v19 stores (pmechdb and rmechdb samples, tokens, bond_index) are pushed to the private rhoMech-data first and pulled with its parallel partial clones (2.5 MB/s vs 0.4 MB/s for direct rsync, memory runpod-pod-access), and checkpoints go back to rhoMech-data, never to the public repo. Fallback: the RX 6600 XT (HSA_OVERRIDE_GFX_VERSION=10.3.0), one run at a time (16 GB host RAM).
   - Files: data/models/ (outputs), docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: Each arm's run.json differs from joint's only in its own flag (checked by compare before the comparison).
10. [S] Dated decisions.md entries: the screen as bookkeeping, and the electron-transfer exclusion it rests on; the -1 filter default; each ablation's default with its numbers; rmechdb's decoder caps; the U20 gate outcome per link; where the runs ran and where the weights are (U18). CLAUDE.md: the training and eval commands (--split map, --open-shell, --closed-minus1-filter, `ksmech-eval compare`).
   - Files: docs/decisions.md, CLAUDE.md
   - Tests: None (documentation).

**Data effect.** None on samples (v19 from P8). Training and decode only. Decode writes per-sample hit files (since P0).

**Rebuilds and bumps.** New checkpoints only. Existing runs still load, with the 3-class head and legacy inputs by default. DECODE_VERSION 3 -> 4. No SAMPLE_VERSION, token or QM bump. Measured base for the GPU estimate: dense_plain (PMechDB, 9,994 train / 1,239 val, 30 epochs) took 45.1 min on a pod (bf16, 2026-10-26, sum of metrics.jsonl epoch seconds). On the local RX 6600 XT (fp16, ROCm also reports 'cuda'), the sparse pmechdb+reactmech run (17,183 train) took 309.5 min and reactmech (7,189) took 163 min (2026-10-28). Per sample that is about 4.5x slower than the pod's sparse PMechDB run (39.9 min). No dense run has been timed locally. Estimates: the joint training set is about 14.0-14.2k samples (PMechDB about 10.1k after Release A + rmechdb about 3,900-4,100 training steps: RC train 1,077 and about 3,114 RS after LEAK_GUARD 74-81, P8's estimate), so a joint run takes about 55-75 min on a pod or 4-5 h locally. There are 36 training runs for the revisers' arms (resolution 19; 38 with pm_v19 at 3 seeds, 33 if the metal-d arm is moot): pm_control 3, pm_v19 1, joint 3, 27 ablation runs, rad_only 1, rad_given 1, about 28-43 GPU-hours on a pod or about 6-7 days of continuous GPU time locally (estimate). Resolution 6's frontier-blocks arm adds 3 runs (39, about 30-47 GPU-hours or about 6.5-7.5 days locally, estimate), and each threshold flag you adopt after P0a's reach report adds 3 more. The old plan had 14 runs and 8-12 GPU-hours. Decode takes about 20 CPU-hours at flex_first's measured 1.18 s per sample, about 27 min per checkpoint on closed_base val + RC val (estimate; dense with 5 atom classes may be slower), plus the decode-only max_candidates arm, run as parallel processes within the host's 15-process limit. Weights go to the private rhoMech-data and pods (U18), never to the public repo.

**Exit criteria.** Both U20 links pass on closed_base val full-R top-1 (one-sided, max(2 x pooled SD of the link's 6 runs, 1 point)) with their paired McNemar results beside them; or the failing link is explained in decisions.md with pm_v19 at 3 seeds. Joint is reported against the P0b baseline. Every ablation flag has a default in decisions.md, justified by its 3-seed paired comparison. The share of closed_base val top-1 completions with odd atom entries is reported with the screen alone and with the -1 filter. RC test full-R top-1/3/5 is read once, for the selected configuration over its 3 seeds, with 95% Wilson intervals (about +-8 points at 60% on 136), by R shape, next to the floor and the radical suite. closed_base and RC test are also reported without the cross-source overlapping samples, with the cohort slices (repaired, open_shell_spectator, carbene_singlet, open_shell_metal; groups under 30 as counts). pytest green, both summary lines pasted (`-m "not realdata"` and `-m realdata`).

**Risks.** Without the pool-level mask in the loss, the model can put probability on -1 entries in polar pools. The always-on screen and the -1 filter act at decode, and the filter's default comes from step 9, so this is measured, not assumed. A closed-shell pool's homolysis is legitimate and passes the screen; on polar PMechDB pools such completions count as misses, and their share is reported. 3-seed SDs have 2 degrees of freedom (95% interval 0.52s to 6.3s), hence the pooled 6-run SD with a 1-point floor. RC val (134, binomial SE about 4.2 points at 60%) and RC test (136) are small, so seeds measure run-to-run spread, not more precision. The run count is about 2.6x the old plan, so a local fallback ties up the GPU for about a week (estimate). The functions-under-60-lines rule is tight in decode.py. Flex and sparse need parity for the new channels; they are tested but not trained in P9. The per-sample hits exist since P0 and compare since P0b.

### S1. Serve 1: Workbench orbital view of radicals and Cu(II)/Fe(III) (no prediction)

**Depends on:** P4

**Goal.** Let the Workbench compute and show organic radicals and the rule-assigned metals behind a 'radicals' capability at level 'workbench', while pools keep refusing radicals. It needs only P4 steps 1-3 (data.spin, qm.spin_for_qm, the tagged records and cache-key sites), not P4's Release-A training. The engine never imports serve.

**Steps.**

1. [S] Precondition carried from P2b, not work of S1. Serve's tests (tests/test_serve_*.py in the `-m "not realdata"` run) are part of every engine phase exit from P2b on. P2b edits serve/chem/loader.py and serve/workbench/orbital_sets.py (load_tokens_for). P4 rewires GFN2Calculator.run, which serve's MetalGFN2Calculator inherits (serve/chem/metal_qm.py:96-97: '`run` itself is inherited'). P7 changes serve's physics_fingerprint. Serve's behaviour stays unchanged until S1 only because its preflight refuses every species where the rule differs from RDKit. S1 starts from a green serve suite and does not repair breakage from engine phases.
   - Files: src/ksmech/serve/chem/metal_qm.py:96-97, tests/test_serve_*.py
   - Tests: The serve suite (tests/test_serve_*.py in the `-m "not realdata"` run) is green at S1's start, with its summary line pasted; S1 makes no change to repair engine-phase breakage.
2. [M] The 'radicals' capability with levels is an API schema change. Today a capability is boolean: CapabilityOut has enabled: bool and reason (serve/core/schemas.py:195-197), and capabilities.py holds {name: (enabled, reason)}. CapabilityOut gains level: str | None, which is None for on/off capabilities and 'workbench' | 'pools' | 'predict' for radicals (off is enabled False). API_SCHEMA goes 1 -> 2 (serve/__init__.py:5). `ksmech-serve schema-ts` regenerates apps/ui/src/api/types.gen.ts. Every reader of /api/meta is updated: the UI (api/meta.ts, components/CapabilityNotice.tsx, state/store.ts, App.tsx), the client (client/commands.py, client.py), and the MCP server, whose enabled() reads only 'enabled' and counts an unlisted capability as on (mcp/server.py:8-9, 62-81). The MCP server must therefore read the level, so predict_mechanism keeps refusing radical pools until 'predict'. The public and lab profiles admit 'radicals' like 'workbench'.
   - Files: src/ksmech/serve/core/schemas.py:195-201, src/ksmech/serve/core/capabilities.py, src/ksmech/serve/__init__.py:5, apps/ui/src/api/types.gen.ts, apps/ui/src/api/meta.ts, apps/ui/src/components/CapabilityNotice.tsx, apps/ui/src/state/store.ts, src/ksmech/client/commands.py, src/ksmech/mcp/server.py:62-81, docs/mcp.md
   - Tests: /api/meta reports radicals at level 'workbench' with API_SCHEMA 2. The MCP server's tool list shows the level, and its predict tool refuses a radical pool. `ksmech meta --json` shows the level.
3. [M] Spin entry point and the composed method tag. Serve switches from resolve_charge_spin to data.spin and qm.spin_for_qm in featurize.py:149-178, pools.py:78-90 (which still refuses until S2) and synthetic.py:75-79. Composition, decided here: serve's method for a key is the engine's qm.method_for(key, base) first ('+spin-v1' only where data.spin differs from RDKit), then serve's metal_geometry.method_for ('+metal-geom-v1' for a transition-metal key). Cu(II)/Fe(III) keys therefore read '<GFN2 method>+spin-v1+metal-geom-v1', Cl[C]Cl reads '<GFN2 method>+spin-v1', and [CH3] keeps the base method. metal_method's endswith check (metal_geometry.py:139-141) still holds. One serve function composes the tag. qm_npz_path, KeyedMethodLoader (featurize.py:90-97, 115-128), serve/chem/loader.py:116 and the probe-cache CLI (serve/app/cli.py:463, which uses QMConfig().method today) all go through it, so serve never reads the old untagged triplet Cl[C]Cl or [C]=O records in the shared cache. The metal preflight calls data.spin for Cu(II)/Fe(III) and keeps refusing everything else. metal_qm's FERMI_SMEARED uses localize.is_fermi_smeared; today metal_qm.py:135-137 flags every occupation between FERMI_TOL and 2 - FERMI_TOL, so every SOMO.
   - Files: src/ksmech/serve/chem/featurize.py:90-128, 146-189, src/ksmech/serve/chem/pools.py:78-90, src/ksmech/serve/chem/synthetic.py:75-79, src/ksmech/serve/chem/metal_geometry.py:139-157, 398-446, src/ksmech/serve/chem/metal_qm.py:133-138, src/ksmech/serve/chem/loader.py:116, src/ksmech/serve/app/cli.py:463
   - Tests: test_serve_keys.py:182-190, 291-294 and tests/conftest.py:86-97 are updated to the new entry point. A carbene and CuCl2 resolve under the composed tag. A grep test checks that every serve cache-path builder goes through the one composing function.
4. [S] The metal records are not the engine's. Serve's Cu/Fe records use template geometry ('+metal-geom-v1'), while P4's engine records use ETKDG/L-BFGS geometry, so the Workbench can show tokens the model never trained on. Today this already holds for every transition metal in serve, because method_for tags every metal key. Decision: the Workbench and Predict both keep serve's own template-geometry record for every transition-metal key, as today, so S1 does not change Predict. The Workbench banner and the S1 decisions.md entry state the mismatch. A read-only diagnostic (scripts/probes/metal_geometry_vs_engine.py) compares serve's and the engine's record for every Cu/Fe species that has both: metal-ligand distances, Mulliken charge on the metal and its ligand atoms, SOMO count and occupation pattern. It writes one JSON under data/serve/, and its numbers go into the decisions.md entry and, in S3, the model card.
   - Files: scripts/probes/metal_geometry_vs_engine.py (new, read-only), docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: The diagnostic runs read-only on the Cu/Fe species that have both records and writes its JSON; no engine or serve record changes.
5. [M] Open-shell synthetic EHT: u SOMOs at 1.0, P = C diag(n) C^T, rec.unpaired = u, and a parity check on n_val - u, so e2e can cover radicals.
   - Files: src/ksmech/serve/chem/synthetic.py:79-83, 135-207
   - Tests: Unit: a synthetic [CH3] record has one SOMO at 1.0, rec.unpaired 1 and passes the parity check; a parity mismatch raises.
6. [M] Workbench. SOMO labels in orbital sets, using P2b's open group (equal GFN2 occupations, no 0.05 eV window). A spin-density kind described as the restricted SOMO density. The frontier panel shows P2b's default convention (f+ = f-) and, where the record has them, the FD f+/f- values beside it. ORBITAL_SET_VERSION and DENSITY_VERSION 1 -> 2; both are in the cache keys (orbital_sets.py:107-128, densities.py:154), so every cached Workbench orbital set and density cube is recomputed on demand, closed shells included. UI: Compute is unblocked with an information banner ('restricted open shell, SOMO at occupation 1'; for a metal key also 'serve template geometry, not the training record'). The MoleculeSceneView SOMO colour is fixed: today occupation > 1 draws a SOMO as virtual.
   - Files: src/ksmech/serve/workbench/orbital_sets.py:47, 248-260, src/ksmech/serve/workbench/densities.py:53-54, 185-199, apps/ui/src/features/workbench/WorkbenchPage.tsx:8, 152-165, 209-213, apps/ui/src/features/workbench/sketch/InfoBar.tsx:81, packages/viz/src/scene/MoleculeSceneView.tsx:51, apps/ui/e2e/workbench.spec.ts:368-387
   - Tests: The workbench refusal assertions (test_serve_molecule.py:101, test_serve_workbench_api.py:171, test_serve_metal_geometry.py:380-389) become acceptance tests. The pool refusals stay. e2e passes.
7. [S] Docs. A dated decisions.md entry: the capability levels and the API_SCHEMA bump, the tag composition, the geometry mismatch with the diagnostic's numbers, and the banner text. A wiki page on the restricted open-shell view. docs/serve_architecture.md if a module moves.
   - Files: docs/decisions.md, content/wiki/, docs/serve_architecture.md
   - Tests: None (documentation); the new wiki page renders through GET /api/wiki in the serve tests.
8. [S] Merge. Only while no build, QM batch or training runs from main, because tracked edits mark that run's artifacts dirty, and with the ksmech-serve instance that runs from main stopped. Then start it again, run `ksmech-serve probe-cache` on the open-shell fixtures and the e2e suite against it.
   - Files: 
   - Tests: probe-cache exits 0 on the open-shell fixtures, and the e2e suite is green against the restarted instance.

**Data effect.** None on training data. New serve caches for radical and Cu/Fe species. All cached Workbench orbital sets and density cubes are recomputed on demand after the version bumps.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** ORBITAL_SET_VERSION 2, DENSITY_VERSION 2, API_SCHEMA 2; types.gen.ts regenerated. QM for a handful of serve species (the fixtures plus whatever users compute), under the composed tags.

**Exit criteria.** The Workbench computes [CH3], TEMPO, Cl[C]Cl (singlet), CuCl2 and FeBr4- in real mode. /api/meta shows radicals at level 'workbench', and pools still refuse radicals with RADICAL_NOT_SUPPORTED. The metal diagnostic is recorded. e2e green. test_serve_layers and the engine-boundary test pass. pytest green, both summary lines pasted. The serve instance was stopped for the merge and started again from main.

**Risks.** A restricted spin density is only the SOMO density, and the page must say so. Diradicals are computed as data.spin assigns them. The Workbench shows template-geometry tokens for metals, which can differ from the training records; the banner and the diagnostic make this visible but do not remove it. A new SPIN_RULE_VERSION in the engine means a new '+spin-vN' tag, so serve's composed keys follow it. The version bumps cost a recompute of every cached Workbench view.

### S2. Serve 2: radical pools, step checks and fishhook readings in Correct

**Depends on:** P8, S1

**Goal.** Let users build radical pools and check or correct one-electron steps, with readings in electron units. Every predictor refuses radical pools explicitly, with a reason, until S3.

**Steps.**

1. [S] Lift the pool refusals; the capability level becomes 'pools'. Species and pool keys follow key v2 through species_key_of (serve/core/keys.py:55-64), with u from the non-bonding count N where the drawing is known and RDKit only for bare SMILES (U12). S2 does no migration of its own. The stored states with u > 0 species were purged or re-keyed at the P6/P8 merge: about 362 of today's 1,388 states, counted 2026-10-28 under an approximation of the P4 rule ([O] 68, [OH+] 53, [CH2] 51, ...). S2 checks the result. Every stored state loads. Closed-shell (u = 0) pool ids are unchanged. No stored state holds a v1 key for a species with u > 0, which matters because PoolState.from_dict (contracts.py:122-131) trusts the stored pool_key and pool_id without a version check. The browse index was rebuilt in P8 (browse INDEX_VERSION 3).
   - Files: src/ksmech/serve/chem/pools.py:58-91, src/ksmech/serve/core/keys.py:34, 55-64, src/ksmech/serve/core/contracts.py:122-131, src/ksmech/serve/core/capabilities.py
   - Tests: A radical pool is created and analyzed in synthetic mode. A scan of data/serve/pools in a test fixture finds no v1-keyed open-shell state. Closed-shell pool ids resolve unchanged.
2. [M] Predictors refuse radical pools explicitly. ### S3. Serve 3: radical prediction

**Depends on:** P9, S2

**Goal.** Predict radical steps with an open-shell checkpoint that passed P9's gate, show them as fishhooks, and expose them only as U18 allows.

**Steps.**

1. [M] The network predictor uses open_shell checkpoints, including joint ones (NAME_RE with '+' from P9). It decodes with P9's settings: the closed-shell screen always on, the --closed-minus1-filter default recorded in P9, and the decoder caps of the checkpoint's sources (the maximum over them, since a serve pool has no source), all recorded in the edge's predictor version. 3-class checkpoints keep refusing radical pools with OPEN_SHELL_POOL. The floor lifts its refusal and proposes homolysis candidates (bond -1, +1/+1), scored by the own-pair channel as the engine floor does after P7 (FLOOR_VERSION from P7), beside _heterolysis. gbdt keeps refusing radical pools: its model has closed-shell features only, and a radical GBDT needs D1's alignment labels (LGBM_VERSION 2). The capability level becomes 'predict' only when an open-shell checkpoint that passed P9's U20 gate is present; otherwise it stays 'pools', with the reason. The default checkpoint for closed-shell pools changes only by a dated decisions.md entry after P9's gate.

A radical chain in serve therefore stops at max_steps (network.py:222, settings.py:65), a turnover, a recurring pool or an empty frontier. NOTE_NETWORK_STOP (serve/runtime/context.py:56-60) is updated to say this also holds for open-shell checkpoints. A learned stop waits for D3.

The public profile, which admits predict (capabilities.py:80-85), serves them only on a non-commercial deployment. RMechDB and FlowER are cited on the wiki page and in GET /api/models. ### D1. Deferred: official RMechDB arrows, classes and canonical split (trigger: you request and download the CSV)

The replay at ingest replaces P0's RADICAL_ARROW skip; drop test_radical_arrows_are_not_implemented.

3. [L] The rmechdb loader reads the CSV (partial maps 10/20, as pmechdb.py does) and joins it to the FlowER lines by map-free step id, computed after the [Ar]/[He] strip, with the RC copy winning collisions, as P8 does. It adds the 7-class and 3-class labels as diagnostics, the spectators, and the official split (core 1,512/150, specific 3,397/367) as a view, official_core. The official subset replaces the inferred flower_suffix meaning. It counts arrow-vs-map disagreements, including any R that the arrows give and P6's per-source label rule does not. Then: the radical GBDT with alignment labels (LGBM_VERSION 2); the RMechRP-exact table, using P9's selected configuration retrained on official_core once P9 exists; and the fishhook alignment rate.

FlowER RS/RC on disk hold 0 SET steps and 1 identity line, which P6 codes NULL_STEP (U13; the 2026-10-28 scan also finds 1 line with no bond change). The official CSV has arrows, so it can hold SET steps that FlowER dropped. Count SET_STEP, P6's dissociative-ET code and NULL_STEP by subset (core, specific), by FlowER match and by arrow pattern. The counts go into decisions.md. A SET anchor stays out of scope until you decide on these counts ('Not doing': revisit only if a source makes them significant).
