The fishhook alignment rate is reported beside PMechDB's two-electron arrow alignment rate on the same code path: 0.970 today, 758 of 25,213 arrows unaligned (data/processed/pmechdb/alignment_report.json). Unaligned fishhooks are grouped by reason, as alignment_report does. If the fishhook rate's 95% interval lies below PMechDB's rate, a review of P7's hole-token design opens with those groups as evidence; the data are not revisited. SET_STEP, dissociative-ET and NULL_STEP counts are recorded. pytest green, both summary lines pasted.

**Risks.** The half-arrow syntax is unknown until the file is read. The ND terms may restrict derivatives; the U18 reading is not legal advice. The official split may not match FlowER's frozen rc_molecule split, so the official view is added beside it and never replaces it.

### D2. Planned ablation after P9: spin-polarized GFN2 and level refinements for open shells

**Depends on:** P9

**Goal.** Measure whether replacing the restricted shortcuts for u > 0 species helps, without recomputing any closed-shell record, and adopt a refinement only on a paired per-sample test or, when physically motivated, on non-inferiority. This is a planned phase after P9, no longer triggered by the P7 suite, the P2 metal flags or P9's SOMO ablation.

**Steps.**

1. [L] spGFN2 companion records under a per-molecule '+spinpol-v1' tag, for u > 0 species only. There are two arms: the organic radicals (2,387 unique RDKit u = 1 fragments in the raw RS/RC lines, species_scan.py 2026-10-28, plus the open-shell species of P3/P4/P5) and, separately, the Cu(II)/Fe(III) species, only if P4 kept the metal cohort. A two-channel _assemble (eps.ndim == 2) and optional beta fields with defaults keep QMRecord SCHEMA_VERSION at 1. Split levels: the alpha SOMO for the donor token and the beta LUMO for the hole. An optional spin_population atom input. The xtb-binary path raises SPINPOL_UNSUPPORTED.
   - Files: src/ksmech/qm/geometry.py:30-46, 141-166, src/ksmech/qm/xtb_calc.py:196-250, src/ksmech/qm/records.py:24, 66-104, 181-195
   - Tests: Unit: a two-channel _assemble round-trips a spGFN2 [CH3] record at QMRecord SCHEMA_VERSION 1 with default beta fields; the xtb-binary path raises SPINPOL_UNSUPPORTED; closed-shell keys are unchanged.
2. [M] Delta-SCF SOMO levels from the N+-1 single points already run for FD Fukui (their energies are discarded today), stored in a sidecar. Open-shell Mayer Wiberg: 2*sum[(S^1/2 P_a S^1/2)^2 + (S^1/2 P_b S^1/2)^2], identical for closed shells and 0.5 instead of 0.25 for a one-electron bond; BOND_INDEX_VERSION 2 if adopted.
   - Files: src/ksmech/qm/somo_levels.py (new), src/ksmech/qm/xtb_calc.py:109-120, src/ksmech/qm/bond_index.py:33-56
   - Tests: Unit: the open-shell Mayer Wiberg equals the closed-shell value on the fixed molecules and gives 0.5 on a one-electron bond fixture; delta-SCF levels are read from the stored N+-1 energies.
3. [M] Ablation runs and the adoption rule. Each refinement is a 3-seed arm of P9's selected joint configuration, decoded on RC val, closed_base val and the open-shell cohorts, and compared with `ksmech-eval compare` (P0b). Closed-shell records and tokens are not recomputed, so closed_base inputs are bitwise unchanged, and any closed_base difference comes from training. Adoption has two routes. A refinement is adopted if the one-sided paired McNemar test on majority-of-3 hits shows it better on RC val (p < 0.05), with no drop on closed_base val beyond max(2 x pooled SD of the 6 runs, 1 point). When the refinement is physically motivated (spGFN2 is the formally appropriate open-shell treatment; delta-SCF levels are the physical removal and addition energies), it is also adopted if it is non-inferior on RC val and closed_base val within that same one-sided margin. RC val is small (134, binomial SE about 4.2 points at 60%), so a null result is weak evidence, and the reference checks below may carry more weight.
   - Files: data/models/ (outputs), docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: The adoption rule is unit-tested on synthetic hit vectors (better, non-inferior, worse).
4. [M] Reference checks (U22), only here and only with your approval at D2's start; scratch cache only, never trained on. Each download goes into a new, empty directory and is read with python -I. (1) GMTKN55 RSE43: 43 radical stabilization energies of carbon-centred radicals, a total-energy benchmark. It checks the energetics of restricted vs spGFN2, not tokens or levels; ksmech uses no total energy as input. (2) St. John et al. 2020 (Sci. Data 7, 244): 246,363 radicals, C/H/N/O only, at M06-2X/def2-TZVP, with Mulliken charges and spin densities, distributed as one SDF plus zipped Gaussian logs (figshare collection 4944855). It cannot be fetched in part: the whole SDF (size unverified) is downloaded, then at most 300 radicals are subsampled. The data-file license is checked before download; the article states CC0 only for the metadata. It is the only external test of the restricted-SOMO-density assumption and says nothing about Br., Cl., S, Sn or the metals. (3) Cited gas-phase IP/EA values beyond the dozen P2 already uses, compared only with gas-phase delta-SCF levels (step 2), never with ALPB-water eigenvalues.
   - Files: scripts/probes/, docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: None (scratch probes, after your approval); each download sits in its own new directory and is read with python -I.
5. [S] Adoption, if any. Spec and decisions.md entries, the '+spinpol-v1' tag documented in data_schema.md, the tokens of the affected molecules rebuilt, then samples at SAMPLE_VERSION 19 -> 20. With no adoption: no version bump, and the ablation table and reference results are recorded.
   - Files: docs/ksmech_architecture.tex, docs/decisions.md, docs/data_schema.md, src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py:92 (SAMPLE_VERSION)
   - Tests: If a refinement is adopted: the realdata store tests pass at SAMPLE_VERSION 20 and the closed_base digests are unchanged. If none is: no version bump.

**Data effect.** Tagged records and sidecars for open-shell species only (about 2,400 organic u = 1 species from RS/RC, the P3/P4/P5 open-shell species, and the Cu/Fe species if kept). Closed-shell records are never recomputed. Samples change only if a refinement is adopted.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** Tagged records for u > 0 only, at about 1.25-1.7x the cost of a restricted run (estimate; P2's Cu/Fe probe measures the actual ratio on its species). Tokens of those molecules rebuilt. SAMPLE_VERSION 20 and BOND_INDEX_VERSION 2 only if adopted. Each run costs about 1 GPU-hour on a pod, 3 per arm (estimate from P9's joint runs).

**Exit criteria.** An ablation table with 3 seeds per arm, paired tests and non-inferiority margins. Each adoption or rejection recorded in decisions.md, and in the spec if adopted. Reference-check results recorded if you approved the downloads. pytest green, both summary lines pasted.

**Risks.** spGFN2 transferability is limited (Moradi 2024). Two-channel localization (IBO with a pivoted-Cholesky start, the code default per P0's doc fix) can choose different Kekule structures in the alpha and beta channels. Delta-SCF levels mix relaxation and solvation into the radicals' levels only. RC val has little power. The St. John download size is unknown and disk is tight.

### D3. Deferred: radical chains from FlowER's USPTO NBS sequences and a trained END_MECH stop head (trigger: the FlowER full-ingest plan)

**Depends on:** P9

**Goal.** Multi-step radical pathways (initiation, propagation, termination) and the first trained END_MECH stop head. Today mechanism length in serve comes from hand-written stops; D3's chains are where stop labels can come from.

**Steps.**

1. [S] The state of END_MECH, measured on the code. The head is never trained. lambda_stop is 0 on PMechDB and 0.5 by default elsewhere (loop.py:81-85), but no source builds stop labels: `stop_label` has no producer anywhere in src, and only model/loss.py reads it. loss.stop_bce (loss.py:68-78) returns None without labels, so even reactmech/sparse_resonance, which ran at lambda_stop 0.5, had no stop term. Serve does not read END_MECH (network_predictor.py:22). It stops at max_steps (8 in network.py:222, 12 in settings.py:65), a turnover, a recurring pool or an empty frontier (NOTE_NO_STOP and NOTE_NETWORK_STOP, serve/runtime/context.py:52-60).
   - Files: src/ksmech/train/loop.py:81-107, src/ksmech/model/loss.py:68-78, src/ksmech/serve/runtime/context.py:52-60
   - Tests: None (a code reading, recorded in decisions.md).
2. [L] A flower loader restricted to integer-suffix sequences that carry a metal-free radical fragment (Wohl-Ziegler NBS with BPO/AIBN). A grep lower bound gives 1,231 train steps in 409 sequences, 128/42 test and 27/9 val. FlowER's numeric-suffix set is about 2.14M lines, not the 1.4M in CLAUDE.md (U17). It needs a FlowER repair for neutral 4-bonded P on Pd. These sequences are FlowER's USPTO-derived set (inferred from the suffix scheme, P8's data_schema.md), not RMechDB lines; their license reading is recorded at D3's start, with FlowER cited.
   - Files: src/ksmech/data/flower.py (new), src/ksmech/data/domain.py, docs/data_schema.md
   - Tests: Unit on hand-written integer-suffix lines (never real FlowER lines): a metal-free radical sequence is kept with its pathway id, and the P-on-Pd repair is counted.
3. [M] Stop labels and the stop weight. The sample builder writes stop_label from pathway ids: 1 at a sequence's last step, 0 before it, and -1 (unknown) for isolated-step sources. The run's lambda_stop becomes the configured value (0.5) whenever any source in the run builds stop labels. This replaces the minimum over sources (loop.py:102-107), which would set it to 0 in every joint run that includes PMechDB or rmechdb. stop_bce already masks samples without a label, so the isolated-step sources contribute nothing to the stop term.
   - Files: src/ksmech/arrows/steps.py (stop_label), src/ksmech/train/loop.py:85, 102-107, src/ksmech/model/loss.py:68-78
   - Tests: A batch mixing pathway and isolated samples has a stop term only from the pathway samples. A joint run with flower and pmechdb logs lambda_stop 0.5.
4. [M] Evaluation and serve. Pathway-level recovery on held-out sequences, and the stop head's calibration: P(END_MECH) at true last steps vs earlier steps. Serve reads END_MECH only for checkpoints whose run.json shows a stop term trained on labels. NOTE_NETWORK_STOP is updated for them, and the hand-written stops stay as limits.
   - Files: src/ksmech/eval/cli.py, src/ksmech/serve/predict/network_predictor.py:22, src/ksmech/serve/runtime/context.py:56-60, docs/decisions.md
   - Tests: Unit: the calibration report on a synthetic pathway set; serve reads END_MECH only for a checkpoint whose run.json shows a stop term trained on labels.

**Data effect.** About 1-2.5k template-generated chain steps (the grep is a lower bound; about 2x is inferred). The new stop_label column is filled only for sources with pathway ids.

**Rebuilds and bumps.** A new source: ingest, QM, tokens and samples. The stop_label column needs a SAMPLE_VERSION bump at D3's build (the next free number: 20, or 21 if D2 adopted a refinement). No change to the other sources' labels.

**Exit criteria.** Pathway-level recovery and stop-head calibration reported on held-out sequences. A joint run trains the stop term on the pathway samples only. pytest green, both summary lines pasted.

**Risks.** The labels are template-generated, and the same files hold thousands of artifact radicals (Pd-[P], [Na], Cp). A stop head trained only on NBS chains may not transfer to polar mechanisms, so serve reads it only for checkpoints that trained it.

## 4. Spec changes

| Version | Phase | Where | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| v9.1 | P0a | new paragraph in the design principles, beside 'no chemist's taxonomy as model input' | Governing principle (v9.1, P0a). Every rule that remains in the model path is one of three things: a physical law or exact bookkeeping (electron and charge conservation, non-negative electron counts); a QM-derived input the model weighs; or an option behind a flag whose default is justified by an ablation recorded in docs/decisions.md. Hand-set lists, element lists, percentiles and go/no-go thresholds are replaced by one of the three. Data curation (repairs of drawing errors, exclusions) stays, always counted and reported, and is applied by one law per phenomenon, never gated by element or parity. Diagnostics (orbital types, alignment sets) are not rules of the model path. (origin: new (P0a)) |
| v9.1 | P0a | tex:618 (valid pairs and pooling), the sample-store and pair-feature text; tokens.py:179-186 and 289-303, store.py:11 and 72-74 | Pair cells kept, rules as flag channels (v9.1, P0a). The sample store keeps every (occupied, virtual) cell. The d-type acceptor rule (VALENCE_D and argmax d: 10.4% of virtual tokens; more than half of the gold anchor's pooled weight removed in 1,520 of 9,997 PMechDB training steps, 15.2%), the donor's own antibond and the site rule (site_weight 0.20, 1.3 r_cov: 1.6% of cells, the arrow's own orbital pair in 237 training and 34 val steps) become pair-track flag channels instead of deletions; the d share is already an orbital input. A ModelConfig switch chooses whether G^atom and y_ij pool only the valid cells or all cells; its default is today's valid-only pooling, so the floor and old checkpoints reproduce, and P0b's GENERAL configuration turns it on. Pair cells grow by about 12% (pair_valid is about 89% of cells) and the stores by about 11-12% (estimate). (origin: new (P0a, audit code items 1-2)) |
| v9.1 | P0a | tex:403 and tex:414 (spectral set); descriptors.py:34-36 and 66 | Spectral truncation as an input (v9.1, P0a). The spectral set keeps entries of weight >= 0.01, at most 8, renormalized, which retains on average 0.77 of an occupied and 0.83 of a virtual token's weight. retained_fraction (the kept weight before renormalization) and the LMO energy E_r become orbital inputs behind a flag. tex:403 already lists the retained fraction with the set while the code stores it as a diagnostic (model_input=False); the sentence 'The scalar E_r is a diagnostic' changes, and CLAUDE.md's input list follows. (origin: new (P0a, audit code item 5)) |
| v9.1 | P0a | Atom tokens (frontier weights, frozen and finite-difference Fukui), tex:386; frontier.py:19; tokens.py:105-150 | Frontier blocks and Fukui columns (v9.1, P0a). Every token file gains a second set of frontier weights (HOMO-1, HOMO, LUMO, LUMO+1) and frozen-orbital f+/f- whose degenerate blocks group only levels degenerate to numerical tolerance (1e-4 eV), beside the legacy columns built with the 0.05 eV window (DEGENERACY_EV), which merges levels split by more than 1e-4 eV in 3,140 of 14,233 PMechDB records (22%). This is token SCHEMA_VERSION 9 -> 10, additive, and the plan's only token schema bump. The finite-difference f+ and f- (rec.fukui_plus/minus, already in every token file as diagnostics) are stored in the v18 store as unselected atom inputs. ModelConfig selects the frontier blocks (legacy default) and, from P2b, atom_fukui (frozen default); both are P9 ablations. (origin: new (consistency pass; resolutions 5 and 6)) |
| v9.1 | P0a | 'Edit labels' (tex:660) and the arrowless ReactMech route (mapped_matrix, resonance.py) | Kekule-closest product before the mapped difference (v9.1, P0a). For a source without arrows, the product is redrawn in the Kekule form closest to the reactant before mapped_matrix takes the mapped difference, so a Kekule swap of a ring never enters R. Today the two mapped sides are kekulized independently: 759 of 8,097 ReactMech train+val labels (9.4%) carry an alternating cycle in every variant, and RESONANCE_CAP is hit on 41% of ReactMech steps. ReactMech labels are re-made in P0a's re-ingest. (origin: new (P0a, audit code item 3)) |
| v9.1 | P0a | sec:decoder and the evaluation section; decode.py:57-59, the eval CLI | Per-source decoder caps (v9.1, P0a). The fixed caps max_bonds 8, max_atoms 2 and max_candidates 48 are replaced by caps derived per source from its label maxima, recorded in the run and exposed as eval CLI flags; 212 ReactMech training and 13 val labels exceed max_bonds today, mostly through the Kekule swaps above. Steps beyond a cap stay in the denominator as misses. rmechdb's caps come from its own label maxima by the same rule (raw lines: 4 atom and 15 bond entries; v10, P8/P9). (origin: new (P0a, audit code item 4)) |
| v9.1 | P0a | tex:806 'Domain coverage' (metal rules) | Metal domain laws (v9.1, P0a). One onium-to-metal dative law: a metal takes -1 per uncompensated onium donor bound to it, for any element and any starting parity, applied only where the fragment's electron count ends up even, the side's net charge stays >= 0 and both sides of the step keep the same total, and checked by a 20-case oxidation-state audit in P0a; it replaces the R2/R3 element and parity gates. One parity-independent METAL_PLACEHOLDER rule: a ligand-less placeholder shape is excluded on any fragment, which removes 24 even PMechDB steps; a metal-charge clause keeps MX+ drawn with the charge on the metal (the 3 [Cl][Zn+] steps), a free-ion clause keeps free atoms and ions such as a spectator [Ag+], and an ML1 exemption keeps L1Pd(0) ([Pd-][P+]R3). One METAL_SUSPECT law, a flag and never an exclusion: an even fragment with a first-row transition metal whose oxidation-state bookkeeping leaves an open d shell (d1-d9), reported as a slice (35 ReactMech pilot steps: Cr(IV) 10, Ni(II) d8 25; 0 PMechDB). Kept steps the law changes are re-charged in P0a (the 49 ReactMech onium Pd/Ni steps, about 59 fragments, 2-4 CPU-h of QM) and listed by rule; excluded steps it returns (205 ReactMech pilot steps) are admitted at ingest and skipped by the sample builder as MISSING_TOKENS, counted, until P5 computes them. The laws apply to every source, rmechdb included. (origin: new (P0a; replaces R2/R3 gating and U5's split)) |
| v9.1 | P0a and P1 (extended in v9.2 and v10) | tex:806 'Domain coverage' | Domain coverage, tex:806 (v9.1 in P0a and P1, extended in v9.2 and v10). List the codes that remain: METAL_PLACEHOLDER (parity-independent), METAL_SUSPECT (a flag), CHARGE_UNREPAIRED, residual RADICAL_DRAWN and QM failures; from v9.2 also MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS, MULTIPLICITY_UNTABULATED, SPIN_RULE_PENDING, SOMO_NOT_INTEGRAL and P3's curated RADICAL_AMBIGUOUS and DRAWING_ERROR_UNREPAIRED; from v10 also SET_STEP, NULL_STEP, the dissociative-electron-transfer code, MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES with its counted exemptions, NONCONJUGATED_SHIFT, DUPLICATE_STEP and LEAK_GUARD, and the counted repairs NOBLE_GAS_STRIPPED, PAIRED_DRAWING (N#N, ozone) and HIGH_SPIN_DRAWING. The Release B exclusions are config flags, and ozone and other paired drawings are read through spin_table_v1, a Lewis pairing rule and a degenerate-frontier (Hund) test instead of the CO/N2/O3/O2 lists; N2 drawn [N]=[N] is a counted repair to N#N, no longer an exclusion. Repairs: V1 (H completion only for a C with 3 radical electrons and the CDI ring, tested on topology), A1 (+1 only when the same alkali atom, same map number, is on both sides), the beta-lactam N as a named per-step repair, ligand-less Sn relabelled METAL_PLACEHOLDER by P0a's placeholder law, and the dative law; all of them apply to every source, rmechdb included. Genuine radicals in polar sources are admitted by counted rules (u conserved, R even, integral SOMO), with the reviewed list as the test oracle and a counted curation file for the cases the laws pass but the reading rejects. Fermi-smeared molecules are kept as flagged samples (the text says excluded today) and reported as slices, with max\|n - aufbau\| as a distribution. The covered fraction is reported per cohort. (origin: original item 3, updated) |
| v9.1 | P0 and P0b | new Evaluation paragraph | New Evaluation paragraph (v9.1, P0 and P0b): frozen append-only splits snapshotted in P0, in files with a schema independent of SAMPLE_VERSION; the frozen closed-shell cohort closed_base (the PMechDB molecule-split step ids after P0a, pinned with per-column digests on the v18 store in P0b); gates on named columns and content hashes; the 3-seed GENERAL reference and LEGACY ablation, and the one-sided U20 tolerance (max(2 x pooled SD of 6 runs, 1 point), with the paired McNemar test beside it) that judges every link and flag; the reach of the thresholds no reviser resolved (tau_atom, the 0.2-1.8 band, the spectral-set 0.05 eV merge, SMEAR_TOL), measured in P0a and turned into flags only on the user's decision; open-shell and radical cohorts reported separately and never pooled into headline numbers. | v9.2 | P2b | Atom tokens (frontier weights, Fukui) | Atom tokens (v9.2, P2b): radical frontier convention. The finite-difference f+ and f- are an input variant behind a flag (their columns are stored since P0a), ablated in P9. The finite-difference dual is unchanged. A radical's philicity enters through the gaps to its open level. (origin: original item 7, updated) |
| v10 | P6 | tex:234-245 BE section and Appendix app:be | tex:234-245 BE section and Appendix app:be (v10, P6): B_ii counts non-bonding electrons. Charge conservation sum R_ii + 2 sum R_ij = 0 is unchanged. (origin: original item 8, kept) |
| v10 | P6 and P9 | tex:297 and tex:631-638 (alphabet, masks, decode screen) | tex:297 and tex:631-638 (v10, P6/P9): R_ii in {-2,-1,0,+1,+2}. The restriction to {-2,0,+2} for a source without an open-shell policy is a label rule applied when labels are built, never a source flag the model sees at inference. Atom masks are non-negativity laws on the non-bonding count N (-1 needs N >= 1, -2 needs N >= 2). The pool-level 'no -1 entry on a pool with no unpaired electron' rule leaves the alphabet, the head and the loss; it is an optional decode filter (--closed-minus1-filter) whose default the P9 ablation sets. The always-on closed-shell decode screen reads 'each +1 odd entry sits on some broken bond', not 'both ends of one bond', which refused 20 of 795 u = 0 FlowER lines: 18 of them pass the new screen (azo initiators such as AIBN, Cl2/Br2 + RH, concerted fragmentations), and the other 2 carry a -1, which only the optional filter refuses. | v10 | P6 | tex:676-683 Resonance | tex:676-683 Resonance (v10, P6): key v2 equals v1 for a pool with no unpaired electron and adds the per-fragment (sigma SMILES, charge, unpaired) multiset otherwise, with no '.' in the suffix. u comes from the non-bonding electron count N where the drawing gives it (samples, eval, decoded products) and from data.spin on RDKit only for bare SMILES, so closed-shell polar products such as [Br+], [OH+] or a singlet [CH2] are not keyed as open shells. A step is resonance only when the product is among the enumerated forms; otherwise it is NONCONJUGATED_SHIFT, counted. Spin changes (C=C -> .C-C.) are steps. O2 drawings are normalized, so O=O and [O][O] are one species where the spin rule says triplet; [C]=O vs [C-]#[O+] stays resonance. Radical resonance forms come from an anion proxy counted on mapped atoms; it misses 3-electron forms, which is stated (low priority). (origin: original item 11, updated) |
| v10 | P7 | Tokens (hole tokens, spin_role, SOMO population) | Tokens (v10, P7): each main-group open-shell token has a derived hole token of virtual kind, built at sample assembly. The orbital input spin_role (+1/-1/0) marks the donor and acceptor copies of a duplicated orbital, the same split as the occupied/virtual block; it is a role flag, not an m_s quantum number. The per-atom restricted SOMO population is a continuous atom input, computed from the restricted orbitals, so it does not change with the resonance drawing. The categorical unpaired count u is kept for keys and bookkeeping; the choice between none, u and the SOMO population is a P9 ablation with 3 seeds. (origin: original item 12, updated) |
| v10 | P7 | tex:161 and Appendix app:ks | tex:161 and Appendix app:ks (v10, P7): extend the Klopman-Salem derivation to open-shell fragments: the one-electron SOMO-empty pair, the three-electron filled-SOMO pair (net one-electron stabilization) and the degenerate SOMO-SOMO pair, which is first order. Treat the small restricted gaps through the two-level problem: two levels dE apart, coupled by beta, lower the bonding level by (sqrt(dE^2 + 4 beta^2) - \|dE\|)/2 = beta^2/D, with D = (\|dE\| + sqrt(dE^2 + 4 beta^2))/2, so beta^2/D tends to the second-order beta^2/\|dE\| for large gaps and to the first-order \|beta\| at degeneracy. The column g2 is G recomputed with each canonical-pair denominator dE_km replaced by sign(dE_km) D_km. This law replaces a percentile delta; beta is the Wolfsberg-Helmholz cross-centre coupling inside a molecule and one stated constant between molecules, frozen once P8 builds. (origin: original item 13, updated) |
| v10 | P7 | tex:477 and Eq. ks3 (tex:483-486) | tex:477 and Eq. ks3 tex:483-486 (v10, P7): G(r,s) = sum (n_k - n_m) p_rk p_sm / (E_k - E_m) with aufbau occupations, which reduces to the factor 2 for closed shells; 'G < 0' becomes 'G <= 0'. The legacy column g is unchanged, so closed-shell G holds to 0 ulp. G is undefined on open-row x hole-column cells, which carry the open-shell pair channel (somo_pair, somo_level_diff). A new own-pair channel carries a donor's pair with its own antibond (sigma -> sigma*, pi -> pi*) outside G^atom; the floor uses it to score break anchors, so homolysis is reachable without learning. The new column g2 (G with each denominator dE_km replaced by sign(dE_km) (\|dE_km\| + sqrt(dE_km^2 + 4 beta_rs^2))/2) is computed on all cells. Donors are O_occ plus open-shell tokens; acceptors are O_virt plus hole tokens. (origin: original item 14, updated) |
| v10 | P7 | tex:618 valid pairs (after the v9.1 change) | tex:618 valid pairs (v10, P7): donors are doubly occupied or open-shell main-group tokens; acceptors are empty or hole tokens. The d-type, own-antibond and site rules are channels since v9.1, not deletions. The one structural exclusion is 'open-row x hole-column cells within one fragment' (a fragment's SOMO with its own hole or another hole of the same fragment), not 'open-shell pairs within one fragment', which would delete radical cyclization and beta-scission cells. There is no SOMO -> degenerate-partner exclusion (C6H6.-) and no percentile cut. Fermi-smeared tokens stay neither, by the aufbau test, not the 0.2-1.8 band; the metal-d open-shell mask is a flag, ablated in P9. (origin: original item 15, updated) |

The FlowER row (tex:883, '~1.4M') becomes about 2.14M numeric-suffix lines. (origin: original item 16, updated) |

(origin: original item 17, updated) |

## 5. Evaluation

Success means open-shell data comes back with no closed-shell number moving except where P0a lists the change by rule, every rule left in the model path is a law, a QM-derived input or a flag whose default an ablation justifies, and every new number is measured against an untrained floor in its own cohort.

(1) Three references, in order, all compared on content per named column, never on header bytes (every header carries built_at and code_git_hash). P0 reproduces today's numbers on today's v17 stores before anything changes: on the PMechDB molecule split (12,418 samples; val 1,239, test 1,182) the floor (val 15.6/26.0/31.1, test 17.8/27.3/31.6, top-1/3/5), the geometry-free GBDT (val 44.1/62.0/70.8 on n 1,203, test 48.8/67.7/75.1 on n 1,120) and flex_first decode (val 63.0/82.6/85.4), and writes per-sample reference files for the floor, the stored GBDT model, flex_first and the two usable ReactMech runs (on ReactMech molecule val). P0a's exit, on the v18 store with every new flag off: the floor, the stored v17 GBDT model (a refit cannot reproduce once 21 training samples leave) and flex_first decode reproduce per sample for every step P0a did not change, the two ReactMech runs on the unchanged ReactMech samples, every rebuilt token file equals its schema-9 payload on every legacy field, and every changed or removed step is listed by rule; the refit on v18 becomes the GBDT reference. P0b pins closed_base, the PMechDB molecule-split step ids after P0a, with per-sample digests per named column on v18 (atom_categorical[:, :2], the pair-feature, flag-channel, orbital-descriptor and atom-feature columns selected by name, the FD f+/f- and exact-frontier columns included, the labels and the ids), so later phases can add columns (spin_role, the SOMO population, the hole columns, g2, the own-pair channel) without failing the gate. From P1 on, every phase exit reproduces exactly the closed_base per-column digests, the floor and flex_first's decode (P7's scratch v19 gate also the P0b checkpoints); legacy closed-shell G is identical to 0 ulp; closed-shell token payloads, sigma keys and the closed-shell part of the edit-labels report are compared by content. The GBDT is pinned at P0b and refit after each training-set change, each refit recorded as the new reference. Old checkpoints keep decoding closed_base with flags off, because ModelConfig defaults keep their heads and inputs are selected by name from a superset store.

(2) GENERAL against LEGACY. In P0b, dense_plain GENERAL (new flags on) x 3 seeds is the reference (U2) and dense_plain LEGACY (new flags off) x 3 seeds is the ablation that justifies dropping the old rules. If GENERAL is worse than LEGACY on closed_base val full-R top-1 beyond the U20 tolerance (one-sided, max(2 x pooled SD of the 6 runs, 1 point), with a paired McNemar test on per-sample hits), the rule that costs accuracy, found by one-flag runs only if needed, stays as a flagged default with the evidence in decisions.md. Old checkpoints are re-scored with flags off, and the four pod runs (dense_plain, dense_resonance, flex_resonance, sparse_resonance) only if their weights are supplied; only SHA256SUMS.pod is on disk.

(3) Every flag carries its ablation, with 3 seeds and a paired per-sample test, and its chosen default is recorded in decisions.md: the kept pair cells with the d-type, own-antibond and site channels and the all-cell pooling switch (P0b); retained_fraction and E_r as inputs (P0b); the Cu/Fe metal cohort with and without (P4); the finite-difference f+/f- against f+ = f- (P9); the exact-degeneracy frontier blocks against the 0.05 eV window (P9); the own-pair channel, scored on homolysis top-k by R shape (P9); g against g2 against both (P9); the atom input none / categorical u / SOMO population (P9); the metal-d mask (P9); the pool-level 'no -1 when u = 0' decode filter (P9); max_candidates (decode-only, P9); the RS training data (P9); and spGFN2 for u > 0 as D2 after P9, adopted only by a paired per-sample test and non-inferiority where the change is physically motivated. The thresholds no reviser resolved (tau_atom 0.15, the 0.2-1.8 fractional band, the 0.05 eV merge in the spectral set, and SMEAR_TOL 0.05 with the 0.5/1.5 closed-shell block thresholds) are measured in P0a for their reach on the v18 store (tokens, samples and gold anchors affected); each whose reach is not negligible becomes a flag with a 3-seed P9 ablation, and which ones is your decision, on the numbers.

(4) Splits never move. The split files are snapshotted in P0 with today's assignment, so old checkpoints stay evaluable without leakage. Frozen split and cohort files carry no file inputs and a schema independent of SAMPLE_VERSION, so 17 -> 18 -> 19 never refuses them. Existing steps keep their part, new groups are hash-placed, bridging steps are SPLIT_CONFLICT and counted, and the ReactMech pilot keeps its 10,000 ids. rmechdb is frozen at its first build, after the [Ar]/[He] strip, the N#N repair and the RC-wins dedup, so its ids do not move afterwards.

(5) The regression chain (U20) is one-sided on closed_base val full-R top-1; only drops count, and every link has one tolerance: max(2 x pooled SD of the link's 6 runs, 1 point), with the paired one-sided McNemar test on the per-sample hits decode writes (majority of 3 seeds) reported beside it. Release A (P4, 3 seeds, GENERAL) is compared with the P0b GENERAL mean, or the gap is explained; pm_control (PMechDB only, open-shell head) with Release A, which isolates the head; joint with pm_control. (The binomial SE of one model's top-1 on about 1,238 val samples is 1.4 points, a scale reference, not the paired noise.) A 1-seed control, pm_v19 (PMechDB only, the v19 store, the 3-class head), separates P7/P8's input changes from the head and is raised to 3 seeds if pm_control misses its tolerance. Joint is also reported against the P0b mean, so the drift the chained links allow stays visible. Multi-source runs select best.pt on pooled val and fit pair statistics on the pooled training set, and every report says so. Val is also the selection set, so every arm is slightly optimistic alike; test is read once, for the selected configuration. The share of closed_base top-1 completions with odd atom entries is reported with the always-on screen ('each +1 odd entry sits on some broken bond') alone and with the optional pool-level -1 filter. P6's dry run counts every label that any P9 mask or screen would refuse.

(6) New data is never pooled into headline numbers. The cohorts are closed_base; repaired (P1: V1, A1, the beta-lactam repair; P5: CDI and the dative law); open_shell_spectator (P3: 10 steps and 2 resonance pairs, 16 open-shell species, an end-to-end test); carbene_singlet (P4); open_shell_metal (P4: Cu 23, Fe 39, 6 resonance pairs); and radical. Slices: METAL_SUSPECT (35 ReactMech pilot steps: Cr(IV) 10, Ni(II) d8 25; 0 PMechDB); fermi_smeared with and without, per cohort (molecule 18/2/8 and scaffold 21/6/1 on v18; 24/2/8 and 27/6/1 on v17), with the max|n - aufbau| distribution; the Birch-tagged step; ReactMech with and without the 49 re-charged onium Pd/Ni steps (29 in val). Groups under 30 are reported as counts, not percentages. Every cohort carries its covered fraction per reason code (SET_STEP, NULL_STEP, the dissociative-electron-transfer code, MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES and its counted exemptions, MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS, MULTIPLICITY_UNTABULATED, SPIN_RULE_PENDING, SOMO_NOT_INTEGRAL, P3's curated codes, NONCONJUGATED_SHIFT, NOT_ELEMENTARY, DUPLICATE_STEP, LEAK_GUARD, SPLIT_CONFLICT, resonance), the counted repairs (NOBLE_GAS_STRIPPED, PAIRED_DRAWING, HIGH_SPIN_DRAWING), and a breakdown by R shape (homolysis, recombination, addition, abstraction/SH2, beta-scission, other) derived from R alone. Steps beyond a per-source decoder cap stay in the denominator as misses.

(7) The radical cohort is rmechdb rc_molecule: 5,088 FlowER RC+RS lines, 4,535 distinct reactions after the [Ar]/[He] strip (4,559 before); RC 1,077/134/136 after dedup (the RC copy wins the 83 RS/RC collisions); RS train-only behind LEAK_GUARD (74 RS steps with sha1 step ids, 81 with line-index labels; about 3,114 RS training steps). RC val (134) serves every model choice, and RC test (136) is read once for the selected configuration, over 3 seeds, with 95% Wilson intervals (about +-8 points at 60%; seeds measure the run-to-run spread, not extra precision). The 34 small-molecule RC val/test steps of 270 (no species above 3 heavy atoms), which escape the molecule-split guarantee, are a slice.

(8) Physics is measured before learning. The untrained floor stands next to every trained number in every cohort. On the radical cohort it scores break anchors through the own-pair channel (sigma -> sigma*, pi -> pi*) outside G^atom, so homolysis is no longer unreachable (1,096 break-only FlowER lines, 21.5%). The six-case radical selectivity suite and the |G| table by cell kind are published with g, with g2 and with both, and with and without the flagged cells (own antibond, site, d-type) that P0a returns to the store. The cited open-shell ratios (1.4-6.7x the strongest closed-shell |G|) were measured with excluded_pair deleting the peroxyl -0.704 and HO2 -0.818 cells; those cells come back as flagged cells, so P7's tabulation is redone on v18. The suite reports; it no longer triggers D2.

(9) Leakage is checked in both directions. rmechdb training steps that share a non-trivial species with closed_base val/test are LEAK_GUARD. PMechDB training species that appear in RC val/test, and every rmechdb test part checked against PMechDB, ReactMech and mech-USPTO, are reported as slices with and without the overlap; the O2 normalization makes O=O and [O][O] one species in these audits where the spin rule says triplet. PMechDB training steps are never removed for cross-source overlap. FlowER PM (a PMechDB copy) is never ingested, and mech-USPTO-31K stays held out.

(10) External references. RMechRP's core 64.3/95.1 and atmospheric 62.1/94.1 (top-1/top-5) are compared only through the separate flower_given run on FlowER's own test, which is leaky (89 of 136 RC and 284 of 346 RS test steps share a species with train) and covers about 91-95% of the official one, while RMechRP ranks reactive-MO pairs, not full R; every caveat is stated, and the exact comparison waits for D1's official split. The dozen cited gas-phase IP/EA values of P2 are compared only with gas-phase delta-SCF levels, never with ALPB-water eigenvalues. RSE43 and St. John 2020 enter only with D2, after a data-license check and your approval of the download.

## 6. Not doing

- No global QM method change and no recompute of the shared PMechDB + ReactMech cache (33,678 records after P0's merge: 14,479 PMechDB + 19,199 ReactMech-only) or of QMugs (148,876, 0 radicals), about 182.5k unique records and about 86 GB. spGFN2 runs only in P2's one-off scan (scratch cache) and in D2, a planned ablation after P9, under a per-molecule tag.
- No alpha/beta spin-orbital tokens (p99 483 -> 836 orbital tokens, 33 samples over 1024), no QMRecord schema bump, and one token schema bump only: the additive 9 -> 10 of P0a (the exact-degeneracy frontier columns), none later. Hole tokens and spin_role are built at sample assembly, and after P0a only u > 0 token files are rebuilt again, once, in P2b, with a per-file config key.
- No change to qm/fukui.py or to QM record fields: the radical frontier convention lives in orbitals/frontier.py and the finite-difference f+/f- variant reads the records' existing fukui_plus/minus through the token files (stored in the v18 store since P0a), so no record changes meaning under its key.
- No change to resolve_charge_spin and no serve behaviour change before S1; serve tests stay green from P2b on, and the engine never imports ksmech.serve.
- No multiplicity chosen by GFN2 energies at build time: data.spin reads RDKit, spin_table_v1 and the Cu(II)/Fe(III) table, and a species the scan cannot separate (gap under about 0.3 eV) is MULTIPLICITY_AMBIGUOUS, not guessed.
- No H completion of a carbon with one radical electron: the 3 sodium isopropoxide drawings stay excluded as ambiguous (possible sodium ketyls), on the U6 candidate list, through P3's counted curation file (RADICAL_AMBIGUOUS).
- No source switch for the repair laws: V1, A1 (the same unchanged alkali atom on both sides) and the dative law apply to every source, rmechdb included, where their narrow signatures touch at most 1 line (measured, P8).
- No hand gate on open-shell species in polar sources: P3 admits by counted rules (u conserved, R even, integral SOMO), and the hand-read list is the test oracle, not the gate.
- No SET anchor: steps with no bond change are SET_STEP (0 on disk), the identity line is NULL_STEP, and dissociative electron transfer has its own code, all counted. Revisit only if a source (D1) makes them significant.
- No open-shell-singlet biradicals and no species with u >= 2 whose open shell is not one degenerate group (the Hund case: triplet O2 and O(3P) on disk): one MULTI_RADICAL_SPECIES policy for every source, with counted exemptions (PMechDB 5d007b38), declares genuine diradical chemistry (Bergman, Norrish, Criegee) out of domain.
- No admission of CuI2, the CuH2 adducts or Cu=C drawings: Cu=C is already METAL_PLACEHOLDER, and data.spin rejects CuI2 and the CuH2 adducts, so no hydride record is computed.
- No Arrow.electrons, fishhook parser or official RMechDB loader before you download the CSV (D1).
- No contact geometry: g2's beta is the existing intramolecular Wolfsberg-Helmholz coupling at the reactant pose and one stated constant between molecules; the stage-b contact terms stay on archive/stage-b.
- No element list, parity gate or source split in the metal domain rules: one dative law, one parity-independent METAL_PLACEHOLDER rule and one METAL_SUSPECT flag, applied once in P0a; the 3 [Cl][Zn+] steps stay as defined ions, and the 35 METAL_SUSPECT ReactMech steps (Cr(IV) 10, Ni(II) d8 25) are flagged and sliced, not removed.
- No change or removal of a training step after P0a: later phases only return excluded steps (P1, P3, P4, P5) or add a source (P8), and closed_base is never edited after P0b.
- No baseline on a store that P0a will change: the 3-seed reference is trained in P0b, after the one re-ingest at SAMPLE_VERSION 18.
- No hand list, element list, percentile or go/no-go threshold in the model path without an ablation: the d-type and own-antibond deletions, the 0.05 eV degeneracy window (equal occupations for u > 0; an exact-degeneracy column with a P9 ablation for closed shells), the percentile delta, the SOMO -> partner exclusion, the 0.1 e metal gate, the CO/N2/O3/O2 lists, the pool-level 'no -1 when u = 0' mask and the fixed max_candidates 48 are replaced by a law, a QM-derived input or a flag whose default an ablation justifies in decisions.md. The thresholds no reviser resolved (tau_atom 0.15, the 0.2-1.8 band, the spectral-set 0.05 eV merge, SMEAR_TOL 0.05 with the 0.5/1.5 closed-shell block thresholds) are measured in P0a and become flags with a P9 ablation where their reach is not negligible, on your decision.
- No source flag at inference: 'R_ii in {-2,0,+2} for sources without an open-shell policy' is a label rule only.
- No trigger-gated D2: spGFN2 for u > 0 is a planned ablation after P9, not pulled ahead by a suite score or a |dq| bound.
- No hard links between data roots: the ReactMech move (P0) and the Release B data root (P6-P8) use cp -a --reflink=always, because non-atomic writers would write through a hard link into main.
- No removal of PMechDB training steps for cross-source overlap: the overlap is a slice, so pm_control and joint train on the same PMechDB steps.
- No exclusion of fermi_smeared samples from closed_base, and no re-split of PMechDB or ReactMech.
- No recovery of the ligand-less placeholder metals (24 + 8 Sn, plus the about 24 even ones P0a's parity-independent rule removes), the wrong-charge steps or DIBAL: ligands would have to be invented.
- No widening of the parsimonious label class to equal-charge resonance-equivalent forms (it misses the dataset's own R in 644 of 12,418 PMechDB labels, 5.2%) and no soft tau_atom incidence (tau_atom's reach is measured in P0a; a flag follows only on your decision): both optional audit items stay unscheduled, and adopting either would change training labels, so it would need a P0a-type re-ingest.
- No change of the electronic temperature (decision of 2026-10-24).
- No external QM dataset (St. John, BDE-db, QCML, OMol25) as training data or pretraining, and no download without your approval; P2's IP/EA values are typed from the literature with citations.
- No FlowER PM or PC ingest and no full FlowER ingest (about 2.14M numeric-suffix lines) in this plan; the NBS chains wait for D3.
- No PMechDB- or RMechDB-derived artifact (steps, samples, tokens, checkpoints) in the public rhoMech repo, and no real FlowER RS/RC line in a tracked test fixture; local copies, the private rhoMech-data and pods are allowed (U18), and FlowER's MIT label is not relied on.
- No serve radical prediction before P9 has metrics: until S3 the predictors refuse radical pools.
- No claim of multi-step mechanism prediction: END_MECH is untrained (lambda_stop 0 on PMechDB) until D3.
- No edits to tracked files, the spec included, and no merge in a checkout while a build, QM batch, training run or ksmech-serve runs from it: commit before building, stop serve before a merge or a code commit on main and start it after.

P5 does not need P4: its re-charged fragments are even closed shells, so RDKit and data.spin agree. → No depends_on list changed.
